# Borba CAD API reference

Generated by `make docs` from the docstrings of the exported symbols; do not edit by hand.

## BORBA-CAD/APP

The application: state, input events, tools, operations, panels and frame
building. It is headless: a window system feeds it events and draws the draw list it builds.

### `*interface-zoom-percentages*` (variable)

The zooms of the interface that the keys step through, in percent: 100 is the interface as designed.

### `*measure-tool-kinds*` (variable)

The tools that measure, which the Measure button opens.

### `*modify-tool-kinds*` (variable)

The tools that change what is drawn, which the Modify button opens, as opposed to those that draw: the ones that
need a selection, and the ones that work on what is clicked.

### `*selection-tool-kinds*` (variable)

The tools that change what is selected as a whole, and so need a selection.

### `+autosave-interval-nanoseconds+` (constant)

How long a drawing with unsaved changes goes without a copy being written.

### `+default-spread-distance+` (constant)

How far, in millimetres, the spread tool moves each piece of a shape from the others until another distance is
typed: one centimetre.

### `+drag-threshold-pixels+` (constant)

Pointer travel, in pixels, before a press becomes a drag; below it a release is a click.

### `+minimum-viewport-size+` (constant)

Smallest width or height, in millimetres of paper, that a viewport made with the pointer may have.

### `+palette-visible-rows+` (constant)

Most commands the palette lists at once; the arrows and the wheel bring the others.

### `+pick-radius-pixels+` (constant)

How close, in pixels, the pointer must be to an entity to pick it: about 1.6 mm on screen,
enough for a steady hand and small enough not to grab distant entities.

### `+polar-increment-degrees+` (constant)

The angle, in degrees, whose multiples polar holds the next point to.

### `+shape-columns+` (constant)

How many tiles of shapes there are across the popover of the shapes.

### `+shape-visible-rows+` (constant)

How many rows of tiles the popover of the shapes shows at once; the wheel brings the others.

### `+snap-radius-pixels+` (constant)

How close, in pixels, the pointer must be to an endpoint or center for object snap to grab it.

### `+zoom-step-per-notch+` (constant)

Zoom factor of one mouse-wheel notch.

### `app` (type)

The whole application state.

- `CONFIG`: configuration.
- `TELEMETRY`: observability hub, or NIL.
- `THEME`: colors.
- `DRAWING`: the drawing being edited.
- `HISTORY`: undo and redo stacks.
- `VIEWPORT`: current view.
- `SELECTION`: hash table of selected entity ids.
- `TOOL`: the active tool.
- `DRAW-LIST`: reused every frame.
- `CURSOR`: last pointer position in world coordinates, or NIL.
- `POINTER-X`: last pointer window x.
- `POINTER-Y`: last pointer window y.
- `INTERACTION`: current pointer drag (:pan or a tool-owned drag) or NIL.
- `TRANSLATION`: (dx . dy) move preview in millimetres, or NIL.
- `WINDOW`: world bounds of the window-selection rectangle, or NIL.
- `WINDOW-CROSSING`: true when that rectangle selects what it touches (dragged from right to left), and
NIL when it selects only what it holds (dragged from left to right).
- `SPACE-DOWN`: true while the space bar is held (pan modifier).
- `FILE-PATH`: path of the drawing file, or NIL.
- `SAVED-REVISION`: drawing revision at the last save or load.
- `FOCUS`: id of the field that has the keyboard focus, or NIL.
- `EDIT`: edit state of that field.
- `PROMPT`: the open modal prompt, or NIL.
- `DIALOG`: the open file dialog, or NIL.
- `MENU`: the open drop-down menu, or NIL.
- `WELCOME`: the start screen, or NIL.
- `POLYGON-SIDES`: the number of sides the polygon tool gives the next polygon.
- `FILLET-RADIUS`: the radius, in millimetres, the fillet tool gives the next arc.
- `SPREAD-DISTANCE`: the distance, in millimetres, the spread tool moves each piece of a shape from the others.
- `FORM-SETTINGS`: what was chosen or typed in the forms that were last accepted, as an alist of the key of each line and
its value.
- `SHEET-VIEW`: the state of looking at the sheets, a LAYOUT-STATE, or NIL while the drawing is shown.
- `QUANTITY-EXPORT`: the report of quantities that is to be written as a CSV file and how, as a cons of the report and
:standard or :excel, or NIL.
- `CUSTOMIZATIONS`: the commands, key bindings and hooks that the init file and the console defined.
- `RECORDED-COMMANDS`: the names of the commands that were saved from recordings, which the folder of commands holds.
- `INTERFACE-ZOOM`: how much larger than designed the user asked the interface to be drawn; 1 is as designed.
- `PLOT-SETTINGS`: how the drawing was put on the sheet the last time it was exported as a PDF or an SVG, or NIL.
- `LAST-BLOCK-NAME`: the name of the block that was last made or placed, or NIL.
- `RECORDING`: the recording of what the user does that is under way, or NIL.
- `RECORDED-SCRIPT`: the script that the last recording made, kept until it is saved, or NIL.
- `SHAPES-GROUP`: which group of shapes the popover of the shapes shows, counting all of them from 0.
- `SHAPE-SCROLL`: how many rows of tiles the popover has scrolled past.
- `USER-AREAS`: the areas of shapes that the user added, as last read from the folder of shapes.
- `SHAPE-PROBLEMS`: the files of that folder that could not be read, as conses of a name and a sentence.
- `USER-AREAS-LOADED`: true once the folder of shapes has been read.
- `GRIP-DRAG`: the grip being dragged, or NIL.
- `CONSOLE`: the console.
- `CONSTRAINT`: :ortho, :polar or NIL, how the next point is held to the last one.
- `COORDINATE-EDIT`: the edit state of a point being typed, or NIL.
- `AUTOSAVE-TIME`: monotonic time of the last look of the autosave, or NIL before the first.
- `AUTOSAVE-REVISION`: the revision of the drawing in the copy written last, or -1.
- `AUTOSAVE-WRITTEN`: true when this session has written a copy that is still in the state directory.
- `IMAGE-CAPTURER`: function of no arguments that returns what the canvas shows as a picture, as three values: the
pixels (bytes, row by row from the top, red, green and blue of each), the width and the height; or NIL when this
window cannot make one.
- `CLIPBOARD-READER`: function of no arguments that returns the text on the system clipboard, or NIL.
- `CLIPBOARD-WRITER`: function of one string that puts it on the system clipboard, or NIL.
- `CLIPBOARD-FALLBACK`: the clipboard text kept here while there is no system clipboard.
- `MESSAGE`: (text . severity) shown in the status bar, or NIL.
- `MESSAGE-TIME`: monotonic time when the message was set, in nanoseconds.
- `OVERLAY-VISIBLE`: whether the performance overlay is shown.
- `LAYER-SCROLL`: first visible layer row.
- `SEEN-CURRENT-LAYER`: the current layer when the layers card was last built, to scroll the card to a
new one.
- `INSPECTOR-SCROLL`: first visible inspector row.
- `FIELD-COMMIT`: function of the typed string run when the focused field is committed.
- `FIELD-ORIGINAL`: the text the focused field started with; an unchanged field commits nothing.
- `WINDOW-WIDTH`: window width in logical pixels.
- `WINDOW-HEIGHT`: window height in logical pixels.
- `DISPLAY-SCALE`: framebuffer pixels per logical pixel.
- `NEEDS-REDRAW`: true when the next frame differs from the last.
- `QUIT-REQUESTED`: true when the application should close.
- `LAST-SCENE-RESULT`: statistics of the last scene build.
- `LAST-ERROR`: the last error condition reported, for tests and diagnostics.

### `app-render` (function)

```lisp
(app-render app)
```

Rebuild the draw list for the current state: canvas, panels, prompt and overlay. Returns the
scene result and clears the redraw flag.

- `APP`: the application.

### `app-request-redraw` (function)

```lisp
(app-request-redraw app)
```

- `APP`: the application.

### `app-resize` (function)

```lisp
(app-resize app width height &optional (scale 1.0d0))
```

Handle a window size change: the canvas follows the layout and keeps its center and zoom.

- `APP`: the application.
- `WIDTH`: new logical window width.
- `HEIGHT`: new logical window height.
- `SCALE`: display scale.

### `apply-benchmark-input` (function)

```lisp
(apply-benchmark-input app frame)
```

Feed the application the input of one benchmark frame: a wheel step at the canvas center
and a small pointer movement, then ask for a redraw. Every benchmark, headless or windowed,
uses this same interaction so that their frame costs are comparable.

- `APP`: the application.
- `FRAME`: frame number, which selects the zoom direction.

### `arc-tool` (type)

Creates an arc from its center, the point where it starts (which gives the radius) and
a point in the direction where it ends, counter-clockwise.

### `autosave-exists-p` (function)

```lisp
(autosave-exists-p app)
```

Return true when a copy of a drawing waits in the state directory.

- `APP`: the application.

### `autosave-tick` (function)

```lisp
(autosave-tick app &optional (now (monotonic-nanoseconds)))
```

Do what the autosave needs now: write a copy when the drawing has changes that are not saved and a minute has
passed since the last look, and take the copy away once the drawing has no unsaved changes. Cheap enough to be
called on every pass of the window loop.

- `APP`: the application.
- `NOW`: the monotonic time, in nanoseconds.

### `breadcrumbs` (function)

```lisp
(breadcrumbs directory)
```

Return the trail from the root to DIRECTORY as (label . directory) pairs, the root first.

- `DIRECTORY`: a directory pathname.

### `build-widgets` (function)

```lisp
(build-widgets app)
```

Build every widget of the window, in drawing order, from the application state: the side
panel, the title bar, the tool palette, the status bar, the tooltip of the widget under the
pointer, the open menu, the file dialog when one is open, and the modal prompt when one is.

- `APP`: the application.

### `circle-tool` (type)

Creates a circle from its center and a point on its outline.

### `clear-message` (function)

```lisp
(clear-message app)
```

Take the message out of the status bar. The next thing the user does is a new beginning, and
what the last one said no longer applies.

- `APP`: the application.

### `clipboard-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(clipboard-drawing app)
```

Return the drawing whose text is on the clipboard, or NIL when the clipboard holds anything else
or is empty.

- `APP`: the application.

### `clipboard-text` (function)

```lisp
(clipboard-text app)
```

Return the text on the clipboard, or NIL when there is none.

- `APP`: the application.

### `close-file-dialog` (function)

```lisp
(close-file-dialog app)
```

Close the file dialog without doing anything.

- `APP`: the application.

### `close-menu` (function)

```lisp
(close-menu app)
```

Close the open menu, if there is one.

- `APP`: the application.

### `close-prompt` (function)

```lisp
(close-prompt app)
```

Close the modal prompt without running anything.

- `APP`: the application.

### `close-welcome` (function)

```lisp
(close-welcome app)
```

Close the start screen, if it is open, and go on with the drawing as it is.

- `APP`: the application.

### `command-file-name` (function)

```lisp
(command-file-name name)
```

Return the name of the file that a command called NAME is kept in, without the extension: the name, trimmed and
with the characters that a file name may not have turned into hyphens. Signals EDIT-REFUSED when nothing is left or
it is too long.

- `NAME`: what the user typed.

### `commands-directory` (function)

```lisp
(commands-directory app)
```

Return the folder of the commands saved from recordings, or NIL when there is no configuration directory.

- `APP`: the application.

### `commit-changes` (function)

```lisp
(commit-changes app changes event &key (context edit))
```

Commit a change set through the history, timed and logged as EVENT. Failures are reported to
the user and nothing changes. Returns true when changes were committed.

- `APP`: the application.
- `CHANGES`: list of change records; an empty list commits nothing and returns NIL.
- `EVENT`: dotted event name such as entity.create.
- `CONTEXT`: short name used in the error message log.

### `commit-sheets` (function)

```lisp
(commit-sheets app change)
```

Change the sheets of the drawing with CHANGE, keeping what the layout has worked out of the drawing, which this
does not change, and say that the next frame is different.

- `APP`: the application.
- `CHANGE`: function of no arguments that changes the sheets, which may signal EDIT-REFUSED.

### `console-focused-p` (function)

```lisp
(console-focused-p app)
```

Return true when the keyboard is on the field where commands are typed.

- `APP`: the application.

### `console-say` (function)

```lisp
(console-say app kind text)
```

Add TEXT to the output of the console, a line for each line of text, and show the end of the output.

- `APP`: the application.
- `KIND`: :input, :output, :value, :error or :note.
- `TEXT`: what to say.

### `console-submit` (function)

```lisp
(console-submit app)
```

Run the line typed in the console, together with the lines before it that left an expression open; if
the expression is still open afterwards, wait for the next line.

- `APP`: the application.

### `constrain-point` (function)

```lisp
(constrain-point mode anchor x y)
```

Return the point that MODE holds (X, Y) to, as two values: with :ortho, the nearest point of the horizontal or
the vertical line through the anchor; with :polar, the nearest point of the ray from the anchor at a multiple
of the polar increment.

- `MODE`: :ortho or :polar.
- `ANCHOR`: an (x . y) cons, the last point.
- `X`: world x of the point.
- `Y`: world y of the point.

### `create-folder` (function)

```lisp
(create-folder dialog name)
```

Create a folder in the folder shown and select it. Says why when it cannot be done.
Returns true when the folder was created.

- `DIALOG`: the file dialog.
- `NAME`: the new folder's name.

### `current-shape-group` (function)

```lisp
(current-shape-group app)
```

Return the group that the popover shows, as a cons of its area and itself, or NIL when there is none.

- `APP`: the application.

### `cursor` (type)

The pointer position in world coordinates.

- `RAW-X`: world x under the pointer.
- `RAW-Y`: world y under the pointer.
- `X`: world x after snapping.
- `Y`: world y after snapping.
- `SNAP-KIND`: :endpoint, :midpoint, :center, :intersection, :quadrant, :perpendicular, :tangent, :grid, :ortho,
:polar or NIL.
- `MODIFIERS`: modifier keys held.

### `default-places` (function)

```lisp
(default-places config)
```

Return the shortcuts that the dialog offers on its side, as (label icon pathname): the home
folder, the documents, desktop and downloads folders in the language of the desktop, and the
sample drawings of the project, each only when it exists.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.

### `demo-save-path` (function)

```lisp
(demo-save-path app)
```

Return the path where the demonstration saves its drawing: var/demo.bcad in the project.

- `APP`: the application.

### `demo-steps` (function)

```lisp
(demo-steps app)
```

Return the steps of the demonstration scenario as functions of no arguments: layers, a floor
plan drawn with every tool, a selection and a move, undo, zoom, pan, the overlay, and a save.

- `APP`: the application.

### `dimension-tool` (type)

Creates an aligned dimension from two clicks and a third that places its line.

### `discard-autosave` (function)

```lisp
(discard-autosave app)
```

Take the copy of the drawing away, because the work in it is saved, or given up.

- `APP`: the application.

### `edit-with-clipboard` (function)

```lisp
(edit-with-clipboard app edit key modifiers)
```

Apply a key to the text of a field: the editing keys, and with Control A (select all), C (copy
the text), X (cut it) and V (paste at the caret, over the text when it is selected). Returns the
new edit state, or NIL when the key does nothing to a field.

- `APP`: the application.
- `EDIT`: the field's edit state.
- `KEY`: key keyword.
- `MODIFIERS`: modifier keys held.

### `ellipse-tool` (type)

Creates an ellipse from its center, the end of its first axis, and a point whose distance
from that axis is the other half-axis.

### `expire-message` (function)

```lisp
(expire-message app &optional (now (monotonic-nanoseconds)))
```

Take the message out of the status bar once it has been there long enough, so that the hint
under the pointer and the prompt of the tool show again. Returns true when it was removed.

- `APP`: the application.
- `NOW`: the monotonic time to judge by, in nanoseconds.

### `export-dxf-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-dxf-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the drawing as a DXF file at PATH-TEXT (.dxf is added to a name without an extension) and say what
changed shape in the writing. On failure the previous file is untouched and the reason is shown. Returns
true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `export-image-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-image-from-app app path-text)
```

Save what the canvas shows as a JPEG file at PATH-TEXT (.jpg is added to a name without an extension), and
tell the user how it went in a modal. The picture is the canvas as it is on the screen, without the panels,
the selection and the cursor. On failure the previous file is untouched. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `export-pdf-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-pdf-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the drawing as a PDF file at PATH-TEXT (.pdf is added to a name without an extension), on the sheet and at the
scale that were chosen. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `export-script-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-script-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the drawing as a script at PATH-TEXT (.lisp is added to a name without an extension) and tell the user
how it went in a modal. On failure the previous file is untouched. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `export-sheets-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-sheets-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the sheets as a PDF file at PATH-TEXT (.pdf is added to a name without an extension), a page for each. Returns
true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `export-svg-from-app` (function)

```lisp
(export-svg-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the drawing as an SVG file at PATH-TEXT (.svg is added to a name without an extension), on the sheet and at the
scale that were chosen. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `file-dialog` (type)

The dialog where the user chooses a drawing to open or a place and a name to save to.

- `MODE`: :open or :save.
- `CONTINUATION`: function of no arguments run after a successful save, or NIL.
- `PURPOSE`: :drawing for a drawing of the program, :dxf to import or export a DXF file, or :script to run a
script or export one.
- `DIRECTORY`: the folder shown, as a directory pathname.
- `ENTRIES`: every entry of that folder, folders first, each group by name.
- `SELECTED`: the name of the selected entry, or NIL.
- `SCROLL`: how many visible entries are scrolled past.
- `CAPACITY`: how many rows of the list fit, known once the dialog has been drawn.
- `SHOW-ALL`: true to list every file, false to list only drawings.
- `FILTER-EDIT`: edit state of the search field.
- `NAME-EDIT`: edit state of the file name field.
- `LOCATION-EDIT`: edit state while the path is being typed, or NIL when it is shown as a trail.
- `FOCUS`: which field has the keyboard: :name, :filter or :location.
- `MESSAGE`: a sentence under the list, or NIL.
- `MESSAGE-SEVERITY`: :info or :error.
- `PLACES`: the shortcuts on the side, as (label icon pathname).
- `RECENT`: the recently used drawings, as pathnames, latest first.
- `LAST-CLICK`: the row last clicked and when, to recognize a double click.
- `VISIBLE-CACHE`: the entries the list showed last time with what they were computed from, so that
a folder of tens of thousands of files is not filtered again for every widget of every frame.

### `import-dxf-into-app` (function)

```lisp
(import-dxf-into-app app path-text)
```

Read the DXF file at PATH-TEXT and make a new drawing of it. A file that does not say the unit of its
numbers is followed by a question, and the import goes on when it is answered. On failure the current
drawing is untouched and the reason is shown. Returns true on success, or when the question was asked.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `import-shape-into-app` (function)

```lisp
(import-shape-into-app app path-text)
```

Add the SVG file at PATH-TEXT to the shapes of the user: it is read and checked, copied into the folder of shapes,
and the popover opens on it. On failure the reason is shown. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

- `APP`: the application.

### `insert-tool` (type)

Places a block: a click for the point its base point goes to, then a click that turns it toward the
pointer, or Enter to leave it as it is. A number typed before the first click is the scale, and one typed after it is
the turn in degrees. F flips it across its vertical axis.

- `KEY`: keyword such as :a, :escape, :enter, :backspace, :delete, :space, :left, :home, :f3.
- `MODIFIERS`: modifier keys held.
- `ACTION`: :down or :up.


Create the application around a drawing (a new empty one by default).

- `WIDTH`: initial logical window width.
- `HEIGHT`: initial logical window height.
- `THEME`: the color theme, or NIL for the one kept from the last run, and the dark one when there is none.

entities that were selected, which are replaced by one placement of it.

```lisp
(move-selection dialog delta)
```

Move the selection up or down the list, stopping at its ends. With nothing selected, going

### `offer-autosave-recovery` (function)

```lisp
(offer-autosave-recovery app)
```

When the program was closed with work that was not saved, ask whether to take it back. Recover opens it as a
drawing with unsaved changes, Discard throws it away, and Not now (which Escape chooses) leaves it for the next
time.

- `APP`: the application.

### `open-choice-prompt` (function)

```lisp
(open-choice-prompt app title message choices &optional message-lines)
```

Open a modal prompt with a row of buttons. Each function runs after the prompt closes.

- `APP`: the application.
- `TITLE`: heading.
- `MESSAGE`: explanation under the heading.
- `CHOICES`: list of (label . function-or-nil); the first is chosen by Enter, the last by Escape.
- `MESSAGE-LINES`: how many lines the message may take, or NIL for the usual three.

### `open-directory` (function)

```lisp
(open-directory dialog directory)
```

Show the folder DIRECTORY. When it cannot be read, say why and stay where the dialog was.
Returns true when the folder was shown.

- `DIALOG`: the file dialog.
- `DIRECTORY`: a directory pathname.

### `open-file-dialog` (function)

```lisp
(open-file-dialog app mode &optional continuation (purpose drawing))
```

Open the file dialog to choose a drawing to open or a place and a name to save to.

- `APP`: the application.
- `MODE`: :open or :save.
- `CONTINUATION`: function of no arguments run after a successful save, or NIL.
- `PURPOSE`: :drawing for a drawing of the program, :dxf to import or export a DXF file, :script to run a script
or export one, :recording to save the script of a recording, :shape to choose an SVG file to add as a shape,
:image to export a picture, :pdf or :svg to export a sheet, :quantities to write a report as a CSV file, or :sheets
to write the sheets as a PDF file.

### `open-form-prompt` (function)

```lisp
(open-form-prompt app title message fields on-accept &key info)
```

Open a modal form: a line for each of FIELDS, with a text field or a few options, then OK and Cancel. Tab and
Shift+Tab (or the up and down arrows) move between the lines, Enter is OK. ON-ACCEPT runs when OK is pressed; when it
returns a sentence the form stays open and shows it.

- `APP`: the application.
- `TITLE`: heading.
- `MESSAGE`: explanation under the heading.
- `FIELDS`: list of (key label initial) for a text field, or (key label initial-key options) for a choice, where OPTIONS
is a list of (key . label); the key is what the value is given under, the label what the line asks for, and the
initial value the text the field starts with or the key of the option that starts chosen.
- `ON-ACCEPT`: function of an alist of (key . value), in the order of the fields, where the value is the typed string or
the key of the chosen option; it returns NIL to close the form or a sentence to keep it open.
- `INFO`: function of the same alist that returns a line saying what OK would do, and true as a second value when the
values cannot be used; or NIL.

### `open-menu` (function)

```lisp
(open-menu app kind anchor)
```

Open the menu KIND hanging from the button ANCHOR, with the keyboard on the choice in force.

- `APP`: the application.
- `KIND`: :draw, :language or :more.
- `ANCHOR`: id of the button.

### `open-parent-directory` (function)

```lisp
(open-parent-directory dialog)
```

Show the folder that holds the one shown, and select the folder just left so that going back
down is one key press. Returns true when there was a parent.

- `DIALOG`: the file dialog.

### `open-report-prompt` (function)

```lisp
(open-report-prompt app title message report choices)
```

Open a modal that shows a table of quantities, to read and scroll, with a row of buttons.

- `APP`: the application.
- `TITLE`: heading.
- `MESSAGE`: explanation under the heading.
- `REPORT`: the QUANTITY-REPORT that is shown.
- `CHOICES`: list of (label . function-or-nil), the buttons; the first is chosen by Enter and the last by Escape.

### `open-text-prompt` (function)

```lisp
(open-text-prompt app title message initial on-accept)
```

Open a modal prompt with a text field, OK and Cancel. ON-ACCEPT runs after the prompt closes.

- `APP`: the application.
- `TITLE`: heading.
- `MESSAGE`: explanation under the heading.
- `INITIAL`: text the field starts with, selected so typing replaces it.
- `ON-ACCEPT`: function of one argument, the typed string.

### `operation-interface-zoom-in` (function)

```lisp
(operation-interface-zoom-in app)
```

### `operation-interface-zoom-out` (function)

Make the interface one step smaller.


Open the dialog where the user finds a drawing to open, after offering to save the current one.

### `operation-paste` (function)

```lisp
(operation-paste app &key in-place)
```

Paste the entities on the clipboard as new entities, selected, so that they can be moved at
once: centered on the pointer, or where they were copied from when IN-PLACE. They go onto the layer
of the same name when there is one that is visible and unlocked, and onto the current layer
otherwise.

- `APP`: the application.
- `IN-PLACE`: true to keep the coordinates they were copied with.

### `operation-quantities` (function)

Ask which report of the quantities of the drawing to make, and show it.

Close the application, after offering to save unsaved changes.

Forget what the init file defined and run it again, and read the folder of commands again, so that a change to
either is in force without leaving the program.

- `APP`: the application.

CONTINUATION.

Activate a tool, cancelling whatever the previous one was doing.

:diameter-dimension, :angular-dimension, :hatch, :solid-fill, :trim, :extend, :offset, :fillet, :spread or

Turn polar on, which holds the next point to the nearest multiple of 15 degrees from the last one, or off. It
replaces ortho.

Begin recording when nothing is recorded, and end the recording when it is.

### `operation-toggle-shapes` (function)


Open the popover of the shapes, or close it when it is open.

- `APP`: the application.

### `operation-toggle-snap` (function)


Turn snapping to the grid on or off.


Change from the dark theme to the light one, or back.



Fit the view to every visible entity.

- `APP`: the application.

### `operation-zoom-in` (function)

```lisp
(operation-zoom-in app)
```

Zoom in by one notch of the wheel, about the center of the canvas.

- `APP`: the application.

### `operation-zoom-out` (function)


Zoom out by one notch of the wheel, about the center of the canvas.

- `APP`: the application.

```lisp
(palette-entries app)
```

Return everything the palette lists, in the order it lists it when nothing is typed: the commands that the user
defined, those saved from recordings, then the choices of the menus and the rest of what the program does.

- `APP`: the application.

### `palette-entry-label` (function)

```lisp
(palette-entry-label instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `palette-entry-shortcut` (function)

```lisp

```

(undocumented)

### `palette-prompt-matches` (function)

```lisp
(palette-prompt-matches prompt)
```

Return the entries of an open palette that answer what is typed in it.

- `PROMPT`: the :palette prompt.

### `parse-point-input` (function)

```lisp
(parse-point-input text anchor pointer unit)
```

Return the point that typed TEXT names, as two values in millimetres. Signals INVALID-INPUT when it names none.
The forms are: a length, which goes from the anchor toward the pointer; x;y, an absolute point; @x;y, a point
at that distance from the anchor along x and y; and length<angle, a point at that length from the anchor in
that direction in degrees (with the @ or without it). Without an anchor the origin takes its place.

- `TEXT`: what was typed.
- `ANCHOR`: an (x . y) cons, the last point, or NIL for the first.
- `POINTER`: an (x . y) cons, where the pointer is, or NIL.
- `UNIT`: the display unit, assumed for numbers without a unit.

### `place-shape` (function)

```lisp
(place-shape app shape)
```

Make the shape the one to place: the drawing defines its block when it has none, the popover closes and the tool that
places it takes over.

- `APP`: the application.
- `SHAPE`: a shape of the library.

### `pointer-event` (type)

A pointer movement or button transition.

- `ACTION`: :move, :down or :up.
- `BUTTON`: :left, :middle or :right for :down and :up; NIL for :move.
- `X`: window x in logical pixels.
- `Y`: window y in logical pixels.
- `MODIFIERS`: modifier keys held.





### `pointer-event-y` (function)

```lisp
(pointer-event-y instance)
```

### `polygon-tool` (type)

Creates a regular polygon from its center and one of its vertices; Up and Down change
the number of sides, which the application keeps for the next polygon.

### `polyline-tool` (type)

Collects vertices; Enter finishes an open polyline, clicking the first vertex closes it.

### `prompt` (type)

A modal question.

- `KIND`: :text (a text field with OK and Cancel), :choice (a row of buttons), :form (several text fields, with OK
and Cancel), :report (a table to read, with a row of buttons) or :palette (a field to search a list of commands in,
and the list).
- `TITLE`: heading.
- `MESSAGE`: explanation shown under the heading.
- `EDIT`: the EDIT-STATE of the text field, for :text prompts, or of what is typed to search a :palette.
- `CHOICES`: for :text and :form, a list of (label . function) with the accept function first; for :choice, the buttons.
The accept function of a :text prompt receives the typed string; that of a :form receives an alist of the key and
the typed string of each line, and returns a sentence when it refuses them, which keeps the form open.
- `MESSAGE-LINES`: how many lines the message may take before it is cut, or NIL for the usual three.
- `FIELDS`: for :form, the FORM-FIELDs, top to bottom; for :palette, the PALETTE-ENTRYs that can be chosen.
- `ACTIVE`: for :form, the position of the field that has the keyboard; for :palette, the position of the command chosen
among those that match what was typed.
- `INFO`: for :form, a function of the alist of typed values that returns a line that says what OK would do, and true as
a second value when the values cannot be used; or NIL.
- `PROBLEM`: for :form, the sentence the last OK was refused with, or NIL.
- `REPORT`: for :report, the QUANTITY-REPORT that is shown.
- `SCROLL`: for :report and :palette, how many rows of the table or of the list are scrolled past.

### `recover-autosave` (function)

```lisp
(recover-autosave app)
```

Make the waiting copy the drawing being edited, as a drawing with unsaved changes that belongs to the file it
came from. Returns true on success. When the copy cannot be read the reason is shown and the copy is kept.

- `APP`: the application.

### `rectangle-tool` (type)

Creates an axis-aligned rectangle from two opposite corners.

### `report-error` (function)

```lisp
(report-error app condition &optional (context operation))
```

Tell the user about a failure and log it; nothing is swallowed.

- `APP`: the application.
- `CONDITION`: the condition that was signaled.
- `CONTEXT`: short name of what failed, used in the log event.

### `report-internal-error` (function)

```lisp
(report-internal-error app condition context backtrace)
```

Tell the user that the program itself failed, a bug rather than something they did, and log it
with the call stack. The program goes on: a failed event leaves the drawing as the last finished
operation left it.

- `APP`: the application.
- `CONDITION`: the condition that was signaled.
- `CONTEXT`: what the program was doing, as a phrase that follows "while", for the message.
- `BACKTRACE`: the call stack at the moment of the failure, as text, or NIL.

### `resize-event` (type)

The window changed size.

- `WIDTH`: new logical width.
- `HEIGHT`: new logical height.
- `SCALE`: display scale (framebuffer pixels per logical pixel).

### `resize-event-height` (function)

```lisp
(resize-event-height instance)
```

```lisp
(resize-event-scale instance)
```

```lisp
(resize-event-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `resized-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(resized-viewport viewport corner x y)
```

Return a viewport like another with one corner moved to a point and the opposite corner where it was, which keeps
what it shows where it is on the sheet, or NIL when that would leave it too small.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `CORNER`: which corner is dragged: 0 for the lower left, 1 for the lower right, 2 for the upper right, 3 for the upper
left.
- `X`: x of the point on the sheet.
- `Y`: y of the point.

### `run-command-by-name` (function)

```lisp
(run-command-by-name app name)
```

Run the command called NAME: one that was defined, one saved from a recording, or one of the program as the command
palette lists it. Returns true when there is one, and says so when there is not.

- `APP`: the application.
- `NAME`: what it is called, in any case.

### `run-hooks` (function)

```lisp
(run-hooks app event)
```

Run what was added to an event, each as a command of its own. A hook that fails is said in the console and the
status bar, and the others still run.

- `APP`: the application.
- `EVENT`: :startup, :drawing-opened, :before-save or :after-save.

```lisp
(run-script-file-in-app app path-text)
```

Read the script file at PATH-TEXT and ask where it is to draw; it runs when the user answers. On failure
the reason is shown. Returns true when the question was asked.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

### `run-user-key` (function)

```lisp
(run-user-key app key modifiers)
```

Run what the user bound a key to. Returns true when the key was bound.

- `APP`: the application.
- `KEY`: the key keyword.
- `MODIFIERS`: the modifier keys held.

```lisp
(save-app-drawing app path-text)
```

Save the drawing to PATH-TEXT; a name without an extension gets .bcad. On failure the
previous file is untouched and the reason is shown. Returns true on success.

- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

```lisp
(save-recording-from-app app path-text)
```

Save the recorded script at PATH-TEXT (.lisp is added to a name without an extension) and tell the user how it
went in a modal. On failure the previous file is untouched. Returns true on success.

- `APP`: the application.
- `PATH-TEXT`: file name as typed by the user.

- `TO-Y`: end world y.
- `BUTTON`: the button held.
- `STEPS`: number of intermediate pointer moves.

Places a shape of the library, as the insert tool places a block: a click for where, a second click

### `step-shape-group` (function)

```lisp
(step-shape-group app direction)
```

Show the next group of shapes, or the one before, going round, from the first row.

- `APP`: the application.
- `DIRECTION`: 1 for the next, -1 for the one before.

Text produced by the keyboard layout, one or more code points.

Places a text annotation: a click puts it at that point, and dragging from the point
sets the direction of its baseline. The content is asked for when the button is released.

```lisp
(toggle-menu app kind anchor)
```

Open the menu KIND, or close it when it is already open.

### `tool` (type)

Base of every tool.

### `tool-anchor` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-anchor tool)
```

Return the last point TOOL has been given, as an (x . y) cons, which ortho and polar hold the
next one to and a typed length is measured from; NIL when it has none.

- `TOOL`: a tool.

### `tool-in-progress-p` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-in-progress-p tool)
```

Return true when TOOL is in the middle of an operation that Escape cancels.

- `TOOL`: a tool.

### `tool-kind` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-kind tool)
```

Return the keyword naming TOOL.

- `TOOL`: a tool.

### `tool-preview-entities` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-preview-entities tool app)
```

Return the entities TOOL would create now, drawn as a preview.

- `TOOL`: a tool.
- `APP`: the application.

### `tool-prompt` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-prompt tool app)
```

Return the hint shown in the status bar while TOOL is active.

- `TOOL`: a tool.
- `APP`: the application.

### `tool-typed-number` (generic function)

```lisp
(tool-typed-number tool app number)
```

Take a plain number that was typed while TOOL waits for a point: an angle in degrees for rotate,
a factor for scale. Return true when the tool used it, and NIL when it has no use for one now, so that the text
is read as a point.

- `TOOL`: a tool.
- `APP`: the application.
- `NUMBER`: the number typed.

### `typed-path` (function)

```lisp
(typed-path dialog text)
```

Return the absolute pathname that TEXT, typed in the dialog, names: relative to the folder
shown, unless it is absolute or starts with ~.

- `DIALOG`: the file dialog.
- `TEXT`: what the user typed or pasted.

### `user-shapes-directory` (function)

```lisp
(user-shapes-directory app)
```

Return the folder where the shapes that the user added are kept, in the state directory, or NIL when there is none.

- `APP`: the application.

### `viewport-corners` (function)

```lisp
(viewport-corners viewport)
```

Return the corners of a viewport on the sheet as a list of (x . y) conses, the lower left first and going round
counter-clockwise, the order that RESIZED-VIEWPORT numbers them in.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.

### `viewport-from-corners` (function)

```lisp
(viewport-from-corners app x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the viewport that fills a rectangle of the sheet and shows all of the drawing at one of the usual scales, or
NIL when the rectangle is too small.

- `APP`: the application.
- `X1`: x of one corner on the sheet.
- `Y1`: y of that corner.
- `X2`: x of the opposite corner.
- `Y2`: y of that corner.

### `visible-entries` (function)

```lisp
(visible-entries dialog)
```

Return the entries the list shows (see COMPUTE-VISIBLE-ENTRIES). The answer is kept until the
folder, the search text or the All files switch changes.

- `DIALOG`: the file dialog.

## BORBA-CAD/BASE

Units, tolerances, conditions and configuration shared by every module.

### `+angle-tolerance+` (constant)

Angles that differ by no more than this, in radians, count as equal. An arc must sweep more than
this and less than a whole turn by more than this; at the largest radius an arc of this sweep is
0.01 mm long, so no arc a drafter means is ever read as degenerate.

### `+default-frame-summary-interval-seconds+` (constant)

Seconds between aggregated frame/input summary log entries.

### `+default-log-max-bytes+` (constant)

Default size at which a log file is rotated: 5 MiB keeps hours of info-level output.

### `+default-log-max-files+` (constant)

Default number of log files kept (current plus rotated); with 5 MiB each, disk use is
bounded at 25 MiB.

### `+default-memory-sample-interval-seconds+` (constant)

Seconds between memory.sample log entries.

### `+default-window-height+` (constant)

Default window height in logical pixels.

### `+default-window-width+` (constant)

Default window width in logical pixels.

### `+drawing-resolution+` (constant)

Smallest difference a drafter can mean, in millimetres (1 micrometre). It is also the
finest zoom level: one screen pixel per resolution step.

### `+length-tolerance+` (constant)

Lengths at or below this value, in millimetres (1 nanometre), count as zero.
It is 500 times larger than the worst-case representation error at the maximum magnitude
(1.9e-9 mm), so rounding noise never makes a valid segment degenerate, and 1000 times smaller
than +DRAWING-RESOLUTION+, so no intentional feature is ever treated as degenerate.

### `+maximum-coordinate-magnitude+` (constant)

Largest absolute coordinate value accepted, in millimetres (10 km).
Site plans fit comfortably inside it. A double has 53 significant bits, so the spacing
between adjacent doubles at this magnitude is 2^-29 mm (about 1.9e-9 mm), which is the
worst-case representation error of any stored coordinate.

### `+maximum-length+` (constant)

Largest length accepted for any single length value, in millimetres. The diagonal of the
square of valid coordinates is 2.83e7 mm, so every distance between two valid points fits;
the bound also keeps every product and sum computed from lengths far from overflow.

### `borba-cad-error` (condition)

Root of every error that Borba CAD signals deliberately. Its report is a
complete, user-facing message in the language of the interface.

### `config` (type)

Central application configuration; every default lives here.

- `PROJECT-ROOT`: directory containing the project (logs and examples are relative to it).
- `LOG-DIRECTORY`: directory receiving the rotating log file.
- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: directory for what the program remembers between runs (the recent files), or NIL
to remember nothing.
- `CONFIG-DIRECTORY`: directory of the user's own configuration, which holds the init file and the commands that were
recorded, or NIL to have none.
- `LOAD-INIT`: true to run the init file at startup.
- `DRAWINGS-DIRECTORY`: folder where the file dialogs start when no drawing is being edited, or NIL for
the user's documents folder.
- `LOG-LEVEL`: least severe level that is written.
- `LOG-MAX-BYTES`: size at which the log file is rotated.
- `LOG-MAX-FILES`: number of log files kept, including the current one.
- `LOG-TO-STDERR`: also echo log lines to standard error.
- `LOG-SAMPLING`: alist of event name and N; only one in N entries of that event is written.
- `FRAME-SUMMARY-INTERVAL-SECONDS`: period of aggregated summary entries.
- `MEMORY-SAMPLE-INTERVAL-SECONDS`: period of memory samples.
- `FONT-PATH`: monospace TrueType font file, or NIL to search the usual locations.
- `WINDOW-WIDTH`: initial window width in logical pixels.
- `WINDOW-HEIGHT`: initial window height in logical pixels.
- `INITIAL-FILE`: drawing file to open at startup, or NIL.
- `LANGUAGE`: the language keyword the user asked for with an option or the environment, or NIL to use the
one saved last time and, failing that, English (US).
- `THEME`: :dark or :light, as asked for with an option or the environment, or NIL to use the one saved last
time and, failing that, the dark one.

### `config-with-arguments` (function)

```lisp
(config-with-arguments config arguments)
```

Apply core command-line options to a copy of CONFIG.
Returns two values: the new configuration and the arguments that were not recognized, in order.
Recognized: --log-dir DIR, --state-dir DIR, --config-dir DIR, --no-init, --drawings-dir DIR, --log-level LEVEL,
--log-stderr, --log-sample EVENT=N, --font PATH, --language TAG, --theme NAME, --file PATH, --width N, --height N.

- `CONFIG`: the configuration to start from.
- `ARGUMENTS`: list of argument strings.

- `CONFIG`: the configuration to start from.
- `READER`: function from variable name to string or NIL; defaults to the process environment.


### `format-decimal` (function)

```lisp

```

Format VALUE with exactly PRECISION decimals, never in exponent notation.
Rounds half away from zero on the exact binary value and never prints a negative zero.

- `PRECISION`: number of digits after the decimal point, 0 or more.

- `WITH-UNIT`: when true, append the unit abbreviation.

### `user-config-directory` (function)

```lisp
(user-config-directory &optional (reader #'environment-variable))
```

Return the folder of the user's own configuration, where the init file is: %APPDATA%\BorbaCAD on Windows,
$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/borba-cad (by default ~/.config/borba-cad) elsewhere, and a folder in the home directory when the
system does not say where.

- `READER`: function from variable name to string or NIL; defaults to the process environment.

### `user-state-directory` (function)

```lisp
(user-state-directory &optional (reader #'environment-variable))
```

Return the folder where an installed program keeps its log and what it remembers between
runs: %LOCALAPPDATA%\BorbaCAD on Windows, $XDG_STATE_HOME/borba-cad (by default
~/.local/state/borba-cad) elsewhere, and a folder in the home directory when the system does not
say where.

- `READER`: function from variable name to string or NIL; defaults to the process environment.

## BORBA-CAD/BENCH

Benchmarks: deterministic workloads W0 to W3, per-operation measurements,
GC and memory observation, instrumentation overhead, and the report writer.

### `*not-measured-statements*` (variable)

What this report does not measure, one statement per entry, as a list of lines. Stated so
that nobody reads the report as covering more.

### `+benchmark-seed+` (constant)

Seed of every generated workload; fixed so that runs are comparable.

### `environment-fingerprint` (function)

```lisp
(environment-fingerprint config)
```

Return the environment fingerprint as a list of (label . value) pairs.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration, which supplies the project root.

### `filename-timestamp` (function)

```lisp
(filename-timestamp)
```

Return the current UTC time as text usable in a file name, like 20261013T231500Z.

### `format-sample` (function)

```lisp
(format-sample value)
```

Format a timing value with a precision proportional to its magnitude.

- `VALUE`: a non-negative real number.

### `log-scenario` (function)

```lisp
(log-scenario logger scenario workload entity-count started)
```

Write the bench.run log entry of one finished scenario.

- `LOGGER`: the benchmark logger, or NIL.
- `SCENARIO`: label of the scenario.
- `WORKLOAD`: keyword naming the workload, or NIL for a scenario of its own.
- `ENTITY-COUNT`: entities in the workload, or NIL.
- `STARTED`: monotonic time at which the scenario began, in nanoseconds.

### `main` (function)

```lisp
(main)
```

Command-line entry point of the benchmarks. Exit status: 0 on success, 2 for a bad option,
1 for any other failure.

### `measure-operation` (function)

```lisp
(measure-operation name repetitions function &key (warmup 3) (unit microseconds))
```

Call FUNCTION REPETITIONS times after WARMUP untimed calls, timing each timed call.
FUNCTION receives the repetition index (the warm-up calls receive negative indices), so a
sweep can visit a different input at each step and a counter can ignore the warm-up.

- `NAME`: label of the measurement.
- `REPETITIONS`: number of timed calls.
- `FUNCTION`: function of one argument, the repetition index (negative while warming up).
- `WARMUP`: untimed calls first, so that code and caches are warm.
- `UNIT`: :microseconds or :milliseconds.

### `measurement` (type)

The outcome of timing one operation repeatedly.

- `NAME`: what was measured.
- `UNIT`: unit of the samples, :microseconds or :milliseconds.
- `SAMPLES`: the raw samples, as a vector of doubles.
- `SUMMARY`: the SAMPLE-SUMMARY of the samples.
- `WORKLOAD`: keyword of the workload it was measured on, filled in by the runner.
- `NOTES`: free-form facts observed during the measurement, as a list of strings.

### `measurement-name` (function)

```lisp
(measurement-name instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `measurement-samples` (function)

```lisp
(measurement-samples instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `measurement-summary` (function)

```lisp
(measurement-summary instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `open-benchmark-logger` (function)

```lisp
(open-benchmark-logger config log-directory)
```

Open the benchmark log.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.
- `LOG-DIRECTORY`: directory of the log, or NIL for the configured one.

### `parse-benchmark-arguments` (function)

```lisp
(parse-benchmark-arguments arguments)
```

Parse the command line into a plist of RUN-BENCHMARKS keywords plus :help.

- `ARGUMENTS`: list of strings.

### `parse-workload-list` (function)

```lisp
(parse-workload-list text)
```

Parse a comma separated list of workload names into keywords.

- `TEXT`: for example "w0,w2".

### `report-rule` (function)

```lisp
(report-rule stream character)
```

Write a horizontal rule.

- `STREAM`: the output stream.
- `CHARACTER`: the character it is drawn with.

### `rotate-list` (function)

```lisp
(rotate-list list places)
```

Return LIST rotated left by PLACES positions (modulo its length).

- `LIST`: any list.
- `PLACES`: how many elements move from the front to the back.

### `run-benchmarks` (function)

```lisp
(run-benchmarks &key (workloads (mapcar #'car *workload-sizes*)) (sizes *workload-sizes*) (quick nil) (overhead t) output-directory log-directory (progress-stream *standard-output*) (config (default-config)))
```

Run the benchmarks and write the report. Returns the report pathname. Every scenario also
leaves a bench.run entry in the benchmark log.

- `WORKLOADS`: keywords of the workloads to run, a subset of the keys of SIZES.
- `SIZES`: alist of workload keyword and entity count.
- `QUICK`: true for a short run with few repetitions, meant to prove that the harness works.
- `OVERHEAD`: true to also measure the instrumentation overhead.
- `OUTPUT-DIRECTORY`: where the report is written; defaults to bench/reports/ of the project.
- `LOG-DIRECTORY`: where the benchmark log is written; defaults to the configured log directory.
- `PROGRESS-STREAM`: where progress lines go, or NIL for silence.
- `CONFIG`: the application configuration, which supplies the project root.

### `sanitize-file-name-part` (function)

```lisp
(sanitize-file-name-part text)
```

Return TEXT with every character outside letters, digits and hyphens replaced by a hyphen.

- `TEXT`: a host name or label.

### `scaled-repetitions` (function)

```lisp
(scaled-repetitions base entity-count)
```

Return how many timed repetitions to run for an operation whose cost grows with the
drawing size.

- `BASE`: repetitions wanted for a drawing of up to +REPETITION-REFERENCE-ENTITIES+ entities.
- `ENTITY-COUNT`: number of entities in the drawing.

### `workload-sizes` (function)

```lisp
(workload-sizes)
```

Return a fresh copy of the named workloads and their entity counts.

### `write-environment` (function)

```lisp
(write-environment fingerprint stream)
```

Write the environment section.

- `FINGERPRINT`: alist of labels and values.
- `STREAM`: the output stream.

## BORBA-CAD/BENCH-GUI

Frame benchmark with the real window, OpenGL renderer and display. It needs
a graphical session, so it is separate from the headless benchmarks.

### `main` (function)

```lisp
(main)
```

Command-line entry point of the windowed benchmark. Exit status: 0 on success, 2 for a bad
option or a missing display, 1 for any other failure.

### `run-gui-benchmarks` (function)

```lisp
(run-gui-benchmarks &key (workloads *default-gui-workloads*) (frames +default-frames+) output-directory (progress-stream *standard-output*) (config (default-config)))
```

Render each workload in a real window and write the report. Returns the report pathname.

- `WORKLOADS`: keywords of the workloads to render.
- `FRAMES`: frames measured per run.
- `OUTPUT-DIRECTORY`: where the report goes; defaults to bench/reports/ of the project.
- `PROGRESS-STREAM`: where progress lines go, or NIL.
- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.

## BORBA-CAD/DOMAIN

The drawing model: entities, layers, the drawing aggregate, change sets,
history, picking and snapping. Pure data and rules; no rendering, input or file I/O.

### `*hatch-patterns*` (variable)

The ways a hatch fills its region: solid, with parallel lines, or with two sets of lines at right angles.

### `*layer-palette*` (variable)

The palette as RGB values.

### `*sheet-papers*` (variable)

The papers a sheet may be on, which the plot module gives sizes to.

### `*spreadable-kinds*` (variable)

The kinds of entity that can be spread.

### `*title-field-keys*` (variable)

The fields of the title block that the user writes, in the order they are asked and kept.

### `+default-grid-spacing+` (constant)

Default grid spacing in millimetres (10 cm): a convenient step for building plans.

### `+default-hatch-angle+` (constant)

The direction of the lines of a new hatch: 45 degrees.

### `+default-layer-gray+` (constant)

Gray level of the default layer color: near-white, readable on the dark canvas.

### `+default-layer-id+` (constant)

Id of the layer every drawing starts with; it cannot be deleted.

### `+default-text-height+` (constant)

Default text height in millimetres: 2.5 mm printed at a 1:100 plan scale.

### `+history-depth-limit+` (constant)

Most change sets kept for undo. Bounds the memory used by history (NFR-6).

### `+inspector-maximum-vertex-rows+` (constant)

Number of polyline vertices that the inspector lists for numeric editing.

### `+maximum-array-entities+` (constant)

Most entities one array may add.

### `+maximum-block-entities+` (constant)

Most entities one block may hold.

### `+maximum-block-name-length+` (constant)

Longest accepted block name.

### `+maximum-dimension-precision+` (constant)

Largest number of decimals shown in a dimension text.

### `+maximum-hatch-lines+` (constant)

Most lines one set of a hatch may have, which keeps a spacing that is tiny next to the region from making a hatch
of millions of segments.

### `+maximum-insert-scale+` (constant)

Largest scale of a placement, which is also the reciprocal of the smallest.

### `+maximum-layer-name-length+` (constant)

Longest accepted layer name.

### `+maximum-polygon-sides+` (constant)

Largest number of sides of a regular polygon.

### `+maximum-polyline-grips+` (constant)

Most vertices of a polyline that get a grip; a polyline with more is changed in the inspector.

### `+maximum-polyline-vertices+` (constant)

Largest vertex count of a polyline. Bounds memory use when loading untrusted files.

### `+maximum-sheets+` (constant)

Most sheets a drawing may have.

### `+maximum-text-length+` (constant)

Largest number of characters in a text annotation.

### `+maximum-title-field-length+` (constant)

Longest text a field of the title block may hold.

### `+maximum-viewports+` (constant)

Most viewports a sheet may have.

### `+minimum-grid-spacing+` (constant)

Smallest accepted grid spacing in millimetres. Finer grids add nothing the user can click
at any practical zoom and would be hidden by the adaptive grid display.

### `add-layer` (function)

```lisp
(add-layer drawing name color)
```

Create a layer with the next layer id and return it. Signals LAYER-ERROR when NAME is empty,
too long or already used.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `NAME`: layer name.
- `COLOR`: an RGB color.

### `add-sheet` (function)

```lisp
(add-sheet drawing sheet)
```

Put a sheet after the others. Returns its position.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SHEET`: the sheet.

### `add-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(add-viewport drawing index viewport)
```

Put a viewport on a sheet, above the others. Returns the position of the viewport on the sheet.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `INDEX`: the position of the sheet.
- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.

### `angular-dimension-end-angle` (function)

```lisp
(angular-dimension-end-angle instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `angular-dimension-entity` (type)

An angle dimension: the angle at a vertex between two arms, drawn as an arc of a given radius from the first arm
to the second, counter-clockwise. The value is derived from the arms.

- `VERTEX-X`: x of the vertex.
- `VERTEX-Y`: y of the vertex.
- `START-ANGLE`: direction of the first arm, in radians counter-clockwise from +x, in [0, 2 pi).
- `END-ANGLE`: direction of the second arm, in [0, 2 pi).
- `RADIUS`: radius of the dimension arc.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size); every part of the dimension scales with it.
- `PRECISION`: decimals of the angle in degrees.
- `SWEEP`: the angle measured, in (0, 2 pi); computed from the arms.
- `TEXT`: the displayed text, computed at construction.
- `LAYOUT`: the geometric layout of all parts, computed at construction.

```lisp
(apply-changes drawing changes)
```

### `arc-entity` (type)

An arc: the part of a circle that is swept counter-clockwise from a start angle to an end angle.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: radius, above the length tolerance.
- `START-ANGLE`: direction of the start, in radians counter-clockwise from +x, in [0, 2 pi).
- `END-ANGLE`: direction of the end, in [0, 2 pi).
- `SWEEP`: how far the arc turns from its start to its end, in (0, 2 pi); computed from the angles.

### `array-changes` (function)

```lisp
(array-changes drawing ids transforms)
```

Return the change set that adds, for each of TRANSFORMS, a copy of every entity with IDS made by it, as new entities
on the layer of the original, and how many were skipped because they are missing or on a locked layer. Signals
EDIT-REFUSED when nothing can be copied or the array would add too many entities, and INVALID-ENTITY when a copy would
leave the drawing limits.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `IDS`: list of entity ids.
- `TRANSFORMS`: list of functions of an entity that return the copy, one for each item but the first.

### `assign-layer-changes` (function)

```lisp
(assign-layer-changes drawing ids layer-id)
```

Return the change set that moves the entities with IDS to another layer, and the number
skipped. Signals LAYER-ERROR when the layer does not exist.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `IDS`: list of entity ids.
- `LAYER-ID`: id of the destination layer.

### `block-definition` (type)

A named group of entities.

- `NAME`: what the block is called, with the case the user gave it.
- `ENTITIES`: the entities, in the order they were drawn, with the base point of the block at the origin.
- `BOUNDS`: the bounds of the entities.

### `dimension-entity` (type)

An aligned linear dimension between two definition points. The measured value is derived
from the points; the displayed text is derived from it, the unit and the precision.

- `X1`: x of the first definition point.
- `Y1`: y of the first definition point.
- `X2`: x of the second definition point.
- `Y2`: y of the second definition point.
- `OFFSET`: signed distance of the dimension line from the measured segment (left of P1-P2 is positive).
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size); every part of the dimension scales with it.
- `UNIT`: length unit keyword of the displayed text.
- `PRECISION`: decimals in the displayed text.
- `TEXT`: the displayed text, computed at construction.
- `LAYOUT`: the geometric layout of all parts, computed at construction.

### `drawing` (type)

The drawing aggregate: layers, entities and drawing-wide settings.

- `STORE`: the entities.
- `LAYERS`: id to layer.
- `LAYER-ORDER`: layer ids in display order.
- `BLOCKS`: block name, without case, to the block definition.
- `CURRENT-LAYER-ID`: layer that receives new entities.
- `UNIT`: unit in which coordinates are stored (always :millimeter in format version 1).
- `DISPLAY-UNIT`: unit in which the user sees and types lengths.
- `GRID-SPACING`: grid and snap spacing in millimetres.
- `GRID-VISIBLE`: whether the grid is drawn.
- `SNAP-TO-GRID`: whether pointer positions snap to the grid.
- `OBJECT-SNAP`: whether pointer positions snap to entity endpoints and centers.
- `DEFAULT-TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height used for new text and dimensions.
- `NEXT-ENTITY-ID`: id the next new entity receives; never decreases.
- `NEXT-LAYER-ID`: id the next new layer receives; never decreases.
- `SHEETS`: the sheets that are printed, in order; see sheets.lisp.
- `REVISION`: counter bumped by every change, for dirty tracking and cache invalidation.

### `ellipse-entity` (type)

An ellipse, given by its center, its two semi-axes and the direction of the first.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis in the direction ROTATION, above the length tolerance.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it, above the length tolerance.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first axis, in radians counter-clockwise from +x, in [0, pi): an ellipse
is the same after half a turn.
