## BORBA-CAD/DOUBLES

The functions of Common Lisp that give a single-float when they are given a whole number (SQRT 2
is 1.4142135), made to give a double-float, as the program counts. The user package takes these in place of those
of Common Lisp, so that what is typed in the console is as exact as the drawing.

### `acos` (function)

```lisp
(acos number)
```

Return the arc cosine of NUMBER, in radians and double-float.

- `NUMBER`: a number.

### `asin` (function)

```lisp
(asin number)
```

Return the arc sine of NUMBER, in radians and double-float.

- `NUMBER`: a number.

### `atan` (function)

```lisp
(atan number &optional (other nil other-p))
```

Return the arc tangent of NUMBER, or of NUMBER over OTHER when it is given, in radians and double-float.

- `NUMBER`: a number, or the y of a direction.
- `OTHER`: the x of a direction.

### `cos` (function)

```lisp
(cos radians)
```

Return the cosine of an angle given in radians, in double-float.

- `RADIANS`: a number.

### `exp` (function)

```lisp
(exp number)
```

Return e to the power NUMBER, in double-float.

- `NUMBER`: a number.

### `float` (function)

```lisp
(float number &optional (prototype 1.0d0))
```

Return NUMBER as a floating point number: a double-float unless PROTOTYPE is a float of another format.

- `NUMBER`: a real number.
- `PROTOTYPE`: a float whose format the result has; double-float when it is left out.

### `log` (function)

```lisp
(log number &optional (base nil base-p))
```

Return the logarithm of NUMBER, natural or to BASE when it is given, in double-float.

- `NUMBER`: a number.
- `BASE`: the base of the logarithm.

### `sin` (function)

```lisp
(sin radians)
```

Return the sine of an angle given in radians, in double-float.

- `RADIANS`: a number.

### `sqrt` (function)

```lisp
(sqrt number)
```

Return the square root of NUMBER, in double-float for a real number.

- `NUMBER`: a number.

### `tan` (function)

```lisp
(tan radians)
```

Return the tangent of an angle given in radians, in double-float.

- `RADIANS`: a number.

## BORBA-CAD/DXF

Import and export of drawings in the ASCII DXF format: a reader of the groups of a DXF
file, the mapping of its entities to the entities of a drawing with a report of what could not be
mapped, and a writer of a DXF file of the oldest widely read version (AutoCAD R12).

### `+maximum-groups+` (constant)

Most groups (pairs of lines) a DXF file may have: well above the largest drawing the program is meant
to open, and enough to bound the memory a file can ask for.

### `aci->rgb` (function)

```lisp
(aci->rgb index)
```

Return the color of an AutoCAD color index as an RGB of the domain, or NIL for an index that has
none (0 and 256 mean the color of the block or of the layer, and a negative one is a layer that is off,
whose color is that of its absolute value).

- `INDEX`: an integer.

### `decode-dxf-octets` (function)

```lisp
(decode-dxf-octets octets)
```

Return the text of a DXF file given its bytes: UTF-8 when they are well-formed UTF-8 (which pure
ASCII is), and Windows Western European otherwise, the code page that DXF files from Brazil have
been written in. Signals DRAWING-FILE-ERROR for a binary DXF file.

- `OCTETS`: the bytes of the file.

### `document-units` (function)

```lisp
(document-units document)
```

Return the unit in which the drawing of DOCUMENT is made, as :millimeter, :centimeter or :meter,
:inch or :foot, or NIL when the file does not say (no $INSUNITS, or 0, which is unitless) or says one
that the program does not take.

- `DOCUMENT`: a DXF-DOCUMENT.

### `dxf-document` (type)

What a DXF file holds that a drawing is made from.

- `HEADER`: the header variables, as records.
- `LAYERS`: the layer records of the layer table.
- `BLOCKS`: the blocks, as DXF-BLOCK values.
- `ENTITIES`: the entity records of the entities section, in order.

### `dxf-document-blocks` (function)

```lisp
(dxf-document-blocks instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dxf-document-entities` (function)

```lisp
(dxf-document-entities instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dxf-document-header` (function)

```lisp
(dxf-document-header instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dxf-document-layers` (function)

```lisp
(dxf-document-layers instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dxf-report` (type)

What an import did and could not do.

- `IMPORTED`: hash table from the kind of entity to how many were made.
- `IGNORED`: hash table from the DXF type of an entity to how many were left out.
- `NOTES`: the sentences that say what was approximated, moved or refused, newest first.
- `PROBLEMS`: the reasons that entities could not be made, newest first.

### `export-drawing-text` (function)

```lisp
(export-drawing-text drawing)
```

Return the DXF text of DRAWING, and a DXF-REPORT of how many entities of each kind were written and
what changed shape in the writing.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.

### `export-summary` (function)

```lisp
(export-summary report)
```

Return what an export did as a sentence or two for the user.

- `REPORT`: the DXF-REPORT of the export.

### `import-document` (function)

```lisp
(import-document document &key units)
```

Make a drawing from a DXF-DOCUMENT. Returns two values: the drawing and the DXF-REPORT that says how
many entities of each kind were made, which DXF types were left out, and what was approximated.

- `DOCUMENT`: the DXF-DOCUMENT.
- `UNITS`: the unit of the numbers in the file as :millimeter, :centimeter, :meter, :inch or :foot, or NIL to
take the one the file says, and millimetres when it says none.

### `load-dxf` (function)

```lisp
(load-dxf path &key units)
```

Read the DXF file at PATH and make a drawing from it. Returns the drawing and the DXF-REPORT of what was
imported and left out. Signals a DRAWING-FILE-ERROR subclass on any problem.

- `PATH`: pathname of the file.
- `UNITS`: the unit of its numbers (:millimeter, :centimeter, :meter, :inch or :foot), or NIL to take the one it
says, and millimetres when it says none.

### `make-dxf-report` (function)

```lisp
(make-dxf-report)
```

(undocumented)

### `parse-dxf-text` (function)

```lisp
(parse-dxf-text text)
```

Read the text of an ASCII DXF file and return the DXF-DOCUMENT that has what a drawing is made
from: the header variables, the layers, the blocks and the entities. Signals DRAWING-FILE-ERROR,
naming the line, when the text is not a DXF file.

- `TEXT`: the text of the file.

### `read-dxf-file` (function)

```lisp
(read-dxf-file path)
```

Read and parse the ASCII DXF file at PATH, and return its DXF-DOCUMENT. Signals a DRAWING-FILE-ERROR
subclass when the file cannot be read or is not a DXF file.

- `PATH`: pathname of the file.

### `record` (type)

One record of a DXF file: group 0 gives its type, and the groups after it, up to the next group 0, its
data.

- `TYPE`: the type, a string such as "LINE", or the name of a header variable such as "$INSUNITS".
- `PAIRS`: the groups after it, as (code . value) conses with the value as a string, in order.
- `LINE`: the line of the file the record starts on.
- `CHILDREN`: for a polyline, its vertex records, in order.

### `record-children` (function)

```lisp
(record-children instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `record-line` (function)

```lisp
(record-line instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `record-pairs` (function)

```lisp
(record-pairs instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `record-type` (function)

```lisp
(record-type instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `report-ignored` (function)

```lisp
(report-ignored report)
```

Return how many entities of each DXF type were left out, as an alist from the type name to the
count, the most frequent first.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `report-ignored-total` (function)

```lisp
(report-ignored-total report)
```

Return how many entities were left out.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `report-imported` (function)

```lisp
(report-imported report)
```

Return how many entities of each kind were made, as an alist from the kind keyword to the count.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `report-imported-total` (function)

```lisp
(report-imported-total report)
```

Return how many entities were made.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `report-notes` (function)

```lisp
(report-notes report)
```

Return the sentences that say what was approximated, moved or refused, oldest first.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `report-summary` (function)

```lisp
(report-summary report)
```

Return the report as a few sentences for the user: how many entities were made, which DXF types
were left out and how many of each, and the notes.

- `REPORT`: a DXF-REPORT.

### `rgb->aci` (function)

```lisp
(rgb->aci color)
```

Return the index of the color of the AutoCAD color index that is nearest to COLOR. White stays
7, which is white on a dark background and black on a light one, as the program draws.

- `COLOR`: an RGB of the domain.

### `save-dxf` (function)

```lisp
(save-dxf drawing path)
```

Save DRAWING as an ASCII DXF file of version R12 at PATH, atomically as drawings are saved. Returns
three values: the saved pathname, the properties of the save (see PERSIST:SAVE-TEXT-FILE) and the
DXF-REPORT of what was written and what changed shape.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `PATH`: destination pathname.

## BORBA-CAD/GEOMETRY

Computational geometry on world coordinates (double-float millimetres).
No screen, pixel or rendering concepts appear here.

### `+dimension-arrow-length-ratio+` (constant)

Arrowhead length as a fraction of the text height.

### `+dimension-arrow-width-ratio+` (constant)

Arrowhead width as a fraction of the arrowhead length.

### `+dimension-extension-gap-ratio+` (constant)

Gap between a definition point and the start of its extension line, as a fraction of the
text height.

### `+dimension-extension-overshoot-ratio+` (constant)

How far an extension line runs past the dimension line, as a fraction of the text height.

### `+dimension-text-gap-ratio+` (constant)

Gap between the dimension line and the text baseline, as a fraction of the text height.

### `+pi+` (constant)

Pi as a double-float.

### `+text-advance-ratio+` (constant)

Horizontal advance of one character, as a fraction of the text height (em size).

### `+text-ascent-ratio+` (constant)

Height of the nominal text box above the baseline, as a fraction of the text height.
Tall enough for accented capital letters.

### `+text-descent-ratio+` (constant)

Depth of the nominal text box below the baseline, as a fraction of the text height.

### `+two-pi+` (constant)

A full turn in radians.

### `angle-between-rays` (function)

```lisp
(angle-between-rays vertex-x vertex-y first-x first-y second-x second-y)
```

Return the angle at a vertex between the rays toward two points, in radians from 0 to pi, or NIL when one of
the points is at the vertex and so gives no direction.

- `VERTEX-X`: x of the vertex.
- `VERTEX-Y`: y of the vertex.
- `FIRST-X`: x of a point on the first ray.
- `FIRST-Y`: y of a point on the first ray.
- `SECOND-X`: x of a point on the second ray.
- `SECOND-Y`: y of a point on the second ray.

### `angle-within-sweep-p` (function)

```lisp
(angle-within-sweep-p angle start-angle sweep)
```

Return true when ANGLE lies on the arc that starts at START-ANGLE and turns SWEEP counter-clockwise,
its ends included, to the angle tolerance.

- `ANGLE`: a direction in radians.
- `START-ANGLE`: the angle at which the arc starts.
- `SWEEP`: how far it turns, in (0, 2 pi).

### `annotation-layout` (type)

Everything needed to draw a radius, diameter or angle dimension, in world coordinates.

- `SEGMENTS`: doubles, x1 y1 x2 y2 for each stroked segment.
- `ARROWHEADS`: doubles, six for each filled triangle (tip, base corner, base corner).
- `TEXT-X`: x of the text baseline start.
- `TEXT-Y`: y of the text baseline start.
- `TEXT-ANGLE`: text direction in radians.
- `TEXT-WIDTH`: width of the text line.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size).
- `BOUNDS`: bounds of every part.

### `annotation-layout-arrowheads` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-arrowheads instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-bounds instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-segments` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-segments instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-text-angle` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-text-angle instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-text-height` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-text-height instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-text-width` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-text-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-text-x` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-text-x instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-layout-text-y` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-layout-text-y instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `annotation-pick-distance` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-pick-distance layout x y)
```

Return the distance from a point to the nearest stroke or the text of an annotation.

- `LAYOUT`: an annotation layout.
- `X`: point x.
- `Y`: point y.

### `annotation-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(annotation-touches-bounds-p layout bounds)
```

Return true when a stroke or the text of an annotation has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge.

- `LAYOUT`: an annotation layout.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `arc-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(arc-bounds center-x center-y radius start-angle sweep)
```

Return the bounds of an arc: its two ends and the points of the circle farthest along an axis
that the arc passes through.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `START-ANGLE`: the angle at which the arc starts.
- `SWEEP`: how far it turns counter-clockwise, in (0, 2 pi).

### `arc-end-point` (function)

```lisp
(arc-end-point center-x center-y radius angle)
```

Return, as two values, the point of the circle at ANGLE.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `ANGLE`: direction from the center, in radians.

### `arc-length` (function)

```lisp
(arc-length radius sweep)
```

Return the length of an arc.

- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `SWEEP`: how far it turns, in radians.

### `arc-sweep` (function)

```lisp
(arc-sweep start-angle end-angle)
```

Return how far the arc from START-ANGLE counter-clockwise to END-ANGLE turns, in [0, 2 pi).

- `START-ANGLE`: the angle at which the arc starts, in radians.
- `END-ANGLE`: the angle at which it ends, in radians.

### `arc-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(arc-touches-bounds-p center-x center-y radius start-angle sweep bounds)
```

Return true when an arc has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge. The arc is one piece, so if
a part of it is in the window either an end is, or the arc crosses an edge of the window on its way.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `START-ANGLE`: the angle at which the arc starts.
- `SWEEP`: how far it turns counter-clockwise.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `bounds` (type)

An axis-aligned rectangle in world coordinates (millimetres).

- `MIN-X`: smallest x.
- `MIN-Y`: smallest y.
- `MAX-X`: largest x; at least MIN-X.
- `MAX-Y`: largest y; at least MIN-Y.

### `bounds-center-x` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-center-x bounds)
```

Return the x of the center of BOUNDS.

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.

### `bounds-center-y` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-center-y bounds)
```

Return the y of the center of BOUNDS.

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.

### `bounds-contains-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-contains-bounds-p outer inner)
```

Return true when INNER lies entirely inside OUTER (touching edges counts).

- `OUTER`: the containing bounds.
- `INNER`: the bounds that must fit.

### `bounds-contains-point-p` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-contains-point-p bounds x y)
```

Return true when the point lies inside BOUNDS (the boundary counts).

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.
- `X`: point x.
- `Y`: point y.

### `bounds-from-corners` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-from-corners x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the bounds of two opposite corners given in any order.

- `X1`: x of the first corner.
- `Y1`: y of the first corner.
- `X2`: x of the opposite corner.
- `Y2`: y of the opposite corner.

### `bounds-height` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-height bounds)
```

Return the extent of BOUNDS along y.

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.

### `bounds-intersect-p` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-intersect-p first second)
```

Return true when the two bounds share at least one point (touching counts).

- `FIRST`: a bounds value.
- `SECOND`: a bounds value.

### `bounds-max-x` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-max-x instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `bounds-max-y` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-max-y instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `bounds-min-x` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-min-x instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `bounds-min-y` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-min-y instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `bounds-near-point-p` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-near-point-p bounds x y radius)
```

Return true when the point lies within RADIUS of BOUNDS along both axes (a cheap
pre-filter: every shape inside BOUNDS closer than RADIUS to the point passes it).

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.
- `X`: point x.
- `Y`: point y.
- `RADIUS`: non-negative search radius.

### `bounds-union` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-union first second)
```

Return the smallest bounds containing both arguments.

- `FIRST`: a bounds value.
- `SECOND`: a bounds value.

### `bounds-width` (function)

```lisp
(bounds-width bounds)
```

Return the extent of BOUNDS along x.

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.

### `circle-area` (function)

```lisp
(circle-area radius)
```

Return the area of a circle.

- `RADIUS`: circle radius.

### `circle-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(circle-bounds center-x center-y radius)
```

Return the bounds of a circle.

- `CENTER-X`: circle center x.
- `CENTER-Y`: circle center y.
- `RADIUS`: circle radius.

### `circle-circle-intersections` (function)

```lisp
(circle-circle-intersections x1 y1 radius1 x2 y2 radius2)
```

Return the points where two circles cross, as a list of (x . y) conses: none, one when they touch, or two.

- `X1`: x of the center of the first circle.
- `Y1`: y of its center.
- `RADIUS1`: its radius.
- `X2`: x of the center of the second circle.
- `Y2`: y of its center.
- `RADIUS2`: its radius.

### `circle-circumference` (function)

```lisp
(circle-circumference radius)
```

Return the circumference of a circle.

- `RADIUS`: circle radius.

### `circle-outline-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(circle-outline-touches-bounds-p center-x center-y radius bounds)
```

Return true when the outline of a circle has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge: the window
holds a point at the distance RADIUS from the center, which is so when the nearest point of the
window is no farther than that and the farthest corner no nearer.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `circle-perpendicular-feet` (function)

```lisp
(circle-perpendicular-feet px py center-x center-y radius)
```

Return the points of a circle where the line through them and the center passes through a point, as a list of
two (x . y) conses; none when the point is the center.

- `PX`: x of the point.
- `PY`: y of the point.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center of the circle.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: its radius.

### `circle-tangent-points` (function)

```lisp
(circle-tangent-points px py center-x center-y radius)
```

Return the points of a circle where a line from a point outside it touches it, as a list of two (x . y)
conses; none when the point is on the circle or inside it.

- `PX`: x of the point.
- `PY`: y of the point.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center of the circle.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: its radius.

### `clip-segment-to-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(clip-segment-to-bounds x1 y1 x2 y2 bounds)
```

Clip a segment to BOUNDS (Liang-Barsky). Returns four values, the clipped x1 y1 x2 y2,
or NIL when no part of the segment lies inside. Done in double precision so far-away
endpoints never reach single-float vertex buffers.

- `X1`: x of the segment start.
- `Y1`: y of the segment start.
- `X2`: x of the segment end.
- `Y2`: y of the segment end.
- `BOUNDS`: the clipping rectangle.

### `closest-point-on-segment` (function)

```lisp
(closest-point-on-segment px py x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the point of the segment nearest to P as three values: x, y and the parameter t in
[0, 1] along the segment. A zero-length segment yields its single point with t = 0.

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `X1`: x of the segment start.
- `Y1`: y of the segment start.
- `X2`: x of the segment end.
- `Y2`: y of the segment end.

### `compute-angular-dimension-layout` (function)

```lisp
(compute-angular-dimension-layout vertex-x vertex-y start-angle end-angle radius text-height character-count)
```

Compute the layout of an angle dimension: the two arms as extension lines, an arc of the given radius from
the first arm to the second, counter-clockwise, an arrowhead at each end of the arc, and the text, level, beyond
the middle of the arc.

- `VERTEX-X`: x of the vertex.
- `VERTEX-Y`: y of the vertex.
- `START-ANGLE`: direction of the first arm, in radians.
- `END-ANGLE`: direction of the second arm; the dimension turns counter-clockwise from the first to this one.
- `RADIUS`: radius of the dimension arc.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size) that sizes every part.
- `CHARACTER-COUNT`: characters in the dimension text.

### `compute-dimension-layout` (function)

```lisp
(compute-dimension-layout x1 y1 x2 y2 offset text-height character-count)
```

Compute the layout of an aligned dimension between two points.
The dimension line is parallel to the segment P1-P2 and displaced by OFFSET along its left
normal (negative OFFSET displaces to the right). P1 and P2 must be farther apart than the
length tolerance.

- `X1`: x of the first definition point.
- `Y1`: y of the first definition point.
- `X2`: x of the second definition point.
- `Y2`: y of the second definition point.
- `OFFSET`: signed distance of the dimension line from the measured segment.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size) that sizes every part.
- `CHARACTER-COUNT`: number of characters in the dimension text.

### `compute-radial-dimension-layout` (function)

```lisp
(compute-radial-dimension-layout center-x center-y radius angle diameter text-height character-count)
```

Compute the layout of a radius or diameter dimension of a circle. A radius dimension is a leader from the center
through the circle at ANGLE, with an arrowhead on the circle and the text on a tail beyond it. A diameter
dimension is the line through the center at ANGLE, with an arrowhead at each end and the text above it.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: radius of the circle.
- `ANGLE`: direction of the leader from the center, in radians.
- `DIAMETER`: true for a diameter dimension.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size) that sizes every part.
- `CHARACTER-COUNT`: characters in the dimension text.

### `contour-area` (function)

```lisp
(contour-area coordinates)
```

Return the area enclosed by a closed contour whose vertices are given in order, by the shoelace formula. The
contour closes itself, so the first vertex is not repeated. A contour that crosses itself gives the difference
of its loops, which is what the formula means; fewer than three vertices enclose nothing.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...

### `contour-signed-area` (function)

```lisp
(contour-signed-area coordinates)
```

Return the area of a polygon, positive when it goes around counter-clockwise and negative when clockwise.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...

### `dimension-layout` (type)

Everything needed to draw one aligned dimension, in world coordinates.

- `EXTENSION-LINES`: 8 doubles, two segments (x1 y1 x2 y2 each).
- `DIMENSION-LINE`: 4 doubles, one segment.
- `ARROWHEADS`: 12 doubles, two filled triangles (tip, base corner, base corner).
- `TEXT-X`: x of the text baseline start.
- `TEXT-Y`: y of the text baseline start.
- `TEXT-ANGLE`: text direction in radians, chosen so the text reads left to right.
- `TEXT-WIDTH`: width of the text line.
- `TEXT-HEIGHT`: text height (em size).
- `BOUNDS`: bounds of every part.

### `dimension-layout-arrowheads` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-arrowheads instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-bounds instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-dimension-line` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-dimension-line instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-extension-lines` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-extension-lines instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-text-angle` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-text-angle instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-text-height` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-text-height instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-text-width` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-text-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-text-x` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-text-x instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `dimension-layout-text-y` (function)

```lisp
(dimension-layout-text-y instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `distance` (function)

```lisp
(distance x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the Euclidean distance between two points.

- `X1`: x of the first point.
- `Y1`: y of the first point.
- `X2`: x of the second point.
- `Y2`: y of the second point.

### `ellipse-area` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse-area radius-x radius-y)
```

Return the area of an ellipse.

- `RADIUS-X`: one semi-axis.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the other.

### `ellipse-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse-bounds center-x center-y radius-x radius-y rotation)
```

Return the bounds of an ellipse.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis in the direction ROTATION.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first axis, in radians.

### `ellipse-perimeter` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse-perimeter radius-x radius-y)
```

Return the perimeter of an ellipse, by Ramanujan's second approximation, which is within a
few parts per billion for the ellipses of a drawing.

- `RADIUS-X`: one semi-axis.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the other.

### `ellipse-points` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse-points center-x center-y radius-x radius-y rotation count)
```

Return the vertices of the polygon of COUNT sides inscribed in an ellipse, starting at the end
of the first axis, as a vector of alternating x and y values.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis in the direction ROTATION.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first axis, in radians.
- `COUNT`: number of vertices.

### `ellipse-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse-touches-bounds-p center-x center-y radius-x radius-y rotation bounds)
```

Return true when the outline of an ellipse has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge, judged on
the polygon of +ELLIPSE-OUTLINE-POINTS+ sides inscribed in it.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis in the direction ROTATION.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first axis, in radians.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `expand-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(expand-bounds bounds margin)
```

Return BOUNDS grown by MARGIN on every side (shrunk when MARGIN is negative).

- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.
- `MARGIN`: distance added on each side, in millimetres.

### `hatch-line-count` (function)

```lisp
(hatch-line-count coordinates angle spacing origin-x origin-y)
```

Return how many parallel lines, SPACING apart and in the direction ANGLE, cross a polygon.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...
- `ANGLE`: direction of the lines, in radians.
- `SPACING`: distance between two lines.
- `ORIGIN-X`: x of the point one of the lines passes through.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: y of that point.

### `hatch-segments` (function)

```lisp
(hatch-segments coordinates angle spacing origin-x origin-y &optional holes)
```

Return the segments of the parallel lines that hatch a polygon, as a simple array of double-float holding x1 y1 x2 y2
for each. The lines are SPACING apart in the direction ANGLE, one of them through the origin, and are cut where they
leave the polygon, so a concave polygon is hatched only inside, and where they enter a hole.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...
- `ANGLE`: direction of the lines, in radians.
- `SPACING`: distance between two lines, above zero.
- `ORIGIN-X`: x of the point one of the lines passes through.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: y of that point.
- `HOLES`: a list of polygons, each such an array, that are left unhatched.

### `make-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(make-bounds min-x min-y max-x max-y)
```

(undocumented)

### `normalize-angle` (function)

```lisp
(normalize-angle radians)
```

Return RADIANS reduced to the range [0, 2 pi).

- `RADIANS`: any finite angle in radians.

### `oriented-box-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(oriented-box-bounds origin-x origin-y length ascent descent angle)
```

Return the axis-aligned bounds of a rotated box.
The box spans 0..LENGTH along the direction ANGLE from the origin, and -DESCENT..ASCENT
perpendicular to it (left of the direction is positive).

- `ORIGIN-X`: x of the box origin (baseline start).
- `ORIGIN-Y`: y of the box origin.
- `LENGTH`: extent along the direction.
- `ASCENT`: extent on the left side of the direction.
- `DESCENT`: extent on the right side of the direction.
- `ANGLE`: direction in radians, counter-clockwise from +x.

### `oriented-box-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(oriented-box-touches-bounds-p origin-x origin-y length ascent descent angle bounds)
```

Return true when a filled rotated box and BOUNDS have a point in common: an edge of the box
reaches the window, or the window lies inside the box. The box is the one that
ORIENTED-BOX-BOUNDS describes.

- `ORIGIN-X`: x of the box origin.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: y of the box origin.
- `LENGTH`: extent along the direction.
- `ASCENT`: extent on the left of the direction.
- `DESCENT`: extent on the right of the direction.
- `ANGLE`: direction in radians, counter-clockwise from +x.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `path-even-distances` (function)

```lisp
(path-even-distances length closed count)
```

Return COUNT distances along a path of the given length, the first at its start, at equal steps: the last at its
end when the path is open, and a step short of it when it is closed, where the end is the start.

- `LENGTH`: the length of the path.
- `CLOSED`: true for a closed path.
- `COUNT`: how many distances, at least one.

### `path-length` (function)

```lisp
(path-length coordinates closed)
```

Return the length of a path.

- `COORDINATES`: flat array of the vertices.
- `CLOSED`: true when a segment joins the last vertex back to the first.

### `path-points-at` (function)

```lisp
(path-points-at coordinates closed distances)
```

Return the points of a path at some distances from its start, as a list of (x y angle) lists, where the angle is the
direction of the path there, in radians. A vertex is given the direction of the segment that leaves it, and the end of
an open path that of its last segment. A distance past the end is taken to be the end.

- `COORDINATES`: flat array of the vertices, with at least two apart.
- `CLOSED`: true when a segment joins the last vertex back to the first.
- `DISTANCES`: ascending list of distances from the start.

### `path-segment-lengths` (function)

```lisp
(path-segment-lengths coordinates closed)
```

Return the lengths of the segments of a path as a vector, in order; a closed path has one more, from the last vertex
back to the first.

- `COORDINATES`: flat array of the vertices.
- `CLOSED`: true when a segment joins the last vertex back to the first.

### `path-spaced-distances` (function)

```lisp
(path-spaced-distances length closed spacing)
```

Return the distances along a path of the given length at which something is put every SPACING from its start: as
many as fit, the one at the end of an open path included when the spacing divides the length, and for a closed path
none at the end, where it would sit on the first.

- `LENGTH`: the length of the path.
- `CLOSED`: true for a closed path.
- `SPACING`: the distance between one and the next, above zero.

### `point-arc-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-arc-distance px py center-x center-y radius start-angle sweep)
```

Return the distance from a point to an arc: to the circle where the point lies in the direction of
the arc, and to the nearer end otherwise. From the center it is the radius.

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius.
- `START-ANGLE`: the angle at which the arc starts.
- `SWEEP`: how far it turns counter-clockwise.

### `point-bounds-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-bounds-distance x y bounds)
```

Return the distance from a point to the filled rectangle BOUNDS (zero inside or on it).

- `X`: point x.
- `Y`: point y.
- `BOUNDS`: a bounds value.

### `point-circle-outline-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-circle-outline-distance px py center-x center-y radius)
```

Return the distance from point P to the circle outline (zero on the circle itself, and
equal to the radius at the center).

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `CENTER-X`: circle center x.
- `CENTER-Y`: circle center y.
- `RADIUS`: circle radius.

### `point-ellipse-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-ellipse-distance px py center-x center-y radius-x radius-y rotation)
```

Return the distance from a point to the outline of an ellipse (zero on it).

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis in the direction ROTATION.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first axis, in radians.

### `point-in-polygon-p` (function)

```lisp
(point-in-polygon-p x y coordinates)
```

Return true when a point is inside a polygon, by the even-odd rule. A point on the boundary may be either.

- `X`: x of the point.
- `Y`: y of the point.
- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...

### `point-in-rings-p` (function)

```lisp
(point-in-rings-p x y coordinates holes)
```

Return true when a point is inside a polygon and not inside any of its holes.

- `X`: x of the point.
- `Y`: y of the point.
- `COORDINATES`: the polygon, a simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...
- `HOLES`: a list of polygons, each such an array.

### `point-oriented-box-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-oriented-box-distance px py origin-x origin-y length ascent descent angle)
```

Return the distance from a point to a filled rotated box (zero inside or on it).
The box is the one described by ORIENTED-BOX-BOUNDS.

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `ORIGIN-X`: x of the box origin.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: y of the box origin.
- `LENGTH`: extent along the direction.
- `ASCENT`: extent on the left of the direction.
- `DESCENT`: extent on the right of the direction.
- `ANGLE`: direction in radians, counter-clockwise from +x.

### `point-polyline-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-polyline-distance px py coordinates closed)
```

Return the distance from point P to a polyline given as flat x/y pairs.

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float, x0 y0 x1 y1 ..., at least two vertices.
- `CLOSED`: when true, the segment from the last vertex back to the first is included.

### `point-rectangle-outline-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-rectangle-outline-distance px py min-x min-y max-x max-y)
```

Return the distance from point P to the rectangle boundary, from inside or outside.

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `MIN-X`: rectangle left edge.
- `MIN-Y`: rectangle bottom edge.
- `MAX-X`: rectangle right edge.
- `MAX-Y`: rectangle top edge.

### `point-segment-distance` (function)

```lisp
(point-segment-distance px py x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the distance from point P to the segment (not the infinite line).

- `PX`: point x.
- `PY`: point y.
- `X1`: x of the segment start.
- `Y1`: y of the segment start.
- `X2`: x of the segment end.
- `Y2`: y of the segment end.

### `point-segment-foot` (function)

```lisp
(point-segment-foot px py x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return, as two values, the foot of the perpendicular from a point to a segment, or NIL when the foot is
beyond an end of the segment.

- `PX`: x of the point.
- `PY`: y of the point.
- `X1`: x of the start of the segment.
- `Y1`: y of the start of the segment.
- `X2`: x of its end.
- `Y2`: y of its end.

### `polygon-region` (function)

```lisp
(polygon-region coordinates)
```

Return the region that a polygon makes: its loop going counter-clockwise, whichever way the polygon was given.

- `COORDINATES`: a flat array of vertices.

### `polyline-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(polyline-bounds coordinates)
```

Return the bounds of all vertices of a flat x/y coordinate array.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float with at least one vertex.

### `polyline-length` (function)

```lisp
(polyline-length coordinates closed)
```

Return the total length of a polyline, including the closing segment when CLOSED.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...
- `CLOSED`: when true, add the segment from the last vertex back to the first.

### `polyline-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(polyline-touches-bounds-p coordinates closed bounds)
```

Return true when a polyline has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge.

- `COORDINATES`: vector of alternating x and y values, at least one vertex.
- `CLOSED`: true when the last vertex joins the first.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `polyline-vertex-count` (function)

```lisp
(polyline-vertex-count coordinates)
```

Return the number of vertices in a flat x/y coordinate array.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...

### `rectangle-area` (function)

```lisp
(rectangle-area width height)
```

Return the area of a rectangle.

- `WIDTH`: extent along x.
- `HEIGHT`: extent along y.

### `rectangle-perimeter` (function)

```lisp
(rectangle-perimeter width height)
```

Return the perimeter of a rectangle.

- `WIDTH`: extent along x.
- `HEIGHT`: extent along y.

### `region-area` (function)

```lisp
(region-area loops)
```

Return the area of a region: its outer boundaries minus its holes.

- `LOOPS`: a list of flat arrays of vertices, with the inside on their left.

### `region-boolean` (function)

```lisp
(region-boolean operation a b)
```

Return the region that results from joining two regions, from what they have in common, or from taking the second
from the first, as a list of loops: the outer boundaries go counter-clockwise and the holes clockwise. The result is
empty, NIL, when nothing is left.

- `OPERATION`: :union, :intersection or :difference.
- `A`: the first region, a list of loops with the inside on their left.
- `B`: the second region.

### `region-triangles` (function)

```lisp
(region-triangles coordinates holes)
```

Return the triangles that cover a polygon without its holes, as a simple array of double-float holding the six
coordinates of each, by cutting the region into slabs at the height of every vertex and each slab, between the edges
that cross it, into trapezoids of two triangles. It does not matter how the holes lie in the polygon.

- `COORDINATES`: the polygon, a simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...
- `HOLES`: a list of polygons, each such an array.

### `region-winding-number` (function)

```lisp
(region-winding-number x y loops)
```

Return how many times the loops of a region go around a point, counter-clockwise ones counting plus one: the point is
inside the region when this is not zero.

- `X`: x of the point.
- `Y`: y of the point.
- `LOOPS`: a list of flat arrays of vertices.

### `regular-polygon-area` (function)

```lisp
(regular-polygon-area radius sides)
```

Return the area of a regular polygon.

- `RADIUS`: the radius of the circle through the vertices.
- `SIDES`: number of sides.

### `regular-polygon-side-length` (function)

```lisp
(regular-polygon-side-length radius sides)
```

Return the length of a side of a regular polygon.

- `RADIUS`: the radius of the circle through the vertices.
- `SIDES`: number of sides.

### `regular-polygon-vertices` (function)

```lisp
(regular-polygon-vertices center-x center-y radius sides rotation)
```

Return the vertices of a regular polygon, the first at the angle ROTATION, as a vector of
alternating x and y values.

- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius of the circle through the vertices.
- `SIDES`: number of sides, at least 3.
- `ROTATION`: direction of the first vertex, in radians.

### `segment-angle` (function)

```lisp
(segment-angle x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the direction of the segment in [0, 2 pi), counter-clockwise from +x.

- `X1`: x of the start.
- `Y1`: y of the start.
- `X2`: x of the end.
- `Y2`: y of the end.

### `segment-circle-intersections` (function)

```lisp
(segment-circle-intersections x1 y1 x2 y2 center-x center-y radius)
```

Return the points where a segment crosses a circle, as a list of (x . y) conses: none, one when it touches or
enters and stops inside, or two.

- `X1`: x of the start of the segment.
- `Y1`: y of the start of the segment.
- `X2`: x of its end.
- `Y2`: y of its end.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center of the circle.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: its radius.

### `segment-length` (function)

```lisp
(segment-length x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Return the length of the segment between two points.

- `X1`: x of the start.
- `Y1`: y of the start.
- `X2`: x of the end.
- `Y2`: y of the end.

### `segment-segment-intersection` (function)

```lisp
(segment-segment-intersection x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 x4 y4)
```

Return, as two values, the point where the segment (X1, Y1)-(X2, Y2) crosses the segment (X3, Y3)-(X4, Y4),
or NIL when they do not cross: parallel ones, and ones that end before they meet, do not.

- `X1`: x of the start of the first segment.
- `Y1`: y of the start of the first segment.
- `X2`: x of its end.
- `Y2`: y of its end.
- `X3`: x of the start of the second segment.
- `Y3`: y of the start of the second segment.
- `X4`: x of its end.
- `Y4`: y of its end.

### `segment-touches-bounds-p` (function)

```lisp
(segment-touches-bounds-p x1 y1 x2 y2 bounds)
```

Return true when a segment has a point inside BOUNDS or on its edge.

- `X1`: x of the segment start.
- `Y1`: y of the segment start.
- `X2`: x of the segment end.
- `Y2`: y of the segment end.
- `BOUNDS`: the window.

### `snap-coordinate` (function)

```lisp
(snap-coordinate value spacing)
```

Return the multiple of SPACING nearest to VALUE (ties go toward +infinity).

- `VALUE`: the coordinate to snap.
- `SPACING`: grid spacing, a positive length.

### `text-width` (function)

```lisp
(text-width character-count height)
```

Return the width of a text line in world units.

- `CHARACTER-COUNT`: number of characters (one cell per code point).
- `HEIGHT`: text height (em size) in world units.

### `triangulate-polygon` (function)

```lisp
(triangulate-polygon coordinates)
```

Return the triangles that cover a simple polygon, as a simple array of double-float holding the six coordinates of
each, by cutting off one ear after another. A corner that is straight is dropped without a triangle, and a polygon
that has no ear to cut, which only a polygon that crosses itself can be, is finished as a fan.

- `COORDINATES`: simple-array of double-float holding x0 y0 x1 y1 ...

## BORBA-CAD/I18N

The languages of the interface. The English text of the program is the key of every
message, and a catalog gives the translation of a key; English (US) is the default and needs no
catalog, and a text with no translation shows in English.

### `*language*` (variable)

The language the interface speaks now, a keyword such as :en-us or :pt-br.

### `+default-language+` (constant)

The language of the program when nothing else was chosen: English as written in the United States.

### `catalog-language-p` (function)

```lisp
(catalog-language-p language)
```

Return true when there is a catalog for LANGUAGE.

- `LANGUAGE`: a language keyword.

### `catalog-translations` (function)

```lisp
(catalog-translations language)
```

Return the translations of LANGUAGE as a list of (english translation) lists, or NIL for the
default language.

- `LANGUAGE`: a language keyword.

### `format-directives` (function)

```lisp
(format-directives control)
```

Return the directives of a format control as a list of strings, in order and in lower case, so
that a translation can be checked to ask for the same values as the English text. A doubled tilde
is a tilde, not a directive.

- `CONTROL`: a format control string.

### `language-name` (function)

```lisp
(language-name language)
```

Return the name of LANGUAGE in that language, as the language menu shows it.

- `LANGUAGE`: a language keyword.

### `language-tag` (function)

```lisp
(language-tag language)
```

Return the tag of LANGUAGE as people write it, for example "pt-BR".

- `LANGUAGE`: a language keyword.

### `mark` (function)

```lisp
(mark text)
```

Return TEXT unchanged. It marks a text that sits in a table as one to be translated, so that
the catalog check finds it; where the text is shown it goes through TR.

- `TEXT`: the English text.

### `parse-language` (function)

```lisp
(parse-language text)
```

Return the language keyword that TEXT names, or NIL when the program does not speak it. The
tag is accepted in any case with a hyphen or an underscore (pt-BR, pt_br), and the bare language
(en, pt) stands for the one the program has.

- `TEXT`: a language tag typed by the user.

### `supported-languages` (function)

```lisp
(supported-languages)
```

Return the languages the program speaks, as keywords, the default one first.

### `tr` (function)

```lisp
(tr text)
```

Return TEXT in the current language: its translation, or TEXT itself when the language is the
default or the catalog has none.

- `TEXT`: the English text.

### `trf` (function)

```lisp
(trf control &rest arguments)
```

Return CONTROL, translated, filled in with ARGUMENTS as FORMAT does.

- `CONTROL`: the English format control.
- `ARGUMENTS`: the values for its directives.

### `with-language` (macro)

```lisp
(with-language (language) &body body)
```

Run BODY speaking LANGUAGE.

- `LANGUAGE`: a language keyword.
- `BODY`: the forms to run.

## BORBA-CAD/IMAGE

Making picture files from pixels: a JPEG encoder in plain Lisp, so that the program needs no
library to export what the canvas shows.

### `+default-jpeg-quality+` (constant)

The quality a picture is encoded with when none is asked for, from 1 to 100.

### `encode-jpeg` (function)

```lisp
(encode-jpeg width height rgb &key (quality +default-jpeg-quality+))
```

Return the bytes of a JPEG file of a picture.

- `WIDTH`: width in pixels, from 1 to 65535.
- `HEIGHT`: height in pixels, from 1 to 65535.
- `RGB`: the pixels, row by row from the top, a byte each of red, green and blue.
- `QUALITY`: from 1 (smallest file) to 100 (best picture).

## BORBA-CAD/OBSERVABILITY

Structured logging with rotation, operation timing, interval aggregation,
GC and memory sampling, the live performance snapshot and the log summarizer.

### `+gc-pause-warning-threshold-microseconds+` (constant)

A collection pausing at least this long (5 ms, a third of a 60 Hz frame) is logged at
info level; shorter ones at debug level.

### `call-with-operation` (function)

```lisp
(call-with-operation telemetry event fields thunk &key (level info))
```

Run THUNK, measure it with the monotonic clock, record it in the metrics and write one
log entry with its duration and outcome (success, error with the condition, or aborted by a
non-local exit). Errors propagate unchanged. A NIL TELEMETRY just runs THUNK.

- `TELEMETRY`: the telemetry, or NIL.
- `EVENT`: dotted event name.
- `FIELDS`: alternating keys and values describing the workload, for example :entity-count 1500.
- `THUNK`: function of no arguments doing the work; its values are returned.
- `LEVEL`: severity of the entry on success.

### `close-logger` (function)

```lisp
(close-logger logger)
```

Flush and close the log file.

- `LOGGER`: the logger.

### `close-telemetry` (function)

```lisp
(close-telemetry telemetry)
```

Write the final summaries, stop recording GC events and close the log.

- `TELEMETRY`: the telemetry.

### `discover-log-files` (function)

```lisp
(discover-log-files directory &key (file-name *log-file-name*))
```

Return the log files found in DIRECTORY, oldest first: the highest rotation number first,
the current file last.

- `DIRECTORY`: directory to look in.
- `FILE-NAME`: name of the current log file.

### `event-summary` (type)

Statistics of the duration_ms values of one event.

- `EVENT`: event name.
- `COUNT`: number of entries.
- `TOTAL-MS`: sum of durations.
- `MEAN-MS`: mean duration.
- `P50-MS`: median.
- `P95-MS`: 95th percentile.
- `MAX-MS`: longest duration.
- `SAMPLE-RATE`: N when the event was logged one in N, else 1.

### `logger` (type)

A rotating log file sink.

- `DIRECTORY`: directory holding the log files.
- `LEVEL`: least severe level written.
- `MAX-BYTES`: size at which the file is rotated.
- `MAX-FILES`: number of files kept, including the current one.
- `TO-STDERR`: also echo each line to standard error.
- `SAMPLING`: alist of event name and N; one in N entries of that event is written.
- `STREAM`: the open file stream, or NIL when file logging is unavailable.
- `PATH`: pathname of the current log file.
- `COUNTERS`: per-event entry counters used for sampling.
- `FAILURE`: description of why file logging stopped, or NIL.


Create the telemetry and start recording GC events.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.
- `LOGGER`: the log sink; defaults to a logger opened from CONFIG.
- `NOW`: current monotonic time, injectable for tests.

### `metrics` (type)

The live figures behind the performance overlay.

- `OPERATIONS`: event name to OPERATION-STATS.
- `RECENT-EVENTS`: event names, most recently recorded first.
- `ENTITY-COUNT`: entities in the drawing.
- `VISIBLE-ENTITY-COUNT`: entities drawn in the last frame.
- `GC-COUNT`: collections observed.
- `GC-TOTAL-PAUSE-MICROSECONDS`: sum of pause times.
- `GC-LAST-PAUSE-MICROSECONDS`: latest pause.
- `GC-MAX-PAUSE-MICROSECONDS`: longest pause.
- `HEAP-BYTES`: Lisp heap in use at the last sample.
- `HEAP-CAPACITY-BYTES`: Lisp heap size.
- `RSS-BYTES`: resident set size at the last sample, or NIL.
- `LAST-FRAME-SUMMARY`: property list describing the last completed frame interval, or NIL.

```lisp
(percentile sorted-values fraction)
```

- `TELEMETRY`: the telemetry, or NIL.
- `EVENT`: dotted event name.
- `DURATION-NANOSECONDS`: how long the operation took.
- `LEVEL`: severity of the entry.
- `FIELDS`: alternating keys and values, for example :bytes 1200 :result :success.

### `telemetry` (type)

The application's observability hub: logger, live metrics and the interval aggregators.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.
- `LOGGER`: the rotating log sink.
- `METRICS`: live figures for the overlay.
- `SUMMARY-INTERVAL-NANOSECONDS`: period of the aggregated summary entries.
- `MEMORY-INTERVAL-NANOSECONDS`: period of memory samples.
- `FRAME-CPU`: per-frame CPU time samples (ms) of the current interval.
- `FRAME-SWAP`: per-frame buffer-swap time samples (ms).

- `INPUT-SAMPLES`: event type to handling-time samples (ms).
- `VIEWPORT-SAMPLES`: viewport update time samples (ms).
- `LAST-PRESENT-NANOSECONDS`: monotonic time of the previous presented frame.
- `NEXT-SUMMARY-NANOSECONDS`: monotonic time of the next summary flush.
- `NEXT-MEMORY-NANOSECONDS`: monotonic time of the next memory sample.

### `telemetry-record-frame` (function)

```lisp
(telemetry-record-frame telemetry cpu-nanoseconds swap-nanoseconds present-nanoseconds)
```

Record one presented frame.


### `telemetry-tick` (function)

```lisp
(telemetry-tick telemetry &optional (now (monotonic-nanoseconds)))
```

Do the periodic work: drain GC events, write the summaries and memory samples that are due.
Call it once per main-loop iteration; it is cheap when nothing is due.

- `TELEMETRY`: the telemetry.
- `NOW`: current monotonic time, injectable for tests.

### `with-operation` (macro)

```lisp
(with-operation (telemetry event &key fields (level info)) &body body)
```

Time BODY as the operation EVENT; see CALL-WITH-OPERATION. A macro because this wrapper
appears around every measured operation.

- `TELEMETRY`: expression giving the telemetry, or NIL.
- `EVENT`: dotted event name.
- `FIELDS`: expression giving the alternating keys and values logged with the entry.
- `LEVEL`: severity of the success entry.
- `BODY`: forms to run.

## BORBA-CAD/PERSISTENCE

The .bcad drawing format: a small safe S-expression reader, a writer whose
numbers round-trip exactly, strict validation, and atomic saving.

### `*drawing-extension*` (variable)

Extension that a drawing file name gets when it does not already end in one that the program
writes.

### `*known-drawing-extensions*` (variable)

Extensions of the file formats that the program writes. A name that ends in one of them is
saved as typed; any other name, including one with a different extension such as plan.txt or
v1.2 final, gets .bcad after it.

### `+maximum-file-bytes+` (constant)

Largest drawing file that will be read. A 100,000-entity drawing is about 12 MB; the limit
bounds the memory used to read an untrusted file (NFR-7).

### `+maximum-nesting-depth+` (constant)

Deepest list nesting accepted. The format needs 4; the limit protects the stack.

### `+maximum-token-length+` (constant)

Longest word or number accepted, in characters. Doubles print in at most 24.

### `+oldest-supported-format-version+` (constant)

The oldest format version this program still reads, migrating it as it is read.

### `+recent-file-limit+` (constant)

Most drawings the recent list remembers.

### `+supported-format-version+` (constant)

The newest format version: the one this program writes, and the newest it reads.

### `check-open-source` (function)

```lisp
(check-open-source file)
```

Signal DRAWING-FILE-IO-ERROR, with a reason a person can act on, when FILE cannot be opened as
a drawing: it does not exist, is a folder or another kind of file, or may not be read.

- `FILE`: the pathname to open.

### `clean-path-text` (function)

```lisp
(clean-path-text text)
```

Return the path that TEXT means: blanks around it and one pair of quotes around it are
removed, and a file:// address becomes the path it names. Signals INVALID-INPUT when nothing is
left.

- `TEXT`: what the user typed, pasted or dropped.

### `default-drawings-directory` (function)

```lisp
(default-drawings-directory)
```

Return the folder where a drawing is offered to be saved or opened when nothing else is known:
the user's documents folder, in whatever language the desktop calls it, or the home folder.
The folder is the first that exists among the XDG documents directory, Documents and Documentos.

### `directory-of` (function)

```lisp
(directory-of path)
```

Return the folder that contains the file PATH, as a directory pathname.

- `PATH`: a pathname.

### `drawing-file-path` (function)

```lisp
(drawing-file-path text)
```

Return the pathname of the drawing file that TEXT names. A name that does not end in an
extension that the program writes gets .bcad after it (plan becomes plan.bcad and v1.2 final
becomes v1.2 final.bcad); a trailing dot is dropped first. A path that names a folder is returned
as it is, for the caller to refuse.

- `TEXT`: a file name as typed by the user.

### `drawing-to-string` (function)

```lisp
(drawing-to-string drawing)
```

Return DRAWING serialized as a string.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.

### `file-path-with-extension` (function)

```lisp
(file-path-with-extension text extension known-extensions)
```

Return the pathname of the file that TEXT names. A name that does not end in one of
KNOWN-EXTENSIONS gets EXTENSION after it (plan becomes plan.bcad and v1.2 final becomes
v1.2 final.bcad); a trailing dot is dropped first. A path that names a folder is returned as it is,
for the caller to refuse.

- `TEXT`: a file name as typed by the user.
- `EXTENSION`: the extension to add, without the dot.
- `KNOWN-EXTENSIONS`: the extensions that need nothing added.

### `format-double` (function)

```lisp
(format-double value)
```

Return the shortest decimal text that reads back as exactly VALUE, bit for bit (including
negative zero). Never uses a reader macro or exponent marker other than e.

- `VALUE`: a finite double-float.

### `load-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(load-drawing path)
```

Read and validate the drawing file at PATH. Returns two values: the drawing and a property
list with :bytes, :read-nanoseconds and :parse-nanoseconds (parsing includes validation and
building). Signals a DRAWING-FILE-ERROR subclass on any problem; no partial result is returned.

- `PATH`: pathname of the drawing file.

### `load-recent-files` (function)

```lisp
(load-recent-files state-directory)
```

Return the recently used drawings as pathnames, the latest first. The list is empty when
there is none, when it cannot be read, or when STATE-DIRECTORY is NIL.

- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: the folder where the program keeps what it remembers, or NIL.

### `load-setting` (function)

```lisp
(load-setting state-directory name)
```

Return the saved value of the setting NAME as a string, or NIL when it was never saved.

- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: the folder where the program keeps what it remembers, or NIL.
- `NAME`: the name of the setting.

### `load-settings` (function)

```lisp
(load-settings state-directory)
```

Return the saved settings as a list of (name . value) conses of strings, in file order. The
list is empty when there is none, when the file cannot be read, or when STATE-DIRECTORY is NIL.

- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: the folder where the program keeps what it remembers, or NIL.

### `native-path` (function)

```lisp
(native-path text)
```

Return the pathname that the user-typed TEXT names. Blanks, quotes and file:// addresses are
dealt with (see CLEAN-PATH-TEXT), characters such as * and [ are taken literally, a leading ~
means the home directory, and relative names are resolved against the current directory, so the
result is always absolute.

- `TEXT`: a file name as typed by the user.

### `parse-drawing-text` (function)

```lisp
(parse-drawing-text text &key path)
```

Parse and validate drawing text; returns a new drawing. Signals a DRAWING-FILE-ERROR
subclass naming the line and column of the first problem. Nothing is evaluated.

- `TEXT`: the file contents.
- `PATH`: file name used in error messages, or NIL.

### `read-file-octets` (function)

```lisp
(read-file-octets file)
```

Read a whole file into a vector of bytes, enforcing the size limit.

- `FILE`: pathname of an existing file.

### `read-file-text` (function)

```lisp
(read-file-text file)
```

Read a whole UTF-8 text file into a string, enforcing the size limit.

- `FILE`: pathname of an existing file.

### `remember-recent-file` (function)

```lisp
(remember-recent-file state-directory path)
```

Put the drawing PATH at the head of the recent list, dropping its older mention and the
entries beyond the limit. Returns true when the list was written, and NIL when it could not be
(or STATE-DIRECTORY is NIL).

- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: the folder where the program keeps what it remembers, or NIL.
- `PATH`: the drawing's pathname.

### `save-binary-file` (function)

```lisp
(save-binary-file path octets &key before-commit)
```

Write a file of bytes atomically, as SAVE-TEXT-FILE writes a text one, and return what it returns.

- `PATH`: destination pathname.
- `OCTETS`: the bytes of the file.
- `BEFORE-COMMIT`: optional function called with the temporary pathname just before the rename.

### `save-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(save-drawing drawing path &key before-commit)
```

Save DRAWING atomically in the .bcad format: see SAVE-TEXT-FILE for how, and for what is
returned. Signals DRAWING-FILE-IO-ERROR on failure.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing to save.
- `PATH`: destination pathname.
- `BEFORE-COMMIT`: optional function called with the temporary pathname just before the rename;
a test seam for injecting failures.

### `save-setting` (function)

```lisp
(save-setting state-directory name value)
```

Save the setting NAME with VALUE, keeping the other settings. Returns true when the file was
written, and NIL when it could not be (or STATE-DIRECTORY is NIL, or the text has a line break).

- `STATE-DIRECTORY`: the folder where the program keeps what it remembers, or NIL.
- `NAME`: the name of the setting, with no `=` in it.
- `VALUE`: its value.

### `save-text-file` (function)

```lisp
(save-text-file path writer &key before-commit (external-format utf-8) (element-type 'character))
```

Write a file atomically, a text file unless ELEMENT-TYPE says bytes: write a sibling temporary file, flush
it to disk, then rename it over PATH. A failure at any point leaves any existing file at PATH untouched and removes the
temporary file. When PATH is a symbolic link the file it points to is replaced, not the link,
and a replaced file keeps its permissions. Returns two values: the saved pathname and a
property list with :bytes, :serialize-nanoseconds (including the disk flush) and
:rename-nanoseconds. Signals DRAWING-FILE-IO-ERROR on failure.

- `PATH`: destination pathname.
- `WRITER`: function of the output stream that writes the contents.
- `BEFORE-COMMIT`: optional function called with the temporary pathname just before the rename;
a test seam for injecting failures.
- `EXTERNAL-FORMAT`: the encoding of the file.
- `ELEMENT-TYPE`: CHARACTER for a text file, or (UNSIGNED-BYTE 8) for one of bytes.

### `user-directories-entry` (function)

```lisp
(user-directories-entry key)
```

Return the folder that the user's XDG directories file assigns to KEY, or NIL. It is how a
Brazilian Portuguese desktop learns that the documents folder is called Documentos.

- `KEY`: the variable name, for example "XDG_DOCUMENTS_DIR".

### `write-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(write-drawing drawing stream)
```

Write DRAWING in the .bcad format (the newest version).

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `STREAM`: character output stream; UTF-8 is expected.

## BORBA-CAD/PLATFORM

The only package that touches SDL, OpenGL and FreeType: window and event loop,
glyph atlas, the OpenGL renderer of the draw list, event translation and the entry point.

### `display-available-p` (function)

```lisp
(display-available-p &key (windows-p nil))
```

Return true when a graphical session is available: always on Windows, whose windows need no variable of the
environment, and on the other systems when Wayland or X11 is there.

- `WINDOWS-P`: true when the program runs on Windows.

### `executable-main` (function)

```lisp
(executable-main)
```

Entry point of the saved executable: MAIN, with the debugger disabled unless
BORBA_CAD_DEBUG=1, so that an unexpected condition ends the process instead of waiting for a
terminal that may not exist.

### `find-font-path` (function)

```lisp
(find-font-path config)
```

Return the path of the monospace font to use: the configured one, or the first usual
location that exists. Signals CONFIGURATION-ERROR when there is none.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.

### `find-font-paths` (function)

```lisp
(find-font-paths config)
```

Return the font file of every role as a property list (:mono path :ui path :ui-strong path).
The interface fonts fall back to the monospace font, and the strong one to the regular one, so
text is always drawn, only less handsomely. Signals CONFIGURATION-ERROR when no monospace font
exists.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.

### `frame-benchmark-result` (type)

Frame timings measured with the real window and GPU.

- `FRAMES`: frames measured.
- `CPU-MS`: sample summary of CPU time per frame (build, submit), in milliseconds.
- `GPU-WAIT-MS`: sample summary of the time glFinish waited for the GPU after the submit, which is
the GPU work that the CPU work did not already cover.
- `SWAP-MS`: sample summary of the buffer swap (includes waiting for vertical sync).
- `INTERVAL-MS`: sample summary of the time between presented frames.
- `GL-RENDERER`: the OpenGL renderer string reported by the driver.
- `SWAP-INTERVAL`: the swap interval in effect, as reported by SDL after it was requested.

### `frame-benchmark-result-cpu-ms` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-cpu-ms instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-frames` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-frames instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-gl-renderer` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-gl-renderer instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-gpu-wait-ms` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-gpu-wait-ms instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-interval-ms` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-interval-ms instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-swap-interval` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-swap-interval instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `frame-benchmark-result-swap-ms` (function)

```lisp
(frame-benchmark-result-swap-ms instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `glyph-raster-size` (function)

```lisp
(glyph-raster-size physical-size)
```

Return the pixel size at which glyphs for text of PHYSICAL-SIZE pixels are rasterized:
exact integers up to 32, then a ladder of sizes growing by 25 percent, capped at 256.

- `PHYSICAL-SIZE`: the on-screen em size in framebuffer pixels.

### `install-system-clipboard` (function)

```lisp
(install-system-clipboard app)
```

Make the application use the system clipboard.

- `APP`: the application.

### `keycode->key` (function)

```lisp
(keycode->key keycode)
```

Return the key keyword for an SDL keycode, or NIL for keys the application ignores.

- `KEYCODE`: an SDL keycode (layout-aware, so shortcuts follow the printed letters).

### `main` (function)

```lisp
(main &key installed)
```

Program entry point: parse the command line and environment, then run the application.
Exit status: 0 on a normal exit, 2 for a configuration problem, 1 for an unexpected error.

- `INSTALLED`: true when running as the saved executable, whose folder holds its examples and fonts
and whose log goes to the user's state directory.

### `parse-platform-options` (function)

```lisp
(parse-platform-options arguments)
```

Interpret the arguments left over by the core option parser. Signals CONFIGURATION-ERROR for
anything unknown.

- `ARGUMENTS`: list of argument strings.

### `platform-options-demo` (function)

```lisp
(platform-options-demo instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `platform-options-exit-after-demo` (function)

```lisp
(platform-options-exit-after-demo instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `platform-options-file` (function)

```lisp
(platform-options-file instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `platform-options-help` (function)

```lisp
(platform-options-help instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `platform-options-screenshot` (function)

```lisp
(platform-options-screenshot instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `run-application` (function)

```lisp
(run-application config &key demo exit-after-demo screenshot)
```

Open the window and run until the user quits. Everything measured is logged.

- `CONFIG`: the application configuration.
- `DEMO`: when true, run the scripted demonstration drawing.
- `EXIT-AFTER-DEMO`: when true, quit when the demonstration has finished.
- `SCREENSHOT`: file name to save the last demonstration frame to, or NIL.

### `run-frame-benchmark` (function)

```lisp
(run-frame-benchmark drawing &key (frames 600) (warmup 30) (width 1440) (height 900) (swap-interval 1))
```

Render FRAMES frames of DRAWING with the shared benchmark input (a zoom oscillating about
the canvas center and a moving pointer), and return the measured CPU, swap and interval
times. Needs a display.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing to show.
- `FRAMES`: frames to measure, after the warm-up.
- `WARMUP`: frames rendered first and not measured.
- `WIDTH`: window width in logical pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: window height in logical pixels.
- `SWAP-INTERVAL`: 1 to wait for the display refresh at each swap, 0 to swap at once.

### `sdl-clipboard-text` (function)

```lisp
(sdl-clipboard-text)
```

Return the text on the system clipboard, or NIL when there is none or it is empty. SDL gives
the text in memory of its own, which is freed here.

### `set-sdl-clipboard-text` (function)

```lisp
(set-sdl-clipboard-text text)
```

Put TEXT on the system clipboard. Returns true when SDL accepted it.

- `TEXT`: the string to copy.

### `translate-sdl-event` (function)

```lisp
(translate-sdl-event event window)
```

Translate one SDL event into a list of application events (possibly empty).

- `EVENT`: an SDL event record.
- `WINDOW`: the window, used to read the size on resize events.

### `write-rgb-png` (function)

```lisp
(write-rgb-png path width height pixels &key (flip-vertical t))
```

Write an RGB image as a PNG file.

- `PATH`: output file name.
- `WIDTH`: image width in pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: image height in pixels.
- `PIXELS`: octet vector of WIDTH x HEIGHT x 3 values, one row after another.
- `FLIP-VERTICAL`: true when the rows run bottom to top, as OpenGL returns them.

## BORBA-CAD/PLOT

Plotting a drawing on a sheet of paper, to scale: the sheet sizes, the scale and where the drawing
goes on the sheet, the entities flattened into strokes, filled triangles and text in sheet coordinates, and two
writers of that, one of a PDF file made by hand and one of an SVG file.

### `*paper-sizes*` (variable)

The sheets, as (keyword name width height) with the width and the height in millimetres, the sheet standing up.

### `+default-viewport-inset+` (constant)

How far inside the area for viewports a viewport that is made to fill it is kept from its edges, in millimetres.

### `+frame-margin+` (constant)

How far the frame of a sheet is from the edge of the paper, in millimetres.

### `+points-per-millimeter+` (constant)

How many PDF points, 1/72 of an inch, there are in a millimetre.

### `+title-block-height+` (constant)

How tall the title block along the bottom of the frame is, in millimetres.

### `clip-fill` (function)

```lisp
(clip-fill fill bounds)
```

Return the filled polygon that lies inside a rectangle, or NIL when none of it does.

- `FILL`: a plot fill.
- `BOUNDS`: the rectangle.

### `clip-item` (function)

```lisp
(clip-item item bounds)
```

Return what lies inside a rectangle of an item of a plot, as a list.

- `ITEM`: a plot stroke, fill or text.
- `BOUNDS`: the rectangle, as bounds in sheet millimetres.

### `clip-stroke` (function)

```lisp
(clip-stroke stroke bounds)
```

Return the strokes that are what lies of a stroke inside a rectangle: the stroke itself when all of it does, and
otherwise the runs of it that do, each its own stroke, which are none when none of it does.

- `STROKE`: a plot stroke.
- `BOUNDS`: the rectangle, as bounds in sheet millimetres.

### `default-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(default-viewport sheet center-x center-y scale)
```

Return a viewport that fills the area of a sheet for viewports, to a margin, showing a point of the drawing at its
center at a scale.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the point of the drawing at the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of that point.
- `SCALE`: millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing.

### `entities-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(entities-bounds entities)
```

Return the bounds that hold every entity.

- `ENTITIES`: a non-empty list of entities.

### `fit-ratio` (function)

```lisp
(fit-ratio bounds width height margin)
```

Return the scale at which the drawing just fills the sheet inside the margins.

- `BOUNDS`: the bounds of the drawing.
- `WIDTH`: width of the sheet.
- `HEIGHT`: height of the sheet.
- `MARGIN`: the empty edge.

### `fitted-text` (function)

```lisp
(fitted-text string width height)
```

Return a text cut to fit a width in the plot font, with three dots at the end when something was cut.

- `STRING`: the text.
- `WIDTH`: the room, in millimetres.
- `HEIGHT`: the height of the text.

### `make-plot-fill` (function)

```lisp
(make-plot-fill points color)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-plot-settings` (function)

```lisp
(make-plot-settings &key (paper a4) ratio (monochrome t) (line-width +default-line-width+) (margin +default-margin+))
```

Create validated plot settings. Signals INVALID-INPUT when one makes no sense.

- `PAPER`: a keyword of *PAPER-SIZES*.
- `RATIO`: millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing, above zero, or NIL to fit the drawing to the sheet.
- `MONOCHROME`: true to draw everything in black.
- `LINE-WIDTH`: thickness of lines in millimetres, above zero.
- `MARGIN`: empty edge of the sheet in millimetres, zero or more and less than half of the sheet.

### `make-plot-stroke` (function)

```lisp
(make-plot-stroke points closed color &optional width)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-plot-text` (function)

```lisp
(make-plot-text string x y height angle color)
```

(undocumented)

### `paper-name` (function)

```lisp
(paper-name paper)
```

Return the name of a sheet, such as "A4".

- `PAPER`: a keyword of *PAPER-SIZES*.

### `paper-size` (function)

```lisp
(paper-size paper)
```

Return the width and the height of a sheet standing up, in millimetres, as two values.

- `PAPER`: a keyword of *PAPER-SIZES*.

### `parse-plot-scale` (function)

```lisp
(parse-plot-scale text)
```

Return the scale that TEXT names: NIL for "fit", or the millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing.
"1:50" and "1/50" and a plain "50" are a fiftieth, "2:1" is twice life size. Signals INVALID-INPUT when
TEXT names none.

- `TEXT`: what was typed.

### `plan-plot` (function)

```lisp
(plan-plot drawing settings)
```

Work out where the drawing goes on the sheet and flatten what is visible into plot items. Signals EXPORT-REFUSED
when nothing is visible.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.

### `plan-sheet` (function)

```lisp
(plan-sheet drawing sheet index count settings &optional (entities (visible-entities drawing)))
```

Work out what is printed on a sheet: the drawing through each of its viewports, then the frame and the title block.
Returns a plot plan in the millimetres of the sheet.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SHEET`: the sheet.
- `INDEX`: the position of the sheet, from 0.
- `COUNT`: how many sheets there are.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings, of which the colors and the thickness of lines are used.
- `ENTITIES`: the entities that are on layers that are not hidden.

### `plan-sheets` (function)

```lisp
(plan-sheets drawing settings)
```

Work out every sheet of the drawing, as a list of plot plans in the order of the sheets. Signals EXPORT-REFUSED
when the drawing has no sheets.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.

### `plans-pdf` (function)

```lisp
(plans-pdf plans)
```

Return the plot plans as the bytes of a PDF file, a page for each, and how many characters of text had to be written
as a question mark because the PDF font cannot show them.

- `PLANS`: the plot plans, in the order of the pages.

### `plot-fill` (type)

A filled polygon, in practice an arrowhead.

- `POINTS`: the vertices as a list of (x . y) conses in sheet millimetres.
- `COLOR`: a list of red, green and blue, each from 0 to 1.

### `plot-fill-color` (function)

```lisp
(plot-fill-color instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-fill-points` (function)

```lisp
(plot-fill-points instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan` (type)

Where a drawing goes on a sheet and what is drawn there.

- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.
- `WIDTH`: width of the sheet in millimetres, in the orientation chosen.
- `HEIGHT`: height of the sheet in millimetres.
- `RATIO`: the millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing, as worked out for a fit.
- `FITS`: true when the drawing lies within the margins.
- `OFFSET-X`: sheet x of the origin of the drawing.
- `OFFSET-Y`: sheet y of the origin of the drawing.
- `ENTITY-COUNT`: how many entities are drawn.
- `ITEMS`: the strokes, fills and texts, in drawing order, in sheet coordinates.

### `plot-plan-entity-count` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-entity-count instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-fits` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-fits instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-height` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-height instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-items` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-items instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-ratio` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-ratio instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-settings` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-settings instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-plan-width` (function)

```lisp
(plot-plan-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-settings` (type)

How a drawing is put on a sheet.

- `PAPER`: a keyword of *PAPER-SIZES*.
- `RATIO`: the millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing, or NIL to make it as large as the sheet allows.
- `MONOCHROME`: true to draw everything in black, false to use the colors of the layers.
- `LINE-WIDTH`: the thickness of lines, in millimetres of paper.
- `MARGIN`: the empty edge of the sheet, in millimetres.

### `plot-settings-line-width` (function)

```lisp
(plot-settings-line-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-settings-margin` (function)

```lisp
(plot-settings-margin instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-settings-monochrome` (function)

```lisp
(plot-settings-monochrome instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-settings-paper` (function)

```lisp
(plot-settings-paper instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-settings-ratio` (function)

```lisp
(plot-settings-ratio instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-stroke` (type)

A run of segments.

- `POINTS`: the vertices as a list of (x . y) conses in sheet millimetres.
- `CLOSED`: true when the last joins the first.
- `COLOR`: a list of red, green and blue, each from 0 to 1.
- `WIDTH`: the thickness in millimetres, or NIL for the thickness of the plot.

### `plot-stroke-closed` (function)

```lisp
(plot-stroke-closed instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-stroke-color` (function)

```lisp

```

(undocumented)

### `plot-stroke-points` (function)

```lisp
(plot-stroke-points instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-stroke-width` (function)

```lisp
(plot-stroke-width instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-text` (type)

A line of text.

- `STRING`: the characters.
- `X`: sheet x of the start of the baseline.
- `Y`: sheet y of the start of the baseline.
- `HEIGHT`: the size of the characters in millimetres.
- `ANGLE`: direction of the baseline, in radians counter-clockwise from the sheet's x.
- `COLOR`: a list of red, green and blue, each from 0 to 1.

### `plot-text-angle` (function)

```lisp
(plot-text-angle instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `plot-text-color` (function)




### `plot-text-x` (function)


### `plot-text-y` (function)
