
(undocumented)

### `render-pdf` (function)

```lisp
(render-pdf drawing settings)
```

Return the drawing as the bytes of a PDF file, and the plot plan that was used, and how many characters of text had
to be written as a question mark because the PDF font cannot show them. Signals EXPORT-REFUSED when nothing is visible.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.

### `render-sheets-pdf` (function)

```lisp
(render-sheets-pdf drawing settings)
```

Return the sheets of the drawing as the bytes of a PDF file with a page for each, the plot plans that were used, and
how many characters of text had to be written as a question mark because the PDF font cannot show them. Signals
EXPORT-REFUSED when the drawing has no sheets.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings, of which the colors and the thickness of lines are used.

### `render-svg` (function)

```lisp
(render-svg drawing settings)
```

Return the drawing as the text of an SVG file, and the plot plan that was used. Signals EXPORT-REFUSED when nothing
is visible.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.

### `scale-text` (function)

```lisp
(scale-text ratio)
```

Return a scale as a ratio, such as 1:50, or 2:1 when it enlarges.

- `RATIO`: millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing.

### `sheet-body` (function)

```lisp
(sheet-body sheet)
```

Return the rectangle inside the frame and above the title block, where the viewports go.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.

### `sheet-frame` (function)

```lisp
(sheet-frame sheet)
```

Return the rectangle inside the margin of a sheet, where its frame is drawn.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.

### `sheet-size` (function)

```lisp
(sheet-size sheet)
```

Return the width and the height of a sheet as it lies on the paper, in millimetres, as two values.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.

### `title-block-items` (function)

```lisp
(title-block-items sheet index count)
```

Return the strokes and texts that draw the frame and the title block of a sheet.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.
- `INDEX`: the position of the sheet, from 0.
- `COUNT`: how many sheets there are.

### `title-cells` (function)

```lisp
(title-cells sheet index count)
```

Return the cells of the title block of a sheet, each as a list of x, y, width and height on the sheet, the small
name, what it says and the height of that: the cells of the project and the title of the sheet, and six small ones.

- `SHEET`: the sheet.
- `INDEX`: the position of the sheet, from 0.
- `COUNT`: how many sheets there are.

### `viewport-items` (function)

```lisp
(viewport-items drawing entities viewport settings)
```

Return the items that draw a viewport: what of the drawing shows in its window, at its scale and cut to its
rectangle, and its border. Also returns how many entities were drawn.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `ENTITIES`: the entities that are on layers that are not hidden.
- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `SETTINGS`: the plot settings.

### `viewport-rectangle` (function)

```lisp
(viewport-rectangle viewport)
```

Return where a viewport is on its sheet, as bounds.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.

### `visible-entities` (function)

```lisp
(visible-entities drawing)
```

Return the entities of the drawing that are on visible layers, in stacking order.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.

## BORBA-CAD/SBCL-SUPPORT

All SBCL-specific code: clocks, memory and GC sources, environment and file-system primitives.

### `+nanoseconds-per-microsecond+` (constant)

Nanoseconds in one microsecond.

### `+nanoseconds-per-millisecond+` (constant)

Nanoseconds in one millisecond.

### `+nanoseconds-per-second+` (constant)

Nanoseconds in one second.

### `allocated-bytes-total` (function)

```lisp
(allocated-bytes-total)
```

Return the total number of bytes allocated by Lisp code since process start.

### `backtrace-string` (function)

```lisp
(backtrace-string &optional (count 30))
```

Return the current call stack, innermost call first, as text, for the log of an unexpected
error.

- `COUNT`: how many calls to show.

### `call-with-timeout` (function)

```lisp
(call-with-timeout seconds function)
```

Call FUNCTION, and stop it with a TIMEOUT condition if it runs for more than SECONDS. The condition
is signaled in the code that runs, so it unwinds through it: what must not be left half done has to be
in WITH-UNINTERRUPTED. Returns what FUNCTION returns.

- `SECONDS`: the longest time to wait, as a number of seconds.
- `FUNCTION`: function of no arguments.

### `command-line-arguments` (function)

```lisp
(command-line-arguments)
```

Return the application's command-line arguments as a list of strings.

When started through `sbcl ... --end-toplevel-options ARGS` the arguments are those after the
marker; when started as a saved executable they are everything after the program name.

### `cpu-model-string` (function)

```lisp
(cpu-model-string)
```

Return the CPU model name from /proc/cpuinfo, or "unknown".

### `current-utc-timestamp` (function)

```lisp
(current-utc-timestamp)
```

Return the current wall-clock time as an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp with millisecond precision.

### `delete-file-if-exists` (function)

```lisp
(delete-file-if-exists path)
```

Delete the file at PATH if it exists; return true when a file was deleted.

- `PATH`: file to delete.

### `describe-os-error` (function)

```lisp
(describe-os-error condition)
```

Return the operating system's wording of the problem behind CONDITION, for example
"Permission denied" or "No space left on device", or the condition's own one-line text when
it carries no error number.

- `CONDITION`: an error condition.

### `directory-listing` (function)

```lisp
(directory-listing directory)
```

Return the entries of the folder DIRECTORY, in no particular order, as a list of
(name kind size modified), and a second value: NIL, or a sentence that says why the folder
cannot be read. KIND is :directory, :file or :other (devices, sockets); SIZE is the byte count of
a file and NIL otherwise; MODIFIED is a universal time.

- `DIRECTORY`: a pathname.

### `drain-gc-events` (function)

```lisp
(drain-gc-events)
```

Return the GC events recorded since the last call, oldest first, and clear the queue.

### `environment-variable` (function)

```lisp
(environment-variable name)
```

Return the value of the environment variable NAME as a string, or NIL when unset.

- `NAME`: the variable name.

### `file-kind` (function)

```lisp
(file-kind path)
```

Return :file, :directory or :other for what PATH names, following symbolic links, or NIL when
nothing can be found there.

- `PATH`: a pathname.

### `file-permissions` (function)

```lisp
(file-permissions path)
```

Return the permission bits of the file at PATH as an integer, or NIL when it cannot be read.

- `PATH`: a pathname.

### `file-size-bytes` (function)

```lisp
(file-size-bytes path)
```

Return the size in bytes of the file at PATH.

- `PATH`: an existing file.

### `fold-for-search` (function)

```lisp
(fold-for-search string)
```

Return STRING in lower case and without accents, the form in which names are sorted and
searched, so that A-acute plus gua, agua and AGUA are the same and a name that starts with an
accented letter sorts among the plain ones.

- `STRING`: a name.

### `format-utc-timestamp` (function)

```lisp
(format-utc-timestamp unix-seconds microseconds)
```

Format a wall-clock instant as an ISO-8601 UTC timestamp with millisecond precision.

- `UNIX-SECONDS`: seconds since 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z.
- `MICROSECONDS`: microseconds within the second, 0 to 999999.

### `function-lambda-list` (function)

```lisp
(function-lambda-list function)
```

Return the lambda list of a function, as written when it was defined, for a help that shows how a
command is used.

- `FUNCTION`: a function or a macro function.

### `gc-event` (type)

One completed garbage collection.

- `TIMESTAMP-NANOSECONDS`: monotonic time at which the after-GC hook ran.
- `PAUSE-MICROSECONDS`: CPU time SBCL spent in this collection (delta of *GC-RUN-TIME*).

### `gc-event-pause-microseconds` (function)

```lisp
(gc-event-pause-microseconds instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `gc-event-timestamp-nanoseconds` (function)

```lisp
(gc-event-timestamp-nanoseconds instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `gc-total-pause-microseconds` (function)

```lisp
(gc-total-pause-microseconds)
```

Return the total CPU time spent in garbage collection since process start, in microseconds.

### `heap-capacity-bytes` (function)

```lisp
(heap-capacity-bytes)
```

Return the size of the Lisp heap (dynamic space) in bytes.

### `heap-usage-bytes` (function)

```lisp
(heap-usage-bytes)
```

Return the number of bytes currently in use in the Lisp heap (dynamic space).

### `host-name-string` (function)

```lisp
(host-name-string)
```

Return the host name, or "unknown".

### `install-gc-event-hook` (function)

```lisp
(install-gc-event-hook)
```

Start recording GC events; idempotent. Events accumulate until DRAIN-GC-EVENTS is called.

### `kernel-release-string` (function)

```lisp
(kernel-release-string)
```

Return the kernel release (for example "7.2.9-arch1-1"), or "unknown".

### `limited-output-text` (function)

```lisp
(limited-output-text stream)
```

Return what a limited stream kept, and true when it dropped some of what was written to it.

- `STREAM`: a limited output stream.

### `lisp-version-string` (function)

```lisp
(lisp-version-string)
```

Return the Lisp implementation name and version, for example "SBCL 2.6.9".

### `make-directory` (function)

```lisp
(make-directory path)
```

Create the folder PATH, which must not exist yet and whose parent must. Returns NIL on
success, and otherwise a sentence that says why it could not be made.

- `PATH`: a pathname.

### `make-limited-output-stream` (function)

```lisp
(make-limited-output-stream limit)
```

Return an output stream that keeps the first LIMIT characters written to it.

- `LIMIT`: the most characters to keep.

### `monotonic-nanoseconds` (function)

```lisp
(monotonic-nanoseconds)
```

Return a reading of the monotonic clock in nanoseconds.

The origin is arbitrary (system boot), so only differences are meaningful.
CL:GET-INTERNAL-REAL-TIME is not used: it advances in ~1 ms ticks on SBCL 2.6.9.

### `octets->string` (function)

```lisp
(octets->string octets external-format)
```

Decode a vector of bytes as text in an encoding.

- `OCTETS`: the bytes.
- `EXTERNAL-FORMAT`: :utf-8 or :cp1252 (Windows Western European).

### `path-accessible-p` (function)

```lisp
(path-accessible-p path access)
```

Return true when the current user has the given access to PATH.

- `PATH`: a pathname.
- `ACCESS`: :read, :write or :execute.

### `process-id` (function)

```lisp
(process-id)
```

Return the operating-system process id.

### `remove-gc-event-hook` (function)

```lisp
(remove-gc-event-hook)
```

Stop recording GC events and discard any pending ones.

### `rename-over` (function)

```lisp
(rename-over from to)
```

Rename FROM to TO, replacing TO if it exists (same file system). The rename is atomic where the
system offers one (POSIX); on Windows the old file is removed first, so a crash between the two
steps can leave only the temporary file.

- `FROM`: pathname of an existing file.
- `TO`: destination pathname.

### `resident-set-bytes` (function)

```lisp
(resident-set-bytes)
```

Return the resident set size of the process in bytes, or NIL when /proc is unavailable.

### `set-file-permissions` (function)

```lisp
(set-file-permissions path permissions)
```

Give the file at PATH the permission bits PERMISSIONS. Returns true on success and NIL when
the system refuses; callers use it to keep a replaced file's permissions, which is best effort.

- `PATH`: a pathname.
- `PERMISSIONS`: permission bits as returned by FILE-PERMISSIONS.

### `sync-output-stream` (function)

```lisp
(sync-output-stream stream)
```

Flush STREAM and force its data to stable storage (fsync).

- `STREAM`: an open file output stream backed by a file descriptor.

### `timeout` (symbol)

(undocumented)

### `true-path` (function)

```lisp
(true-path path)
```

Return the pathname of the file that PATH names after following symbolic links, or PATH itself
when nothing exists there.

- `PATH`: a pathname.

### `utf-8-octets->string` (function)

```lisp
(utf-8-octets->string octets)
```

Decode UTF-8 octets into a string. Returns NIL when they are not valid UTF-8.

- `OCTETS`: a vector of integers 0 to 255.

### `with-uninterrupted` (macro)

```lisp
(with-uninterrupted &body body)
```

Run BODY so that a timeout cannot unwind it half way: the interruption waits until BODY is done.
For the steps that must finish to leave a data structure whole.

- `BODY`: the forms to run.

## BORBA-CAD/SCENE

The boundary between the headless application and any renderer: a draw list
of screen-space vertices and text runs, a theme, and the builder that turns a drawing, a
viewport and the interaction state into a draw list.

### `+circle-chordal-error-pixels+` (constant)

Largest distance, in pixels, between a tessellated circle and the true circle.

### `+circle-maximum-segments+` (constant)

Most segments used for a circle; keeps the error under 0.3 px up to radius ~1e6 px.

### `+circle-minimum-segments+` (constant)

Fewest segments used for a circle, however small on screen.

### `+culling-margin-pixels+` (constant)

Border around the canvas inside which geometry is still emitted, so lines do not end
visibly at the edge.

### `+floats-per-vertex+` (constant)

Single-floats per vertex: x y u v r g b a.

### `+maximum-shape-segments+` (constant)

Most segments of any round shape.

### `+minimum-shape-segments+` (constant)

Fewest segments of any round shape.

### `+minimum-text-pixels+` (constant)

Text smaller than this many pixels is drawn as a baseline stroke instead of glyphs.

### `+snap-marker-half-size-pixels+` (constant)

Half the size of the snap marker, in pixels.

### `add-arc-stroke` (function)

```lisp
(add-arc-stroke list center-x center-y radius start-angle end-angle thickness color)
```

Append a stroked arc: the part of a ring between two angles. Angles grow clockwise on screen,
because the y axis points down; a full turn is a ring.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `CENTER-X`: center x in pixels.
- `CENTER-Y`: center y in pixels.
- `RADIUS`: radius of the middle of the stroke, in pixels.
- `START-ANGLE`: where the arc begins, in radians.
- `END-ANGLE`: where it ends, in radians.
- `THICKNESS`: stroke width in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-dashed-line` (function)

```lisp
(add-dashed-line list x1 y1 x2 y2 dash gap color)
```

Append a dashed one-pixel line.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X1`: start x in pixels.
- `Y1`: start y in pixels.
- `X2`: end x in pixels.
- `Y2`: end y in pixels.
- `DASH`: length of each dash in pixels.
- `GAP`: length of each gap in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-disc` (function)

```lisp
(add-disc list center-x center-y radius color)
```

Append a filled circle as a fan of triangles.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `CENTER-X`: center x in pixels.
- `CENTER-Y`: center y in pixels.
- `RADIUS`: radius in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-drawing-entities` (function)

```lisp
(add-drawing-entities frame drawing state)
```

Emit every visible entity: unselected ones in layer colors, then selected ones in the
selection color (shifted by the move preview), then the preview entities.

- `FRAME`: the build context.
- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `STATE`: the scene state.

### `add-filled-rectangle` (function)

```lisp
(add-filled-rectangle list x y width height color)
```

Append a filled rectangle as two triangles, in pixels.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-gradient-rectangle` (function)

```lisp
(add-gradient-rectangle list x y width height top-color bottom-color)
```

Append a rectangle that shades from one color at the top to another at the bottom.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `TOP-COLOR`: an RGBA at the top edge.
- `BOTTOM-COLOR`: an RGBA at the bottom edge.

### `add-grid` (function)

```lisp
(add-grid list drawing viewport theme)
```

Emit the adaptive grid and the axes.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `DRAWING`: the drawing, which supplies the base spacing and visibility.
- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `THEME`: the theme.

### `add-line` (function)

```lisp
(add-line list x1 y1 x2 y2 color)
```

Append one line segment, in pixels.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X1`: start x.
- `Y1`: start y.
- `X2`: end x.
- `Y2`: end y.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-polygon` (function)

```lisp
(add-polygon list points color)
```

Append a filled convex polygon as a fan from its first point.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `POINTS`: a list of (x . y) conses, three or more, in order around the polygon.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-rectangle-outline` (function)

```lisp
(add-rectangle-outline list x y width height color)
```

Append the four edges of a rectangle as lines, in pixels.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-ring` (function)

```lisp
(add-ring list center-x center-y radius thickness color)
```

Append a circle outline of the given thickness.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `CENTER-X`: center x in pixels.
- `CENTER-Y`: center y in pixels.
- `RADIUS`: radius of the middle of the stroke, in pixels.
- `THICKNESS`: stroke width in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-rounded-rectangle` (function)

```lisp
(add-rounded-rectangle list x y width height radius color)
```

Append a filled rectangle with rounded corners.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `RADIUS`: corner radius in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-rounded-rectangle-outline` (function)

```lisp
(add-rounded-rectangle-outline list x y width height radius thickness color)
```

Append the outline of a rounded rectangle, with its stroke inside the given bounds.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `RADIUS`: corner radius in pixels.
- `THICKNESS`: stroke width in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-shadow` (function)

```lisp
(add-shadow list x y width height radius spread color)
```

Append a soft drop shadow under a rounded rectangle: translucent copies that grow and fade
outward, lowered a little so that the light seems to come from above. Draw it before the
rectangle itself.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X`: left edge of the rectangle that casts it.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `RADIUS`: corner radius of that rectangle.
- `SPREAD`: how far the shadow reaches beyond it, in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA; its opacity is the opacity of the whole shadow near the rectangle.

### `add-snap-marker` (function)

```lisp
(add-snap-marker list viewport snap theme)
```

Emit the snap indicator: a square for endpoints, a circle for centers, a cross for the grid and for
ortho, a diagonal cross for polar and for intersections, a triangle for midpoints, a diamond for quadrants,
a right angle for perpendiculars and a circle on a line for tangents.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `SNAP`: (x y kind) in world coordinates, or NIL.
- `THEME`: the theme.

### `add-text` (function)

```lisp
(add-text list string x y size color &key (angle 0.0) (font mono))
```

Append a text run.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `STRING`: the characters to draw.
- `X`: x of the baseline start, in pixels.
- `Y`: y of the baseline start, in pixels (down).
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.
- `ANGLE`: counter-clockwise rotation in radians.
- `FONT`: :mono, :ui or :ui-strong.

### `add-thick-line` (function)

```lisp
(add-thick-line list x1 y1 x2 y2 thickness color)
```

Append a straight stroke of the given thickness as a quad. A zero-length stroke adds nothing.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X1`: start x in pixels.
- `Y1`: start y in pixels.
- `X2`: end x in pixels.
- `Y2`: end y in pixels.
- `THICKNESS`: stroke width in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `add-triangle` (function)

```lisp
(add-triangle list x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 color)
```

Append one filled triangle, in pixels.

- `LIST`: a draw list.
- `X1`: first corner x.
- `Y1`: first corner y.
- `X2`: second corner x.
- `Y2`: second corner y.
- `X3`: third corner x.
- `Y3`: third corner y.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `build-scene` (function)

```lisp
(build-scene list drawing viewport state theme)
```

Fill LIST (already reset) with the canvas content: grid, entities, selection window and
snap marker. Returns a SCENE-RESULT.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `DRAWING`: the drawing.
- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `STATE`: a scene state.
- `THEME`: the theme.

### `circle-segment-count` (function)

```lisp
(circle-segment-count radius-pixels)
```

Return how many segments tessellate a circle of RADIUS-PIXELS so the chordal error stays
under +CIRCLE-CHORDAL-ERROR-PIXELS+, between the minimum and the maximum.

- `RADIUS-PIXELS`: on-screen radius in pixels.

### `default-theme` (function)

```lisp
(default-theme)
```

Return the dark theme, the one the application starts with.

### `draw-command` (type)

A run of primitives of one kind.

- `KIND`: :lines (vertex pairs), :triangles (vertex triples) or :text (text runs).
- `START`: first vertex, or first text run for :text.
- `COUNT`: number of vertices, or of text runs for :text.

### `draw-command-count` (function)

```lisp
(draw-command-count instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-command-kind` (function)

```lisp
(draw-command-kind instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-command-start` (function)

```lisp
(draw-command-start instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list` (type)

Everything a renderer needs for one frame.

- `VERTICES`: x y u v r g b a for every vertex; only the first VERTEX-COUNT are meaningful.
- `VERTEX-COUNT`: vertices written so far.
- `COMMANDS`: draw commands in painter's order.
- `TEXT-RUNS`: text runs referenced by :text commands.
- `WHITE-U`: u of a fully opaque white texel, set by the renderer's texture atlas.
- `WHITE-V`: v of that texel.

### `draw-list-commands` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-commands instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list-line-count` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-line-count list)
```

Return the number of line segments in LIST.

- `LIST`: a draw list.

### `draw-list-reset` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-reset list)
```

Empty LIST for the next frame, keeping its storage.

- `LIST`: a draw list.

### `draw-list-text-runs` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-text-runs instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list-triangle-count` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-triangle-count list)
```

Return the number of triangles in LIST.

- `LIST`: a draw list.

### `draw-list-vertex-count` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-vertex-count instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list-vertices` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-vertices instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list-white-u` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-white-u instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `draw-list-white-v` (function)

```lisp
(draw-list-white-v instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `light-theme` (function)

```lisp
(light-theme)
```

Return the light theme: a white canvas and panels, and the same accents darkened to read on white.

### `make-draw-list` (function)

```lisp
(make-draw-list)
```

Create an empty draw list.

### `make-rgba` (function)

```lisp
(make-rgba r g b a)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-scene-result` (function)

```lisp
(make-scene-result drawn-entities culled-entities)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-scene-state` (function)

```lisp
(make-scene-state &key selected-ids translation preview-entities window window-crossing grips hot-grip snap)
```

(undocumented)

### `rgb->rgba` (function)

```lisp
(rgb->rgba rgb &optional (alpha 1.0))
```

Convert an 8-bit RGB color of the domain to a float color.

- `RGB`: a domain rgb value.
- `ALPHA`: opacity in [0, 1].

### `rgba` (type)

A color with channels in [0, 1].

- `R`: red.
- `G`: green.
- `B`: blue.
- `A`: opacity.

### `rgba-a` (function)

```lisp
(rgba-a instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `rgba-b` (function)

```lisp
(rgba-b instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `rgba-g` (function)

```lisp
(rgba-g instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `rgba-r` (function)

```lisp
(rgba-r instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `rounded-rectangle-points` (function)

```lisp
(rounded-rectangle-points x y width height radius &key (steps (corner-steps radius)))
```

Return the outline of a rounded rectangle as a list of (x . y) points, clockwise from the end
of the top-left corner arc.

- `X`: left edge.
- `Y`: top edge.
- `WIDTH`: width.
- `HEIGHT`: height.
- `RADIUS`: corner radius, limited to half the smaller side.
- `STEPS`: segments per corner; outlines that must pair up point for point share it.

### `scene-result` (type)

What building a scene did.

- `DRAWN-ENTITIES`: entities that produced geometry.
- `CULLED-ENTITIES`: entities skipped because they are off screen or on a hidden layer.

### `scene-result-culled-entities` (function)

```lisp
(scene-result-culled-entities instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-result-drawn-entities` (function)

```lisp
(scene-result-drawn-entities instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-state` (type)

The interaction state that decorates the drawing.

- `SELECTED-IDS`: hash table of selected entity ids, or NIL.
- `TRANSLATION`: (dx . dy) in millimetres applied to selected entities while dragging, or NIL.
- `PREVIEW-ENTITIES`: entities being created, drawn in the preview color.
- `WINDOW`: world bounds of a window-selection rectangle, or NIL.
- `WINDOW-CROSSING`: true when that rectangle is a crossing selection, drawn dashed and in another color.
- `GRIPS`: the grips of the selected entity, drawn as handles.
- `HOT-GRIP`: the one of them the pointer is on, drawn filled.
- `SNAP`: (x y kind) in world coordinates of the snap marker, or NIL.

### `scene-state-preview-entities` (function)

```lisp
(scene-state-preview-entities instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-state-selected-ids` (function)

```lisp
(scene-state-selected-ids instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-state-snap` (function)

```lisp
(scene-state-snap instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-state-translation` (function)

```lisp
(scene-state-translation instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `scene-state-window` (function)

```lisp
(scene-state-window instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `shape-segments` (function)

```lisp
(shape-segments radius)
```

Return how many segments make a circle of RADIUS pixels look round, between the minimum and
the maximum.

- `RADIUS`: radius in pixels.

### `text-run` (type)

A line of text to draw.

- `STRING`: the characters.
- `X`: x of the baseline start, in pixels.
- `Y`: y of the baseline start, in pixels (down).
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.
- `ANGLE`: counter-clockwise rotation of the baseline as seen on screen, in radians.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.
- `FONT`: :mono for drawing text, one cell per code point on a 0.6 em grid; :ui or :ui-strong for
the proportional faces of the user interface, whose widths the renderer measures.

### `theme` (type)

Every color the application draws with.

- `KIND`: :dark or :light, which says how the colors of the layers are drawn on this canvas.
- `CANVAS-BACKGROUND`: drawing area.
- `GRID-MINOR`: minor grid lines.
- `GRID-MAJOR`: major grid lines.
- `AXIS-X`: the x axis, the horizontal line through the origin.
- `AXIS-Y`: the y axis, the vertical line through the origin.
- `SELECTION`: selected entities.
- `SELECTION-FILL`: translucent fill of a window selection, which takes what lies inside.
- `CROSSING-FILL`: translucent fill of a crossing selection, which takes what it touches.
- `PREVIEW`: entity being created.
- `SNAP-MARKER`: snap indicator.
- `PANEL-BACKGROUND`: panels and cards.
- `PANEL-BORDER`: separators and outlines.
- `TITLE-BAR-TOP`: top of the title bar's shading.
- `TITLE-BAR-BOTTOM`: bottom of the title bar's shading.
- `TEXT`: primary text.
- `TEXT-DIM`: secondary text.
- `TEXT-FAINT`: tertiary text and disabled items.
- `BUTTON`: idle button.
- `BUTTON-HOVER`: button under the pointer.
- `BUTTON-ACTIVE`: toggled-on button; a soft tint of the accent.
- `ACCENT`: highlights and the primary action.
- `ACCENT-SOFT`: translucent accent for the background of active items.
- `ACCENT-CONTRAST`: text on a solid accent.
- `DANGER`: error messages.
- `WARNING`: cautions.
- `FIELD-BACKGROUND`: text fields.
- `OVERLAY-BACKGROUND`: translucent background of the performance overlay.
- `SHADOW`: drop shadows of cards and dialogs.
- `SCRIM`: the veil drawn over the window behind a dialog.
