## BORBA-CAD/SCRIPT

The language the program can be driven with: commands that draw and edit, written in Lisp
and run against a drawing; the context they run in, with the transaction that makes each command a single step
of undo; the evaluator, which reads, runs and reports; and the writer that turns a drawing back into the
commands that draw it.

### `*context*` (variable)

The context of the command that is running.

### `*customizations*` (variable)

The customizations that the definitions being run are added to.

### `*hook-events*` (variable)

What a hook can be added to, and when it runs: when the program has started, after a drawing is opened or a new one
is made, before a drawing is saved and after it is saved.

### `*maximum-changes*` (variable)

Most entities a single command may create, change or erase: well beyond what a drawing is meant to hold,
and a bound on the memory a runaway loop can ask for.

### `+default-timeout-seconds+` (constant)

Longest time a command typed in the console may run.

### `+script-timeout-seconds+` (constant)

Longest time a script file may run.

### `add-hook` (function)

```lisp
(add-hook event function)
```

Make a function run when something happens: :startup, when the program has started; :drawing-opened, after a drawing
is opened or a new one is made; :before-save, before a drawing is saved; :after-save, after it is saved. Each runs as
a command of its own, so what it changes is a step of undo, and a hook that fails says so and does no harm. Returns
the event.

- `EVENT`: one of :startup, :drawing-opened, :before-save and :after-save.
- `FUNCTION`: function of no arguments, or the name of a command.

### `angle-dimension` (function)

```lisp
(angle-dimension x y start end radius &key height decimals)
```

Dimension the angle at the vertex (X, Y) from the arm that points toward START to the arm that points toward
END, counter-clockwise, with an arc of the given radius. Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the vertex.
- `Y`: y of the vertex.
- `START`: the direction of the first arm, in degrees.
- `END`: the direction of the second arm, in degrees.
- `RADIUS`: the radius of the arc that carries the dimension.
- `HEIGHT`: the text height, or the one the drawing uses.
- `DECIMALS`: how many decimals of a degree the text shows, or 1.

### `arc` (function)

```lisp
(arc x y radius start end)
```

Draw the arc of the circle centered on (X, Y) that turns counter-clockwise from the angle START to the
angle END. Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: its radius.
- `START`: the angle it starts at, in degrees.
- `END`: the angle it ends at, in degrees.

### `array-grid` (function)

```lisp
(array-grid ids rows columns row-spacing column-spacing &key (angle 0))
```

Lay copies of entities in a grid, the originals being its first item: ROWS go up from them and COLUMNS to their
right, or turned by ANGLE. A negative spacing goes the other way. Returns the ids of the copies, row by row.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `ROWS`: how many rows, at least 1.
- `COLUMNS`: how many columns, at least 1.
- `ROW-SPACING`: distance from one row to the next.
- `COLUMN-SPACING`: distance from one column to the next.
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the columns in degrees, or (rad x).

### `array-path` (function)

```lisp
(array-path ids path &key count spacing (turn t))
```

Lay copies of entities along a line, polyline, arc, circle, ellipse, rectangle or polygon, the originals being the
item at its start. With COUNT the items are at equal steps from the start to the end (round a closed path); with
SPACING there is one every that distance, as many as fit. Returns the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `PATH`: the id of the entity the items follow.
- `COUNT`: how many items in all, the originals included.
- `SPACING`: the distance from one item to the next.
- `TURN`: true to turn each copy to follow the direction of the path, false to keep it as drawn.

### `array-polar` (function)

```lisp
(array-polar ids center-x center-y count &key (angle 360) (turn t))
```

Lay copies of entities round a point, the originals being the first of COUNT items. A full turn spreads the items
round the whole circle; a smaller angle ends with the last item. Returns the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of the center.
- `COUNT`: how many items in all, the originals included, at least 2.
- `ANGLE`: the angle to fill in degrees, or (rad x); negative goes clockwise.
- `TURN`: true to turn each copy as it goes round, false to keep it as drawn.

### `bind-key` (function)

```lisp
(bind-key chord target)
```

Bind a key to a command, replacing what it was bound to. The command may be defined after the binding. Returns the
binding as it is written.

- `CHORD`: the key and its modifiers written with +, such as "Ctrl+Alt+H" or "F7".
- `TARGET`: the name of a command that was defined with DEFCOMMAND, or one of the program (as the command palette lists
it), or a function of no arguments.

### `bindings` (function)

```lisp
(bindings)
```

Return the key bindings that were made, as a list of (key command): the key as it is written, and the name of the
command or a function.

### `blocks` (function)

```lisp
(blocks)
```

Return the names of the blocks, in alphabetical order.

### `chord-text` (function)

```lisp
(chord-text key modifiers)
```

Return a key binding as it is written: Ctrl+Alt+H.

- `KEY`: the keyword of the key.
- `MODIFIERS`: the list of :control, :shift and :alt held with it.

### `circle` (function)

```lisp
(circle x y radius)
```

Draw a circle centered on (X, Y). Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: its radius.

### `cm` (function)

```lisp
(cm value)
```

Return VALUE centimetres as millimetres: (cm 20) is 200.

- `VALUE`: a number.

### `commands` (function)

```lisp
(commands)
```

Return the names of the commands that were defined with DEFCOMMAND, in the order they were defined.

### `context-change-count` (symbol)

(undocumented)

### `context-changes` (function)

```lisp
(context-changes context)
```

Return the net changes of the command as a list of CHANGE records, in id order: what takes the drawing
from how it was when the command began to how it is. An entity that was created and erased again is not
in it, and one that was changed back is not either.

- `CONTEXT`: a script context.

### `context-reset` (function)

```lisp
(context-reset context)
```

Forget what the command did, which is to say that it is done and recorded: the next command begins
from the drawing as it is now.

- `CONTEXT`: a script context.

### `context-rollback` (function)

```lisp
(context-rollback context)
```

Undo everything the command did, so that the drawing is as it was when the command began.

- `CONTEXT`: a script context.

### `copy` (function)

```lisp
(copy ids dx dy)
```

Make copies of entities, moved by (DX, DY) from the originals. Returns the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `DX`: how far along x.
- `DY`: how far along y.

### `count-source-forms` (function)

```lisp
(count-source-forms text)
```

Return how many forms TEXT holds, or NIL when it cannot be read.

- `TEXT`: Lisp source.

### `customizations` (type)

What the user's own files defined.

- `COMMANDS`: the USER-COMMANDs, in the order they were defined.
- `KEYS`: list of (key modifiers target), the target being the name of a command or a function.
- `HOOKS`: list of (event . functions), each function being a function of no arguments or the name of a command.

### `customizations-commands` (function)

```lisp
(customizations-commands instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `customizations-hooks` (function)

```lisp
(customizations-hooks instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `customizations-keys` (function)

```lisp
(customizations-keys instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `defcommand` (macro)

```lisp
(defcommand name description &body body)
```

Define a command that is run from the command palette (Ctrl+K) or from a key bound to it. It takes no arguments, does
what BODY does, which can draw and edit with any command, and is one step of undo. Returns its name.

- `NAME`: what it is called, a string.
- `DESCRIPTION`: what it does, a string.
- `BODY`: the forms it runs.

### `deselect` (function)

```lisp
(deselect)
```

Select nothing. Returns 0.

### `diameter-dimension` (function)

```lisp
(diameter-dimension x y radius &key (angle 45) height unit decimals)
```

Dimension the diameter of the circle centered on (X, Y), with a line through the center at ANGLE. Returns its
id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius of the circle (the text says twice that).
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the line, in degrees.
- `HEIGHT`: the text height, or the one the drawing uses.
- `UNIT`: :millimeter, :centimeter or :meter, or the one lengths are shown in.
- `DECIMALS`: how many decimals the text shows, or the usual number for the unit.

### `dimension` (function)

```lisp
(dimension x1 y1 x2 y2 offset &key height unit decimals)
```

Dimension the distance between (X1, Y1) and (X2, Y2) with a line OFFSET away from the line through them,
to its left when positive. Returns its id.

- `X1`: x of the first point.
- `Y1`: y of the first point.
- `X2`: x of the second point.
- `Y2`: y of the second point.
- `OFFSET`: how far the dimension line is, to the left of the direction from the first point to the second.
- `HEIGHT`: the text height, or the one the drawing uses.
- `UNIT`: :millimeter, :centimeter or :meter, or the one lengths are shown in.
- `DECIMALS`: how many decimals the text shows, or the usual number for the unit.

### `display-unit` (function)

```lisp
(display-unit &optional unit)
```

Return the unit that lengths are shown in, after setting it to UNIT when one is given. The numbers that
commands take stay millimetres whatever it is.

- `UNIT`: :millimeter, :centimeter or :meter.

### `drawing-script` (function)

```lisp
(drawing-script drawing)
```

Return the text of a script that makes DRAWING again when it is run on an empty drawing: its settings, its
layers, its entities in the order they are drawn, each on the layer it is on, and last the layers that are
hidden or locked, so that locking a layer does not stop its entities from being drawn.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.

### `edit` (function)

```lisp
(edit id &rest changes)
```

Change properties of an entity. Returns its id.

- `ID`: the id of the entity.
- `CHANGES`: alternating property keywords and values, such as :radius 500 or :string "Kitchen"; the keywords
are the ones the inspector lists (:center-x, :center-y, :radius, :x1, :min-x, :height, :angle, :sides, ...),
lengths are millimetres and angles are degrees.

### `edit-sheet` (function)

```lisp
(edit-sheet number &key name paper (landscape nil landscape-given) project title author date revision)
```

Change a sheet: what is named is changed and the rest stays. Returns its number.

- `NUMBER`: the number of the sheet, from 1.
- `NAME`: its new name.
- `PAPER`: its new paper.
- `LANDSCAPE`: true for paper lying down, false for standing.
- `PROJECT`: the project, for the title block.
- `TITLE`: the title of the sheet.
- `AUTHOR`: who drew it.
- `DATE`: when, as text.
- `REVISION`: which revision.

### `ellipse` (function)

```lisp
(ellipse x y radius-x radius-y &optional (rotation 0))
```

Draw an ellipse centered on (X, Y) with the semi-axes RADIUS-X, along the direction ROTATION, and RADIUS-Y,
across it. Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS-X`: the semi-axis along ROTATION.
- `RADIUS-Y`: the semi-axis across it.
- `ROTATION`: the direction of the first axis, in degrees.

### `entities` (function)

```lisp
(entities &key kind layer)
```

Return the ids of the entities, in the order they are drawn, bottom first.

- `KIND`: only the entities of this kind: :line, :rectangle, :circle, :polyline, :text, :dimension,
:radial-dimension, :angular-dimension, :arc, :ellipse, :polygon or :hatch.
- `LAYER`: only the entities on the layer with this name.

### `entity-count` (function)

```lisp
(entity-count)
```

Return how many entities the drawing has.

### `erase` (function)

```lisp
(erase ids)
```

Erase entities. Entities on a locked layer are left. Returns how many were erased.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.

### `evaluate-function` (function)

```lisp
(evaluate-function context function &key (timeout +default-timeout-seconds+))
```

Call a function as a single command against the drawing of CONTEXT, with the guarantees of EVALUATE-SOURCE: what it
changes is put back when it fails or takes too long, and what it prints is kept. Returns a SCRIPT-RESULT.

- `CONTEXT`: the script context to run in.
- `FUNCTION`: function of no arguments, such as the one that a command defined in the init file stands for.
- `TIMEOUT`: the longest time to run, in seconds.

### `evaluate-source` (function)

```lisp
(evaluate-source context text &key (timeout +default-timeout-seconds+))
```

Read the forms in TEXT and run them one after the other against the drawing of CONTEXT, as a single
command: if one fails, or the time runs out, the entities it changed are put back as they were; layers and
settings are not part of that, as in the layers panel. Returns a SCRIPT-RESULT. The context begins by
forgetting what the command before it did, and ends holding what this one did, as its net changes, for the
caller to record.

- `CONTEXT`: the script context to run in.
- `TEXT`: Lisp source, read in the user package.
- `TIMEOUT`: the longest time to run, in seconds.

### `explode` (function)

```lisp
(explode ids)
```

Replace the blocks, rectangles, polygons and polylines among IDS by the entities they come apart into: what a block
stands for, and the sides of a shape. Returns how many were exploded.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.

### `extents` (function)

```lisp
(extents)
```

Return the smallest rectangle that holds the drawing, as the list (min-x min-y max-x max-y), or NIL when
it is empty.

### `find-key-binding` (function)

```lisp
(find-key-binding customizations key modifiers)
```

Return the command or function that a key is bound to, or NIL.

- `CUSTOMIZATIONS`: what the user defined.
- `KEY`: the keyword of the key that was pressed.
- `MODIFIERS`: the modifier keywords that were held.

### `find-user-command` (function)

```lisp
(find-user-command customizations name)
```

Return the command with a name, or NIL.

- `CUSTOMIZATIONS`: what the user defined.
- `NAME`: the name of the command, in any case.

### `fit` (function)

```lisp
(fit)
```

Zoom the window to show the whole drawing.

### `foot` (function)

```lisp
(foot value)
```

Return VALUE feet as millimetres: (foot 1) is 304.8.

- `VALUE`: a number.

### `grid-spacing` (function)

```lisp
(grid-spacing &optional spacing)
```

Return the spacing of the grid, in millimetres, after setting it to SPACING when one is given.

- `SPACING`: the new spacing.

### `hatch` (function)

```lisp
(hatch x y &key (pattern lines) (angle 45) spacing)
```

Hatch the smallest closed shape that holds the point (X, Y): a rectangle, circle, ellipse, polygon or closed
polyline. Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the point.
- `Y`: y of the point.
- `PATTERN`: :solid, :lines or :cross.
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the lines, in degrees.
- `SPACING`: the distance between two lines, or the usual one for the size of the shape.

### `hatch-polygon` (function)

```lisp
(hatch-polygon points &key (pattern lines) (angle 45) spacing holes)
```

Hatch the polygon through POINTS, leaving holes. Returns its id.

- `POINTS`: a list of (x y) lists, three or more, the last joined to the first.
- `PATTERN`: :solid, :lines or :cross.
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the lines, in degrees.
- `SPACING`: the distance between two lines, or the usual one for the size of the polygon.
- `HOLES`: a list of polygons, each a list of (x y) lists, that the hatch leaves unfilled.

### `help` (function)

```lisp
(help &optional command)
```

List the commands, or say what one of them does when COMMAND, a symbol, is given.

- `COMMAND`: the name of a command, such as 'line.

### `hook-functions` (function)

```lisp
(hook-functions customizations event)
```

Return what has been added to an event, in the order it was added.

- `CUSTOMIZATIONS`: what the user defined.
- `EVENT`: the event.

### `inch` (function)

```lisp
(inch value)
```

Return VALUE inches as millimetres: (inch 1) is 25.4.

- `VALUE`: a number.

### `info` (function)

```lisp
(info id)
```

Say what an entity is: its kind, its layer, and each of its properties and measures. Returns its id.

- `ID`: the id of the entity.

### `insert` (function)

```lisp
(insert name x y &key (scale 1) (rotation 0) flipped)
```

Place the block called NAME with its base point at (X, Y). Returns the id of the placement.

- `NAME`: the name of the block.
- `X`: x of the point.
- `Y`: y of the point.
- `SCALE`: how many times larger than the block.
- `ROTATION`: the turn, in degrees.
- `FLIPPED`: true to reflect the block across its vertical axis before it is turned.

### `layer` (function)

```lisp
(layer name &key color (visible nil visible-p) (locked nil locked-p))
```

Make the layer called NAME the one new entities go on, making the layer first when there is none. Returns
the name.

- `NAME`: the name of the layer.
- `COLOR`: a color (see RGB) or a name such as :red, for a layer that is new or whose color is to change.
- `VISIBLE`: true or false, to show or hide the layer.
- `LOCKED`: true or false, to lock or unlock it.

### `layers` (function)

```lisp
(layers)
```

Return the names of the layers, in the order they are listed.

### `line` (function)

```lisp
(line x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Draw a line from (X1, Y1) to (X2, Y2). Returns its id.

- `X1`: x of the start.
- `Y1`: y of the start.
- `X2`: x of the end.
- `Y2`: y of the end.

### `m` (function)

```lisp
(m value)
```

Return VALUE metres as millimetres: (m 5) is 5000.

- `VALUE`: a number.

### `make-block` (function)

```lisp
(make-block name ids &key (base-x 0) (base-y 0))
```

Make a block called NAME of the entities with IDS, which are replaced by one placement of it at the base point.
Returns the id of the placement.

- `NAME`: what the block is called.
- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `BASE-X`: x of the point that is the base point of the block.
- `BASE-Y`: y of that point.

### `make-customizations` (function)

```lisp
(make-customizations)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-script-context` (function)

```lisp
(make-script-context drawing &key selection-reader selection-writer view-fitter)
```

(undocumented)

### `mirror` (function)

```lisp
(mirror ids x1 y1 x2 y2 &key copy)
```

Reflect entities in the line through two points, or, with COPY, add reflected copies of them. A text keeps
facing the way it did; only where it starts is reflected. Entities on a locked layer are left. Returns how many
were reflected, or the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `X1`: x of the first point of the line.
- `Y1`: y of the first point.
- `X2`: x of the second point.
- `Y2`: y of the second point.
- `COPY`: true to keep the originals and add the reflected copies.

### `mm` (function)

```lisp
(mm value)
```

Return VALUE millimetres in millimetres, the unit of every length: (mm 3) is 3.

- `VALUE`: a number.

### `move` (function)

```lisp
(move ids dx dy)
```

Move entities by (DX, DY). Entities on a locked layer are left where they are. Returns how many moved.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `DX`: how far along x.
- `DY`: how far along y.

### `move-to-layer` (function)

```lisp
(move-to-layer ids name)
```

Move entities to the layer called NAME. Returns how many moved.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `NAME`: the name of the layer.

### `parse-chord` (function)

```lisp
(parse-chord text)
```

Return the key and the modifiers that a key binding names, as two values: the keyword of the key and the list of
:control, :shift and :alt. The text is the modifiers and then the key, joined by +, in any case: Ctrl+Alt+H,
Shift+F5, F7, Ctrl+Up. A key may be a letter, F1 to F12, Delete, Home, End, PageUp, PageDown or an arrow; the keys
that the program needs as they are (Escape, Enter, Tab, Space, Backspace) may not be bound. A letter needs Ctrl or
Alt, because it is what the user types otherwise. Signals an error when the text is none of that.

- `TEXT`: a key binding as it is written, such as "Ctrl+Shift+K".

### `pline` (function)

```lisp
(pline points &key closed)
```

Draw a polyline through POINTS. Returns its id.

- `POINTS`: a list of (x y) lists, two or more.
- `CLOSED`: true to join the last point to the first.

### `polygon` (function)

```lisp
(polygon x y radius sides &optional (rotation 0))
```

Draw a regular polygon whose vertices are on the circle centered on (X, Y), the first in the direction
ROTATION. Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius of that circle.
- `SIDES`: the number of sides, from 3 to 1000.
- `ROTATION`: the direction of the first vertex, in degrees.

### `quantities` (function)

```lisp
(quantities kind &key ids)
```

Count and measure what the drawing holds. Returns a list of rows, the totals last, each a list of what a column of
the report has: with :layer, :kind and :block a name, a count, a length in millimetres and an area in square
millimetres; with :cut a layer, the length of a bar, how many there are and their total length.

- `KIND`: :layer for the totals by layer, :kind for those by kind of entity, :block for the blocks that are placed, or :cut
for the cut list.
- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids to count, or NIL for everything on the layers that are not hidden.

### `rad` (function)

```lisp
(rad value)
```

Return an angle of VALUE radians, to be given where a command takes an angle: (rad pi) is half a turn.

- `VALUE`: a number of radians.

### `radius-dimension` (function)

```lisp
(radius-dimension x y radius &key (angle 45) height unit decimals)
```

Dimension the radius of the circle centered on (X, Y), with a leader that leaves the center at ANGLE. Returns
its id.

- `X`: x of the center.
- `Y`: y of the center.
- `RADIUS`: the radius of the circle.
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the leader, in degrees.
- `HEIGHT`: the text height, or the one the drawing uses.
- `UNIT`: :millimeter, :centimeter or :meter, or the one lengths are shown in.
- `DECIMALS`: how many decimals the text shows, or the usual number for the unit.

### `read-source-forms` (function)

```lisp
(read-source-forms text)
```

Return the forms in TEXT, read in the user package. Signals END-OF-FILE when the text ends inside a form,
and READER-ERROR when it is not Lisp. Nothing is evaluated.

- `TEXT`: Lisp source.

### `rect` (function)

```lisp
(rect x1 y1 x2 y2)
```

Draw a rectangle with sides along the axes from one corner, (X1, Y1), to the opposite one, (X2, Y2).
Returns its id.

- `X1`: x of one corner.
- `Y1`: y of one corner.
- `X2`: x of the opposite corner.
- `Y2`: y of the opposite corner.

### `rect-size` (function)

```lisp
(rect-size x y width height)
```

Draw a rectangle with sides along the axes whose lower left corner is (X, Y), WIDTH wide and HEIGHT high.
Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the lower left corner.
- `Y`: y of the lower left corner.
- `WIDTH`: its width.
- `HEIGHT`: its height.

### `redraw` (macro)

```lisp
(redraw id command)
```

Replace the entity with ID by the entity that COMMAND draws, keeping its id, its layer and its place in the
drawing order. Returns the id.

- `ID`: the id of the entity.
- `COMMAND`: a command that draws one entity, such as (line 0 0 100 0); it does not add one.

### `region-intersect` (function)

```lisp
(region-intersect ids)
```

Keep only where the closed shapes among IDS all overlap, as a polyline that replaces them. Returns the ids of the
polylines that result.

- `IDS`: a list of ids, two or more.

### `region-subtract` (function)

```lisp
(region-subtract base cutters)
```

Cut the closed shapes among CUTTERS away from the closed shape BASE: the polylines that result, outlines and holes,
replace them all. Returns their ids.

- `BASE`: the id of the shape to cut from.
- `CUTTERS`: an id or a list of ids of the shapes to cut away.

### `region-union` (function)

```lisp
(region-union ids)
```

Join the closed shapes among IDS (rectangles, circles, ellipses, polygons and closed polylines) into one outline
that replaces them. Returns the ids of the polylines that result.

- `IDS`: a list of ids, two or more.

### `remove-sheet` (function)

```lisp
(remove-sheet number)
```

Take a sheet away. The sheets after it move up by one. Returns NIL.

- `NUMBER`: the number of the sheet, from 1.

### `rgb` (function)

```lisp
(rgb red green blue)
```

Return a color from its red, green and blue, each from 0 to 255.

- `RED`: the red.
- `GREEN`: the green.
- `BLUE`: the blue.

### `rotate` (function)

```lisp
(rotate ids angle &key (about-x 0) (about-y 0) copy)
```

Turn entities counter-clockwise by an angle about a point, or, with COPY, add turned copies of them. A
rectangle turned to a slant becomes a closed polyline. Entities on a locked layer are left. Returns how many
were turned, or the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `ANGLE`: the angle in degrees, or (rad x).
- `ABOUT-X`: x of the center of the turn.
- `ABOUT-Y`: y of the center.
- `COPY`: true to keep the originals and add the turned copies.

### `say` (function)

```lisp
(say &rest things)
```

Write THINGS in the console, separated by spaces. Returns NIL.

- `THINGS`: anything.

### `scale` (function)

```lisp
(scale ids factor &key (about-x 0) (about-y 0) copy)
```

Scale entities about a point by a positive factor, or, with COPY, add scaled copies of them. Entities on a
locked layer are left. Returns how many were scaled, or the ids of the copies.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `FACTOR`: what every length is multiplied by.
- `ABOUT-X`: x of the center of the scaling.
- `ABOUT-Y`: y of the center.
- `COPY`: true to keep the originals and add the scaled copies.

### `script-context` (type)

What a command runs against.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing it draws on.
- `SELECTION-READER`: function of no arguments that returns the ids of the selected entities, or NIL.
- `SELECTION-WRITER`: function of a list of ids that selects them, or NIL.
- `VIEW-FITTER`: function of no arguments that zooms to the whole drawing, or NIL.
- `NET`: hash table from entity id to a cons of how the entity was when the command began (NIL when it did not
exist) and how it is now (NIL when it is gone).
- `CHANGE-COUNT`: how many changes the command has made.

### `script-context-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(script-context-drawing instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `script-result` (type)

What came of running some text.

- `STATUS`: :ok, :error (the text is wrong or a command failed, and nothing was changed), :timeout (it took too
long, and nothing was changed) or :incomplete (the text ends inside a form).
- `FORMS`: how many forms were run.
- `VALUES`: for each form run, what it returned, printed, in order.
- `OUTPUT`: what the commands printed.
- `MESSAGE`: for :error and :timeout, what went wrong.

### `script-result-forms` (function)

```lisp
(script-result-forms instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `script-result-message` (function)

```lisp
(script-result-message instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `script-result-output` (function)

```lisp
(script-result-output instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `script-result-status` (function)

```lisp
(script-result-status instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `script-result-values` (function)

```lisp
(script-result-values instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `select` (function)

```lisp
(select ids)
```

Select entities in the window. Returns how many are selected.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.

### `select-all` (function)

```lisp
(select-all)
```

Select every entity that can be selected. Returns how many are selected.

### `selected` (function)

```lisp
(selected)
```

Return the ids of the entities selected in the window.

### `sheet` (function)

```lisp
(sheet name &key (paper a3) (landscape t) project title author date revision)
```

Add a sheet that is printed: a page of a paper with a title block. Returns its number, from 1. Sheets, like layers,
are not part of what a command takes back when it fails.

- `NAME`: what it is called, a string.
- `PAPER`: :a4, :a3, :a2, :a1, :a0 or :letter.
- `LANDSCAPE`: true for paper lying down.
- `PROJECT`: the project, for the title block.
- `TITLE`: the title of the sheet.
- `AUTHOR`: who drew it.
- `DATE`: when, as text.
- `REVISION`: which revision.

### `sheets` (function)

```lisp
(sheets)
```

Return the sheets of the drawing, each as a list of its number, its name, its paper, whether it lies and how many
viewports it has.

### `spread` (function)

```lisp
(spread ids &optional (distance 10))
```

Take apart the rectangles, polygons, polylines, circles and blocks among IDS, and move each piece DISTANCE
millimetres from the others: a shape comes apart into its sides, a circle into four arcs, a block into its entities.
Returns the ids of the pieces.

- `IDS`: an id or a list of ids.
- `DISTANCE`: how far each piece moves, 10 mm (one centimetre) unless given.

### `steps-script` (function)

```lisp
(steps-script steps drawing)
```

Return the text of a script that makes STEPS again: the commands of each step, a blank line between steps,
after the blocks that the steps place.

- `STEPS`: lists of changes, oldest first, as undo keeps them.
- `DRAWING`: the drawing the steps ended in.

### `strip-comments` (function)

```lisp
(strip-comments text)
```

Return TEXT with the Lisp comments taken out, from a semicolon to the end of its line, but not the
semicolons inside strings or written as the character #;.

- `TEXT`: Lisp source.

### `text` (function)

```lisp
(text x y string &key height (angle 0))
```

Write STRING with its baseline starting at (X, Y). Returns its id.

- `X`: x of the start of the baseline.
- `Y`: y of the start of the baseline.
- `STRING`: the text.
- `HEIGHT`: the text height, or the one the drawing uses for new text.
- `ANGLE`: the direction of the baseline, in degrees.

### `user-command` (type)

A command that the user defined.

- `NAME`: what it is called, with the case the user gave it.
- `DESCRIPTION`: what it does, in a sentence.
- `FUNCTION`: function of no arguments that does it.

### `user-command-description` (function)

```lisp
(user-command-description instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `user-command-function` (function)

```lisp
(user-command-function instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `user-command-name` (function)

```lisp
(user-command-name instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `viewport` (function)

```lisp
(viewport number &key x y width height center-x center-y scale)
```

Put a viewport on a sheet: a window on the drawing at a scale. What is not given is worked out: it fills the area of
the sheet above the title block, shows the middle of the drawing, and is at the largest scale at which all of the
drawing shows. Returns the number of the viewport on the sheet, from 1.

- `NUMBER`: the number of the sheet, from 1.
- `X`: x of its lower left corner on the sheet, in millimetres from the sheet's own corner.
- `Y`: y of that corner.
- `WIDTH`: its width on the sheet.
- `HEIGHT`: its height on the sheet.
- `CENTER-X`: x of the point of the drawing at its center.
- `CENTER-Y`: y of that point.
- `SCALE`: millimetres of paper for a millimetre of the drawing, or text such as "1:50".

### `with-block` (macro)

```lisp
(with-block name &body body)
```

Make a block called NAME of the entities that BODY draws; they are not drawn in the drawing. Place it with INSERT.
Returns the name.

- `NAME`: what the block is called.
- `BODY`: drawing commands, such as LINE and CIRCLE.

### `with-layer` (macro)

```lisp
(with-layer (name &rest options) &body body)
```

Run BODY with the layer called NAME, made when there is none, as the one new entities go on, and then go
back to the layer that was in use.

- `NAME`: the name of the layer.
- `OPTIONS`: the keyword arguments of LAYER.
- `BODY`: the commands to run.

## BORBA-CAD/SCRIPTS

### `*project-root*` (variable)

Project root: the parent of the scripts/ directory that holds this file.

### `build-executable` (function)

```lisp
(build-executable &key (output build/borba-cad))
```

Load the window program and save it as a standalone executable (an SBCL image with the
runtime options baked in). Exits the process when the image has been written.

- `OUTPUT`: path of the executable, relative to the project root.

### `build-strict` (function)

```lisp
(build-strict &key (systems *project-systems*))
```

Recompile this project's SYSTEMS from scratch and fail when anything signals a WARNING or
STYLE-WARNING (the project's zero-warning gate).

- `SYSTEMS`: names of the systems to build.

### `fail` (function)

```lisp
(fail control &rest arguments)
```

Print an error message and exit the process with status 1.

- `CONTROL`: a format control string.
- `ARGUMENTS`: format arguments.

### `generate-api-reference` (function)

```lisp
(generate-api-reference &key (output docs/reference.md))
```

Write the API reference, generated from the docstrings of the exported symbols.

- `OUTPUT`: path of the Markdown file, relative to the project root.

### `install-dependencies` (function)

```lisp
(install-dependencies)
```

Download and compile the third-party systems every project system needs, then list each
one with the Quicklisp release it came from, so the exact versions in use are on record.

### `lint` (symbol)

(undocumented)

### `logs-summary` (function)

```lisp
(logs-summary &key directory)
```

Print the summary of the log files in DIRECTORY, or fail when there are none.

- `DIRECTORY`: log directory; defaults to var/log/ of the project.

### `make-example` (function)

```lisp
(make-example &key (output examples/floor-plan.bcad))
```

Regenerate the shipped example drawing by running the demonstration scenario headlessly.

- `OUTPUT`: path of the example, relative to the project root.

### `project-path` (function)

```lisp
(project-path relative)
```

Return the absolute pathname of RELATIVE inside the project.

- `RELATIVE`: path string relative to the project root.

### `quickload-quietly` (function)

```lisp
(quickload-quietly &rest system-names)
```

Load SYSTEM-NAMES through Quicklisp (installing third-party dependencies when missing).

- `SYSTEM-NAMES`: strings naming ASDF systems.

### `run-platform-tests` (function)

```lisp
(run-platform-tests &key (suite all))
```

Run the window-program tests (the renderer tests need a graphical session).

- `SUITE`: name of the FiveAM suite to run.

### `run-tests` (function)

```lisp
(run-tests &key (suite all))
```

Run the headless correctness tests.

- `SUITE`: name of the FiveAM suite to run.

### `start-repl` (function)

```lisp
(start-repl &key (systems '(borba-cad)))
```

Load SYSTEMS, create the BORBA-CAD/USER package with a short nickname for every module and
make it current, so that an interactive session starts ready to explore the program.

- `SYSTEMS`: names of the systems to load.

## BORBA-CAD/SHAPES

The library of shapes that can be placed in a drawing: ornaments such as the acanthus leaves of
ironwork, kept by the trade they belong to and the group they are in, made by procedures or read from SVG files, and
turned into the blocks that the drawing places.

### `+curve-tolerance+` (constant)

The most that the points of a flattened curve may stray from the curve, in the units of the shape (a shape is 100
units high, which is 100 millimetres as it comes).

### `all-areas` (function)

```lisp
(all-areas &optional user-areas)
```

Return the areas of the catalog: those that come with the program and then those the user added, with the shapes of
an area of the same name added to it.

- `USER-AREAS`: areas read from the user's folder of shapes, or NIL.

### `area` (type)

A trade or a field that has shapes, such as ironwork.

- `ID`: a string that identifies the area.
- `TITLE`: the English title of the area, translated when shown.
- `GROUPS`: the groups, in the order they are shown.

### `area-groups` (function)

```lisp
(area-groups instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `area-id` (function)

```lisp
(area-id instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `area-title` (function)

```lisp
(area-title instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `built-in-areas` (function)

```lisp
(built-in-areas)
```

Return the areas that come with the program: ironwork, with its acanthus leaves.

### `contour` (type)

One polyline of a shape.

- `POINTS`: a list of conses of x and y.
- `CLOSED`: true when the last point joins the first.

### `contour-closed` (function)

```lisp
(contour-closed instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `contour-points` (function)

```lisp
(contour-points instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `find-shape` (function)

```lisp
(find-shape areas area-id group-id shape-id)
```

Return the shape with an id, in the group and area with theirs, or NIL.

- `AREAS`: a list of areas.
- `AREA-ID`: the id of the area.
- `GROUP-ID`: the id of the group.
- `SHAPE-ID`: the id of the shape.

### `flatten-cubic` (function)

```lisp
(flatten-cubic p0 p1 p2 p3 &optional (tolerance +curve-tolerance+))
```

Return the points of a polyline that follows a cubic Bezier curve, after its start and up to and including its end,
as a list of conses of x and y.

- `P0`: the start, a cons of x and y.
- `P1`: the first control point.
- `P2`: the second control point.
- `P3`: the end.
- `TOLERANCE`: the most that the polyline may stray from the curve.

### `flatten-quadratic` (function)

```lisp
(flatten-quadratic p0 p1 p2 &optional (tolerance +curve-tolerance+))
```

Return the points of a polyline that follows a quadratic Bezier curve, after its start and up to and including its
end, as a list of conses of x and y.

- `P0`: the start, a cons of x and y.
- `P1`: the control point.
- `P2`: the end.
- `TOLERANCE`: the most that the polyline may stray from the curve.

### `group` (type)

Shapes that belong together, such as the acanthus leaves.

- `ID`: a string that identifies the group inside its area.
- `TITLE`: the English title of the group, translated when shown.
- `SHAPES`: the shapes, in the order they are shown.

### `group-id` (function)

```lisp
(group-id instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `group-shapes` (function)

```lisp
(group-shapes instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `group-title` (function)

```lisp
(group-title instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `load-user-areas` (function)

```lisp
(load-user-areas directory)
```

Return the areas that the user's folder of shapes holds, and the problems that came up as a second value. A folder
inside it is an area, a folder inside that a group, and the SVG files in a group are its shapes; the SVG files that are
directly in an area make a group of that name, and those directly in the folder make the area My shapes. A file that
cannot be read is left out and listed among the problems, as a cons of its name and a sentence.

- `DIRECTORY`: the folder of shapes, or NIL for none.

### `make-area` (function)

```lisp
(make-area id title groups)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-contour` (function)

```lisp
(make-contour points &optional closed)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-group` (function)

```lisp
(make-group id title shapes)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-shape` (function)

```lisp
(make-shape id name contours &optional name-argument)
```

(undocumented)

### `parse-svg-path` (function)

```lisp
(parse-svg-path text)
```

Return the contours that the data of an SVG path draws, in the coordinates of the file: open ones for the subpaths
that are not closed and closed ones for those that are, with the curves cut into segments. Signals INVALID-INPUT when
the data is not made of commands and numbers.

- `TEXT`: the value of the d attribute of a path.

### `read-svg-contours` (function)

```lisp
(read-svg-contours text)
```

Return the contours that an SVG file draws, in the coordinates of the file with y pointing up, as a list. Signals
INVALID-INPUT when the file is too large or draws too many points.

- `TEXT`: the text of the file.

### `read-svg-shape` (function)

```lisp
(read-svg-shape text id name)
```

Return the shape that an SVG file draws, 100 units across its larger side with its base at the origin. Signals
INVALID-INPUT when the file is too large, draws too much, or draws none of the elements that are read.

- `TEXT`: the text of the file.
- `ID`: the id to give the shape.
- `NAME`: the name to give the shape.

### `shape` (type)

A shape of the library.

- `ID`: a string that identifies the shape inside its group.
- `NAME`: the English name of the shape, translated when shown; it may have a ~d for the NAME-ARGUMENT.
- `CONTOURS`: a list of contours.
- `NAME-ARGUMENT`: the number that the name asks for, or NIL.
- `PREVIEW`: the contours cut down to the few points that a small picture needs, made the first time they are asked for.

### `shape-block-definition` (function)

```lisp
(shape-block-definition shape)
```

Return the definition of the block that a shape is placed as: a polyline for each contour of the shape, on the
default layer, with the base point at the origin. Contours with fewer than two points are left out.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

### `shape-block-name` (function)

```lisp
(shape-block-name shape)
```

Return the name of the block that a shape is placed as: the English name of the shape, which does not change with the
language of the interface.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

### `shape-contours` (function)

```lisp
(shape-contours instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `shape-height` (function)

```lisp
(shape-height shape)
```

Return the height of a shape, from its lowest point to its highest.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

### `shape-id` (function)

```lisp
(shape-id instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `shape-name` (function)

```lisp
(shape-name instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `shape-name-argument` (function)

```lisp
(shape-name-argument instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `shape-preview-polylines` (function)

```lisp
(shape-preview-polylines shape)
```

Return the picture of a shape as a list of polylines, each a cons of a vector of single-floats (x and y, point after
point) and true when the polyline is closed.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

### `shape-title` (function)

```lisp
(shape-title shape)
```

Return the name of a shape as it is shown, in the language of the interface.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

### `shape-width` (function)

```lisp
(shape-width shape)
```

Return the width of a shape, from its leftmost point to its rightmost.

- `SHAPE`: a shape.

## BORBA-CAD/TEST-SUPPORT

Seeded PRNG and deterministic workload generation for tests and benchmarks.

### `drawing-checksum` (function)

```lisp
(drawing-checksum drawing)
```

Return the checksum of the canonical text of DRAWING, as 16 hexadecimal digits.

- `DRAWING`: the drawing.

### `fnv1a-64` (function)

```lisp
(fnv1a-64 string)
```

Return the FNV-1a 64-bit hash of the UTF-8 encoding of STRING.

- `STRING`: the text to hash.

### `generate-drawing` (function)

```lisp
(generate-drawing &key (seed 1) (entity-count 1000))
```

Generate a deterministic drawing: the default layer plus five named layers and
ENTITY-COUNT entities of mixed kinds scattered over a site whose size grows with the count.

- `SEED`: PRNG seed; equal seeds give identical drawings.
- `ENTITY-COUNT`: number of entities to create.

### `make-prng` (function)

```lisp
(make-prng &optional (seed 0))
```

Create a generator.

- `SEED`: any non-negative integer below 2^64; equal seeds give equal sequences.

### `make-temporary-directory` (function)

```lisp
(make-temporary-directory)
```

Create and return a fresh, empty directory below the system's temporary directory.

### `prng` (type)

A SplitMix64 generator. Its whole state is one 64-bit integer.

- `STATE`: current 64-bit state.

### `prng-next-below` (function)

```lisp
(prng-next-below prng limit)
```

Return an integer in [0, LIMIT). The modulo bias is below 2^-40 for LIMIT under 2^24.

- `PRNG`: the generator.
- `LIMIT`: positive integer upper bound (exclusive).

### `prng-next-boolean` (function)

```lisp
(prng-next-boolean prng)
```

Return T or NIL with equal probability.

- `PRNG`: the generator.

### `prng-next-double` (function)

```lisp
(prng-next-double prng low high)
```

Return a double-float uniformly distributed in [LOW, HIGH).

- `PRNG`: the generator.
- `LOW`: inclusive lower bound.
- `HIGH`: exclusive upper bound.

### `prng-next-integer-64` (function)

```lisp
(prng-next-integer-64 prng)
```

Advance PRNG and return an unsigned 64-bit integer.

- `PRNG`: the generator.

### `prng-next-unit-double` (function)

```lisp
(prng-next-unit-double prng)
```

Return a double-float in [0, 1) with 53 random bits.

- `PRNG`: the generator.

### `prng-pick` (function)

```lisp
(prng-pick prng sequence)
```

Return a uniformly chosen element of SEQUENCE.

- `PRNG`: the generator.
- `SEQUENCE`: a non-empty sequence.

### `random-entity` (function)

```lisp
(random-entity prng kind id layer-id side)
```

Create one valid entity of KIND.

- `PRNG`: the generator.
- `KIND`: entity kind keyword.
- `ID`: entity id.
- `LAYER-ID`: layer id.
- `SIDE`: extent of the site in millimetres.

### `random-kind` (function)

```lisp
(random-kind prng)
```

Return an entity kind drawn according to *WORKLOAD-KIND-WEIGHTS*.

- `PRNG`: the generator.

### `with-temporary-directory` (macro)

```lisp
(with-temporary-directory (variable) &body body)
```

Run BODY with VARIABLE bound to a fresh temporary directory, deleted afterwards.

- `VARIABLE`: symbol bound to the directory pathname.
- `BODY`: forms to run.

## BORBA-CAD/UI

Declarative user-interface widgets: layout of the window, drawing and hit
testing of buttons, labels, rows and text fields into the draw list, single-line text
editing, and the performance overlay. No input device and no renderer appear here.

### `*text-measurer*` (variable)

Function of a font keyword (:ui, :ui-strong or :mono), a string and an em size in pixels that
returns the width of the text in pixels. The window program installs one that reads the real
glyph advances; the default measures a fixed 0.6 em per character. A global because layout is
computed far from any renderer and every widget must measure the same way.

### `+card-radius+` (constant)

Corner radius of cards and dialogs, in logical pixels.

### `+chip-height+` (constant)

Height of a pill-shaped toggle, in logical pixels.

### `+chip-padding+` (constant)

Space between a chip's edge and its content, in logical pixels.

### `+console-height+` (constant)

Height of the console when it is open, in pixels, unless the window is too low for it.

### `+font-pixels+` (constant)

Em size of the user-interface text, in logical pixels.

### `+gap+` (constant)

Standard gap between neighbouring user-interface elements, in logical pixels.

### `+heading-font-pixels+` (constant)

Em size of headings, in logical pixels.

### `+icon-button-size+` (constant)

Side of a square icon button, in logical pixels.

### `+icon-size+` (constant)

Size of an icon inside a title bar button, in logical pixels.

### `+padding+` (constant)

Standard gap between a container's edge and its content, in logical pixels.

### `+panel-margin+` (constant)

Gap between the side panel's edge and its cards, and between the cards, in logical pixels.

### `+radius+` (constant)

Corner radius of buttons and rows, in logical pixels.

### `+row-height+` (constant)

Height of one list row or field, in logical pixels.

### `+side-panel-width+` (constant)

Width of the right-hand panel, in logical pixels.

### `+small-font-pixels+` (constant)

Em size of secondary text, in logical pixels.

### `+small-icon-size+` (constant)

Size of an icon that stands beside a label, in logical pixels.

### `+small-radius+` (constant)

Corner radius of fields and chips' inner parts, in logical pixels.

### `+status-height+` (constant)

Height of the status bar, in logical pixels.

### `+tool-icon-size+` (constant)

Size of an icon inside a button of the tool palette, in logical pixels.

### `+tool-palette-width+` (constant)

Width of the column of drawing tools, in logical pixels.

### `+toolbar-height+` (constant)

Height of the title bar, in logical pixels.

### `+tooltip-height+` (constant)

Height of a tooltip, in logical pixels.

### `compute-layout` (function)

```lisp
(compute-layout width height &key console)
```

Divide a window of WIDTH x HEIGHT logical pixels into regions.

- `WIDTH`: window width in pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: window height in pixels.
- `CONSOLE`: true when the console is open, which takes its strip from the bottom of the canvas.

### `draw-icon` (function)

```lisp
(draw-icon list name x y size color)
```

Draw the icon NAME inside the square whose top-left corner is (X, Y). An unknown name draws
nothing.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `NAME`: an icon keyword from ICON-NAMES.
- `X`: left edge in pixels.
- `Y`: top edge in pixels.
- `SIZE`: side of the square in pixels.
- `COLOR`: an RGBA.

### `draw-logo` (function)

```lisp
(draw-logo list x y size theme)
```

Draw the brand mark: the tile and the letter in the colors of the theme.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `X`: left edge in pixels.
- `Y`: top edge in pixels.
- `SIZE`: side of the tile in pixels.
- `THEME`: the theme.

### `draw-overlay` (function)

```lisp
(draw-overlay list theme lines x y)
```

Draw the performance overlay: a translucent card with one monospaced text line per entry.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `THEME`: the theme.
- `LINES`: list of strings.
- `X`: left edge in pixels.
- `Y`: top edge in pixels.

### `draw-widgets` (function)

```lisp
(draw-widgets list theme widgets pointer-x pointer-y)
```

Draw every widget in order.

- `LIST`: the draw list.
- `THEME`: the theme.
- `WIDGETS`: list of widgets in drawing order.
- `POINTER-X`: pointer x in pixels, for hover highlighting.
- `POINTER-Y`: pointer y in pixels.

### `edit-backspace` (function)

```lisp
(edit-backspace state)
```

Delete the character before the caret, or the whole selection.

- `STATE`: the edit state.

### `edit-delete` (function)

```lisp
(edit-delete state)
```

Delete the character after the caret, or the whole selection.

- `STATE`: the edit state.

### `edit-handle-key` (function)

```lisp
(edit-handle-key state key)
```

Apply an editing key to STATE; returns the new state, or NIL when KEY is not an editing key.

- `STATE`: the edit state.
- `KEY`: :backspace, :delete, :left, :right, :home or :end.

### `edit-insert` (function)

```lisp
(edit-insert state string)
```

Insert STRING at the caret (replacing everything first when the text is selected);
characters that are not printable are dropped, and the result is cut to the maximum length.

- `STATE`: the edit state.
- `STRING`: typed text.

### `edit-move` (function)

```lisp
(edit-move state where)
```

Move the caret and clear any selection.

- `STATE`: the edit state.
- `WHERE`: :left, :right, :home or :end.

### `edit-select-all-replace` (function)

```lisp
(edit-select-all-replace state)
```

Select the whole text so the next typed character replaces it.

- `STATE`: the edit state.

### `edit-state` (type)

The content of a text field.

- `TEXT`: the characters.
- `CARET`: insertion position, from 0 to the length.
- `MAXIMUM-LENGTH`: longest accepted text.
- `REPLACE-ON-TYPE`: when true the whole text is selected: the next typed character replaces it.

### `fit-text` (function)

```lisp
(fit-text string maximum-width &optional (size +font-pixels+) (font ui))
```

Return STRING, shortened with an ellipsis when it would be wider than MAXIMUM-WIDTH. When not
even one character and the ellipsis fit, the longest prefix that fits is returned.

- `STRING`: the text.
- `MAXIMUM-WIDTH`: available width in pixels.
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.
- `FONT`: :ui, :ui-strong or :mono.

### `fixed-advance-width` (function)

```lisp
(fixed-advance-width font string size)
```

Measure STRING on the fixed 0.6 em grid, whatever the font. It is the default text measurer,
which keeps layout deterministic where no renderer exists, as in tests.

- `FONT`: the font keyword; ignored.
- `STRING`: the text.
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.

### `icon-names` (function)

```lisp
(icon-names)
```

Return the names of the available icons.

### `icon-shapes` (function)


Return the shape list of the icon NAME, or NIL when there is none.

- `NAME`: an icon keyword.

### `interactive-widget-p` (function)

```lisp

```

Return true for widgets that react to the pointer.

- `WIDGET`: a widget.

### `layout` (type)

The regions of the window. They tile it without gaps.

- `TOOLBAR`: the title bar along the top.
- `TOOL-PALETTE`: the column of drawing tools along the left.
- `CANVAS`: the drawing area.
- `SIDE-PANEL`: right-hand column.
- `LAYERS-PANEL`: upper part of the side panel.
- `INSPECTOR-PANEL`: lower part of the side panel.
- `STATUS-BAR`: strip along the bottom.
- `CONSOLE`: the strip under the canvas where commands are typed; it has no height when the console is closed.

An axis-aligned screen rectangle in logical pixels.

### `text-width-pixels` (function)

```lisp
(text-width-pixels string &optional (size +font-pixels+) (font ui))
```

Return the width of STRING at SIZE in FONT, as a single-float, as measured by the installed
text measurer.

- `STRING`: the text.
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.

### `tooltip-width` (function)

```lisp
(tooltip-width label shortcut)
```

Return the width of a tooltip with the given text and key cap.

### `visible-text-window` (function)

```lisp
(visible-text-window string caret maximum-width size font)
```

Return the start and end of the part of STRING to show in a field MAXIMUM-WIDTH wide so that
the caret is visible: the window ends at the caret when the text before it is too long to fit,
and otherwise starts at the beginning.

- `STRING`: the text.
- `CARET`: caret position, a count of characters from the start.
- `MAXIMUM-WIDTH`: width of the text area in pixels.
- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.
- `FONT`: :ui, :ui-strong or :mono.

### `widget` (type)

One user-interface element.

- `KIND`: :panel, :card, :button, :label, :row, :field, :swatch, :scrim, :separator, :icon, :logo or
:tooltip.
- `ID`: identifies the widget for focus and tests.
- `X`: left edge in pixels.
- `Y`: top edge in pixels.
- `WIDTH`: width in pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: height in pixels.
- `LABEL`: the text shown.
- `ACTIVE`: toggled on, selected or current.
- `ENABLED`: when false the widget is dimmed and ignores clicks.
- `BLOCKED`: when true a modal covers the widget; it is drawn but ignores the pointer.
- `SWATCH`: an RGBA drawn as a color chip.
- `ACTION`: function of no arguments called on click.
- `EDIT`: the EDIT-STATE of a field that has the keyboard focus, or NIL.
- `ALIGN`: :left, :center or :right text alignment.
- `COLOR`: text or icon color override, an RGBA.
- `HINT`: what the widget does, shown as a tooltip and in the status bar while hovering.
- `SHORTCUT`: the keys that do the same, shown beside the hint, or NIL.
- `ICON`: the name of the icon drawn in a button, row or on its own.
- `VARIANT`: for buttons, :default, :primary, :ghost, :tool (palette), :icon (title bar), :floating (an icon
with a body, over the canvas), :tile (a picture of a shape) or :chip
(a pill that toggles); for panels, :default, :title-bar, :status-bar, :palette, :side or :console.
- `FONT`: :ui, :ui-strong or :mono.
- `SIZE`: em size of the text in pixels.
- `TRAILING`: pixels at the right edge that the text must stay clear of, where other widgets sit
over the widget.
- `DATA`: what a widget draws that is not text or an icon: for a :tile button, a list of polylines, each a cons of a
vector of single-floats (x and y, point after point, in the coordinates of the shape, y pointing up) and true when
the polyline is closed.

### `wrap-text` (function)

```lisp
(wrap-text string maximum-width maximum-lines &optional (size +font-pixels+) (font ui))
```

Break STRING into lines no wider than MAXIMUM-WIDTH, at spaces where there are any. A word
wider than a line is cut where it overflows. When the text needs more than MAXIMUM-LINES lines,
the last one is truncated with an ellipsis.

- `STRING`: the text.

- `SIZE`: em size in pixels.
- `FONT`: :ui, :ui-strong or :mono.

## BORBA-CAD/USER

The package that what the user types in the console is read in: all of Common Lisp and the
commands of the program, and nothing else.

## BORBA-CAD/VIEWPORT

The only place where world coordinates (millimetres, +y up) meet screen
coordinates (logical pixels, +y down). Zoom, pan, fitting and the adaptive grid.

### `+default-visible-width+` (constant)

World width in millimetres shown by the default viewport (20 m).

### `+fit-margin-pixels+` (constant)

Empty border left around the content by FIT-VIEWPORT-TO-BOUNDS.

### `+grid-major-interval+` (constant)

Every this many displayed grid lines, one is drawn as a major line.

### `+maximum-grid-lines-per-axis+` (constant)

Safety bound on the lines drawn per axis; reachable only with a degenerate viewport.

### `+maximum-scale+` (constant)

Largest zoom, in pixels per millimetre: one pixel is 1 micrometre, the drawing resolution.

### `+minimum-grid-spacing-pixels+` (constant)

Closest the displayed grid lines may be, in pixels. Below it a grid reads as noise.

### `+minimum-scale+` (constant)

Smallest zoom, in pixels per millimetre: one pixel is 10 m, so the whole coordinate range
(20 km across) fits in about 2000 pixels.

### `clamp-scale` (function)

```lisp
(clamp-scale scale)
```

Return SCALE limited to the allowed zoom range.

- `SCALE`: pixels per millimetre.

### `compute-grid-level` (function)

```lisp
(compute-grid-level viewport base-spacing)
```

Return the grid level to display: BASE-SPACING times the smallest ladder multiplier that
keeps the lines at least +MINIMUM-GRID-SPACING-PIXELS+ apart.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `BASE-SPACING`: the drawing's grid spacing in millimetres.

### `default-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(default-viewport width height &key (origin-x 0.0d0) (origin-y 0.0d0))
```

Return the viewport shown for an empty drawing: origin at the center, 20 m across.

- `WIDTH`: canvas width in logical pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: canvas height in logical pixels.
- `ORIGIN-X`: window x of the canvas's left edge.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: window y of the canvas's top edge.

### `fit-viewport-to-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(fit-viewport-to-bounds viewport bounds &key (margin-pixels +fit-margin-pixels+))
```

Return the viewport that shows BOUNDS centered with a margin around them (zoom extents).
Bounds smaller than the drawing resolution in both directions are treated as that size.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport supplying the canvas size.
- `BOUNDS`: world bounds to show.
- `MARGIN-PIXELS`: empty border kept on every side.

### `grid-level` (type)

The grid as displayed at the current zoom.

- `SPACING`: distance between displayed minor lines, in millimetres.
- `MAJOR-INTERVAL`: every this many minor lines is a major line.

### `grid-level-major-interval` (function)

```lisp
(grid-level-major-interval instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `grid-level-spacing` (function)

```lisp
(grid-level-spacing instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-screen-point` (function)

```lisp
(make-screen-point x y)
```

(undocumented)

### `make-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(make-viewport &key center-x center-y scale width height (origin-x 0.0d0) (origin-y 0.0d0))
```

Create a viewport; the scale is clamped to the allowed range and the canvas size must be
positive.

- `CENTER-X`: world x at the canvas center, in millimetres.
- `CENTER-Y`: world y at the canvas center, in millimetres.
- `SCALE`: pixels per millimetre.
- `WIDTH`: canvas width in logical pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: canvas height in logical pixels.
- `ORIGIN-X`: window x of the canvas's left edge.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: window y of the canvas's top edge.

### `make-world-point` (function)

```lisp
(make-world-point x y)
```

(undocumented)

### `pan-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(pan-viewport viewport delta-x delta-y)
```

Return the viewport after dragging the content by (DELTA-X, DELTA-Y) pixels: a point that
was at screen position P is afterwards at P + (DELTA-X, DELTA-Y).

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `DELTA-X`: pixels the content moves right.
- `DELTA-Y`: pixels the content moves down.

### `pixels->world-length` (function)

```lisp
(pixels->world-length viewport pixels)
```

Convert a length in pixels to a world length.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `PIXELS`: logical pixels.

### `resize-viewport` (function)

```lisp
(resize-viewport viewport width height &key (origin-x (viewport-origin-x viewport)) (origin-y (viewport-origin-y viewport)))
```

Return a viewport with a new canvas rectangle and the same center and scale.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `WIDTH`: new canvas width in logical pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: new canvas height in logical pixels.
- `ORIGIN-X`: new window x of the canvas's left edge.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: new window y of the canvas's top edge.

### `screen->world` (function)

```lisp
(screen->world viewport screen-x screen-y)
```

Convert a screen position in pixels to world millimetres; returns two values.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `SCREEN-X`: x in logical pixels.
- `SCREEN-Y`: y in logical pixels (down).

### `screen-point` (type)

A position in screen coordinates.

- `X`: logical pixels from the left edge of the canvas.
- `Y`: logical pixels from the top edge of the canvas (+y down).

### `screen-point->world` (function)

```lisp
(screen-point->world viewport point)
```

Convert a typed screen point to a typed world point.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `POINT`: a screen-point.

### `screen-point-x` (function)

```lisp
(screen-point-x instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `screen-point-y` (function)

```lisp
(screen-point-y instance)
```

(undocumented)

### `viewport` (type)

A view of the drawing. Immutable: every operation returns a new viewport.

- `CENTER-X`: world x shown at the center of the canvas.
- `CENTER-Y`: world y shown at the center of the canvas.
- `SCALE`: pixels per millimetre, between +MINIMUM-SCALE+ and +MAXIMUM-SCALE+.
- `WIDTH`: canvas width in logical pixels.
- `HEIGHT`: canvas height in logical pixels.
- `ORIGIN-X`: window x of the canvas's left edge.
- `ORIGIN-Y`: window y of the canvas's top edge.


### `viewport-visible-bounds` (function)

```lisp
(viewport-visible-bounds viewport &optional (margin-pixels 0.0d0))
```

Return the world bounds covered by the canvas, grown by MARGIN-PIXELS on every side.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `MARGIN-PIXELS`: extra border in pixels, used so geometry just outside the canvas still draws.

### `viewport-width` (function)


(undocumented)

### `visible-grid-range` (function)

```lisp
(visible-grid-range viewport spacing)
```

Return the integer line indices visible on the canvas as four values: first and last index
along x, first and last along y. Line I lies at I times SPACING. The range is clipped to
+MAXIMUM-GRID-LINES-PER-AXIS+ lines per axis.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `SPACING`: displayed grid spacing in millimetres.

### `world->screen` (function)

```lisp
(world->screen viewport world-x world-y)
```

Convert a world position to screen pixels; returns two values.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `WORLD-X`: x in millimetres.
- `WORLD-Y`: y in millimetres.

### `world-length->pixels` (function)

```lisp
(world-length->pixels viewport length)
```

Convert a world length to a length in pixels.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `LENGTH`: millimetres.

### `world-point` (type)

A position in world coordinates.

- `X`: millimetres along +x.
- `Y`: millimetres along +y (up).

### `world-point->screen` (function)

```lisp
(world-point->screen viewport point)
```

Convert a typed world point to a typed screen point.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `POINT`: a world-point.


### `zoom-viewport-at` (function)

```lisp
(zoom-viewport-at viewport factor screen-x screen-y)
```

Return the viewport zoomed by FACTOR around a screen position: the world point under
(SCREEN-X, SCREEN-Y) stays under it. The scale is clamped, so zooming past a limit keeps the
anchor fixed at the limit scale.

- `VIEWPORT`: the viewport.
- `FACTOR`: multiplier of the scale, above 1 to zoom in.
- `SCREEN-X`: anchor x in logical pixels.
- `SCREEN-Y`: anchor y in logical pixels (down).
